Food prepared with excessive salt, spices, or vinegar causes burning sensation (विदाही/burning feeling) and vitiates pitta and blood (पित्त रक्त दूषण/pitta blood vitiator).
Food cooked with ghee and milk (घृत क्षीर पक्व/ghee milk cooked food) is wholesome (पथ्य/wholesome), nourishing (बृंहण/nourishing), and aphrodisiac (वृष्य/virility booster).
Food prepared by deep frying in oil (तैल भृष्ट/oil fried food) is heavy to digest (गुरु/heavy digestion), hot in potency (उष्ण/heating), and causes skin diseases (कुष्ठकर/skin disease causing).
Roasted meats cooked on charcoal (अंगार पक्व मांस/charcoal roasted meat) are light to digest (लघु/light digestion) and appetizing (रुचिकर/taste booster).
Meats cooked in ovens or earthen pots (पिठर पक्व/pot roasted meat) are unctuous (स्निग्ध/unctuous) and provide strength (बल्य/strength giving).
Food cooked with sour fruits and curds (अम्ल सिद्ध/sour cooked food) increases pitta and kapha (पित्त कफकर/pitta kapha increaser).
Food items that are sweet, unctuous, and heavy should be eaten first in meal (प्रथमाशन/first part of meal).
Items that are sour and salty should be consumed in middle of meal (मध्याशन/middle of meal).
Items that are pungent, bitter, and astringent should be eaten at end of meal (अंताशन/last part of meal).
Fruits like pomegranate and sugarcane should be consumed in beginning of meal (भोजनादौ/before meal).
Consuming food in proper order maintains health (स्वास्थ्य रक्षण/health preservation) and keeps humors balanced (दोष साम्य/humor balance).
Overeating (अत्यशन/overeating) destroys digestive fire (अग्निनाशन/digestion destroyer) and causes indigestion diseases (अजीर्ण रोग/indigestion illness).
Eating before previous meal is digested (अध्यशन/eating over undigested food) causes severe toxic accumulation (विषम आम/severe toxic residue).
Eating proper quantity according to individual digestive power (मात्रावत् अश्रन/measured eating) promotes longevity (आयुर्वर्धन/longevity promoter).
One third of stomach should be filled with solid food (एक तृतीय भाग ठोस/one third solid).
One third of stomach should be filled with liquids (एक तृतीय भाग द्रव/one third liquid).
Remaining one third of stomach should be left empty for free movement of humors (एक तृतीय भाग वायु हेतु/one third empty for air).
Warm fresh food (उष्ण स्निग्ध अन्न/warm unctuous food) promotes digestion (सुपाच्य/easy digesting) and kindles digestive fire (दीपन/digestion booster).
Eating in pleasant clean environment with peaceful mind (प्रसन्न चित्त/peaceful mind) ensures proper assimilation of food.
Eating food very fast (द्रुत भुक्त/eating too fast) leads to wrong pathway entry and choking.
Eating food extremely slowly (अति विलंबित/eating too slow) causes coldness and uneven digestion.
Thus ends the classification of cooked food preparations (कृतान्नवर्ग/cooked foods group).
Harina (हिरण/red deer), Ena (एण/black buck), Kuranga (कुरंग/spotted deer), Shardha, Gokarna (गोकर्ण/cow-eared deer), and Mrigamatrika (मृगमातृका/small deer) are deer varieties.
Kalapuchha (कालपुच्छ/black-tailed deer), Charushka, Varapota, Shasha (शश/rabbit), and Urana (उरण/wild sheep) are small wild quadrupeds.
Shvadamshtra (श्वदंष्ट्र/canine-toothed beast), Rama, Sharabha (शरभ/eight-legged deer), Kohakaraka, and Shambara (शंबर/sambar stag) belong to the wild game group.
Karala, Kritamala, and Prishata (पृषत/spotted axis deer) are declared as wild game animals (जांगल मृग/wild game animals).
Lavartika, Vartira, Raktavartmaka, and Kakubha are small spurfowl and quail species.
Kapinjala (कपिंजल/grey partridge), Upachakra, Asthya, Chakora (चकोर/greek partridge), and Kurubahava are bushland birds.
Vartaka (वर्त्तक/bush quail), Vartika (वर्त्तिका/rain quail), Tittiri (तित्तिर/francolin partridge), Krakara (क्रकर/spurred partridge), and Shikhi (शिखी/peacock) are gallinaceous birds.
Tamrachuda (ताम्रचूड/rooster), Ahavaraka, Gonerda, and Girivartika (गिरावर्त्तिका/mountain quail) belong to the pecking bird group (विष्किर/pecking birds).
Sarapada, Indrabha, and Vata are pecking birds (विष्किर/pecking birds) that scatter food before eating.
Shatapatra (शतपत्र/woodpecker), Bhringaraja (भृंगराज/racket-tailed drongo), Koyashti (कोयष्टि/lapwing), and Jeevanjeevaka (जीवंजीवक/greek pheasant) are tree birds.
Khanjaritaka (खंजरीटक/wagtail), Hareetu (हारीत/green pigeon), Durnamari, Rusha, and Gruha (गृहा/house sparrow) are small perching birds (प्रतुद/perching birds).
Latwa, Lattusha, Vataha, Gokshweda, and Dindimanava are perching forest birds.
Jati, Dundubhi, Pakarka, Lohaprishtha, and Kulingaka are small perching birds.
Sarika (सारिका/myna), Shuka (शुक/parrot), Sharangikhy, Chiriti, and Kakuyashtika are talking perching birds.
Manjuliyaka, Datyuha (दात्यूह/gallinule waterhen), Gopaputra, and Priyatmaja belong to perching species.
Kalavinka (कलविंक/sparrow), Parabhrita (परभृत/cuckoo), Kapota (कपोत/pigeon), and Angarachudaka are perching birds.
Pashavata and Panavika are declared as pecking and perching birds (प्रतुद द्विज/perching birds).
Shweta (श्वेत/white hedgehog), Shyama, Chitreprishtha, Kalaka, and Kakuli Mriga are small burrowing animals.
Bheka (भेक/frog), Chillata, Kuchika, Godha (गोधा/monitor lizard), Shalaka, and Shandaka are burrowing reptiles and amphibians.
Vrishahika, Kadali, Shvavit (श्वाविद्/porcupine), and Nakula (नकुल/mongoose) are burrowing animals (बिलेशय/burrowing animals).
Gorakha, Ashvatara (अश्वतर/mule), Ustra (उष्ट्र/camel), Ashva (अश्व/horse), Dveepi (द्वीपि/leopard), Sinha (सिंह/lion), Riksha (ऋक्ष/bear), and Vanara (वानर/monkey) belong to carnivore/beast groups.
Marjara (मार्जार/cat), Mushaka (मूषक/rat), Vyaghra (व्याघ्र/tiger), Vrika (वृक/wolf), Babhru (बभ्रू/large mongoose), and Tarakshu (तरक्षु/hyena) are predatory beasts.
Lopaka (लोपाक/fox), Jambuka (जंबुक/jackal), Shyena (श्येन/falcon), Chasha (चाष/blue jay), Uluka (उलूक/owl), Shva (श्व/dog), and Vayasa (वायस/crow) are predatory flesh-eaters.
Shashaghni (शशघ्नी/eagle), Bhasa (भास/bearded vulture), Kurara (कुरर/osprey), Gridhra (गृध्र/vulture), Veshya, and Kulingaka are predatory birds (प्रसह/predatory birds).
Dhumika and Madhuha are predatory birds (प्रसह/predatory birds) that snatch food before eating.
Mahisha (महिष/wild buffalo), Nyanku, Rohita deer, Varaha (वराह/wild boar), Ruru, and Varana (वारण/elephant) are large land beasts.
Srimara, Chamara (चमर/yak), Khanga (खड्ग/rhinoceros), and Gavaya (गवय/gayal ox) belong to large wild quadrupeds (महामृग/large wild quadrupeds).
Hansa (हंस/swan), Sarasa (सारस/crane), Kadamba, Baka (बक/heron), Karandava (कारंडव/coot), and Plava (प्लव/pelican) are aquatic birds.
Mrinalakantha, Chakrahva (चक्राह्व/ruddy shelduck), Balaka (बलाक/egret), and Raktashirshaka (रक्तशीर्ष/red-headed crane) are riverbank waterfowls.
Ukrosha, Pundarikaksha, Sharari, and Manitundika are waterfowls inhabiting ponds and lakes.
Kakatunda, Ghanarava, Madgu (मद्गु/cormorant), Krauncha (क्रौंच/curlew), and Ambukukkuta (अंबुकुक्कुट/water hen) are aquatic birds.
Nandyasya, Mallika, and similar waterfowls belong to aquatic bird group (जलचारि पक्षी/aquatic birds).
Rohita fish (रोहित/rohu fish), Pathina (पाठीन/sheat fish), Kurma (कूर्म/turtle), Kumbheera (कुंभीर/crocodile), and Karkata (कर्कट/crab) are aquatic creatures.
Shukti (शुक्ति/pearl oyster), Shankha (शंख/conch), Udra (उद्र/otter), Shambuka (शंबूक/snail), Shafari (शफरी/small carp), Varmi (वर्मि/eel), and Chandrika are water creatures.
Chuluki (चुलुकि/porpoise), Nakra (नक्र/alligator), Makara (मकर/sea monster), Shishumara (शिशुमार/gangetic dolphin), and Timigila (तिमिगिल/whale) are marine beasts.
Raji fish and Chilichima fish (चिलिचिम/chilichima fish) complete the eightfold classification of meat sources (अष्टधा मांस/eightfold meat categories).
Meats of goat (अज/goat) and sheep (अवि/sheep) belong to a combined habitat category because they graze across both arid and marshy lands.
Game birds and quadrupeds (जांगल/arid land/game) are first; marshy/aquatic animals (आनूप/marshy land) are last; mixed land creatures are intermediate.
Pecking birds (विष्किर/pecking birds) and perching birds (प्रतुद/perching birds) are classified according to their feeding habits and movement.
Meats of arid land animals (जांगल मांस/game meat) bind bowel stool (बद्धमल/constipating), increase appetite (रुचिकर/palatable), are supreme in quality, and cooling.
Jangala meat is astringent-sweet in taste (कषाय मधुर/astringent sweet), light to digest (लघु/light digestion), and wholesome (हितकर/wholesome).
Jangala meat is indicated in Pitta-dominant diseases, moderate Vata disorders, and Kapha-associated Sannipata fevers.
Copper-colored deer meat (हरिण/copper deer) and black deer meat (एण/black deer) are heart-pleasing (हृद्य/cardiac tonic) and pacify all three humors (त्रिदोषजित्/tridosha pacifier).
Rabbit meat (शश/rabbit) is light (लघु/light digestion), sweet-astringent in taste, bowel-binding (ग्राहि/binder), dry (रूक्ष/dry), and cooling (हिम/cooling).
Rabbit meat has pungent post-digestive effect (कटुविपाक/pungent end-product), kindles digestion (अग्निकृत्/appetite booster), and is wholesome in Sannipata with low Vata.
Shava deer meat is non-dry, while Kapinjala partridge meat (कपिंजल/grey partridge) is slightly dry and pacifies Pitta and Kapha.
Paravata pigeon meat and Kapota wild pigeon meat (कपोत/pigeon) are light, bowel-binding, and highly praised in medical texts.
Vartaka quail meat (वर्त्तक/quail) is slightly heating (ईषद् उष्ण/slightly warm), heavy to digest (गुरु/heavy digestion), unctuous (स्निग्ध/oily), and body-nourishing (बृंहण/nourishing).
Among all gallinaceous birds, Tittiri partridge meat (तित्तिर/francolin) is supreme, enhancing intellect (मेधा/intellect), digestive fire (अग्नि/digestion), strength (बल/stamina), and semen (शुक्र/semen/reproductive tissue).
Tittiri meat is bowel-binding (ग्राहि/binder), improves complexion (वर्ण्य/complexion enhancer), and excels at pacifying Vata-dominant Sannipata disorders.
Peacock meat (शिखी/peacock) is unctuous (स्निग्ध/oily), hot in potency (उष्ण/heating), heavy to digest (गुरु/heavy digestion), and body-building (बृंहण/nourishing).
Wild peacock meat is wholesome for hearing (श्रोत्र/hearing), voice (स्वर/voice), youthfulness (वयस्/youthfulness), and vision (दृश्/eyesight).
Rooster meat (कुक्कुट/cock) is aphrodisiac (वृष्य/virility booster); domesticated rooster meat is heavy (गुरु/heavy digestion) and increases Kapha (श्लेष्मल/kapha aggravating).
Krakara partridge meat and Upachakra meat enhance intellect (मेधा/intellect), digestive fire (अनल/digestion), and are pleasing to the heart (हृद्य/cardiac tonic).
Kana-kapota pigeon meat is heavy (गुरु/heavy digestion), slightly salty (सलवण/salty), and aggravates all three humors (सर्वदोषकृत्/aggravating all three humors).
Meats of marshy animals (आनूप/marshy land), burrowing animals (बिलेशय/burrowing animals), and water creatures (जलचारि/aquatic creatures) are heavy, warm, unctuous, and sweet.
Anupa and aquatic meats increase urine (मूत्र/urine) and semen (शुक्र/semen), provide strength (बल्य/strength), pacify Vata, and increase Kapha and Pitta.
Mahamriga meats (large quadrupeds) are cold in potency; predatory flesh-eating birds (प्रसह/predatory birds) and carnivorous beasts (क्रव्याद/carnivorous animals) are hot.
Meats of flesh-eating predators (क्रव्याद/carnivorous animals) improve eyesight (चक्षुष्य/vision booster), clear urine/stool, increase muscle tissue (मांसल/muscle builder), and have pungent vipaka.
Predatory meats are supreme for chronic piles (जीर्णांशः/chronic piles), IBS (ग्रहणी/irritable bowel syndrome), and wasting disease (शोष/tissue wasting).
Monitor lizard meat (गोधा/monitor lizard) neutralizes poisons (विषघ्न/poison neutralizer); rat meat (मूषक/rat) increases semen (शुक्रवर्धन/semen booster).
Beef (गोमांस/beef) cures dry cough (शुष्ककास/dry cough), fatigue (श्रम/fatigue), hyper-active digestion (अत्यग्नि/hyperactive digestive fire), irregular fever (विषमज्वर/irregular fever), and rhinitis (पीनस/chronic rhinitis).
Beef relieves severe emaciation (कार्श्य/wasting) and standalone severe Vata disorders (केवलवात/isolated vata disorder).
Sparrow meat (चटक/sparrow) increases Kapha, is unctuous (स्निग्ध/oily), pacifies Vata, and is a supreme aphrodisiac (शुक्रल/semen increaser).
Buffalo meat (महिष/buffalo) is heavy (गुरु/heavy digestion), warm (उष्ण/heating), unctuous (स्निग्ध/oily), induces deep sleep (स्वप्नकृत्/sleep inducer), and increases body bulk (बृहत्त्व/body bulk).
Wild boar meat (वराह/pork) relieves fatigue (श्रमहा/fatigue reliever), improves appetite (रुचि/palatability), and increases semen and stamina (शुक्रबलप्रद/semen stamina enhancer).
Swan meat (हंस/swan) improves voice (स्वरकृत्/voice enhancer), pacifies Pitta and blood disorders (पित्तरक्तजित्/pitta blood conqueror), is sweet, and cold in potency.
Fish meat (मत्स्य/fish) increases Kapha and Pitta, but is a supreme pacifier of Vata humor (पवननाशन/vata pacifier).
Fish swim against water currents and leap into the air, making their flesh hot and heavy.
Among fish, Rohita fish (रोहित/rohu) is supreme in quality, while Chilichima fish (चिलिचिम/chilichima) is the worst.
Chilichima fish feeds on foul matter and moves in dirty waters, thereby aggravating all three humors (सर्वदोषकर/aggravator of all three humors).
Crab meat (कुलीर/crab) is a supreme aphrodisiac (वृष्य/virility booster), body-nourishing (बृंहण/nourishing), gratifying (प्रीणन/refreshing), and heavy to digest.
Goat meat (छाग/mutton) is not overly cold, heavy, or unctuous, and does not vitiate bodily humors (अदोषल/non-vitiating).
Because goat tissue matches human body tissue structure (धातुसामान्य/tissue similarity), mutton is non-clogging (अनभिष्यंदि/non-clogging) and highly nourishing (बृंहणीय/body nourisher).
Mutton's opposite is sheep meat (आविक/mutton-sheep), which is heavy, heating, clogging, and increases Kapha and Pitta.
One should avoid meat of animals that are diseased, poisoned, drowned in water, bitten by venomous snakes, or dead naturally.
One should avoid meat of emaciated animals, animals killed in smoke, or animals living in foul habitats.
Freshly slaughtered meat of young, healthy, clean, and fragrant animals is highly recommended (शस्यते/praised/recommended).
Ena deer, Kuranga deer, Harina deer, Rabbit, Lava quail, and Kapinjala partridge;
Tittiri partridge, Krakara partridge, Monitor lizard, Porcupine, and Lion;
Peacock, Myna bird, Nyanku deer, Swan, Rohita fish, and Turtle;
And Varmi fish are the supreme representatives in their respective meat categories (प्रवर/superior quality).
Lava quail, Ena deer, Monitor lizard, and Lion are praised, while beef (गौ/cow/ox) and frog meat (दर्दुर/frog) are contraindicated.
Kana-kapota pigeon meat is improper (कृश/emaciated person), while other meats should be evaluated according to habitat and properties.
Eggs of birds (अंड/eggs) and flesh of young animals are heavy; flesh of mature wild quadrupeds is astringent-salty.
Meat of old animals is full of tough sinews (स्नायुभूयिष्ठ/tendon-heavy meat), lacks strength-giving power, and aggravates humors.
Front-half flesh of male quadrupeds and hind-half flesh of female quadrupeds is heavier; pregnant animal flesh is heavy.
Female flesh is lighter among four-legged quadrupeds (चतुष्पाद/four pillars of treatment), whereas male flesh is lighter among birds (विहंग/birds).
Flesh of head, neck, chest, back, waist, and thighs increases in heaviness sequentially in order of listing.
Flesh from stomach (आमाशय/stomach) and intestine (पक्वाशय/large intestine) is heavier than surrounding tissue in order.
Tissues ranging from blood (रक्त/blood tissue) onwards increase in heaviness sequentially up to reproductive tissue (शुक्र/semen/reproductive tissue).
Testicular tissue (वृषण/testicles), umbilical muscle (ऐंद्रकथ्य/testicular disease), and cardiac muscle (हृद्गद/cardiac disease) are heavier than general skeletal meat.
Thus ends the classification of animal meats (मांसवर्ग/meat classification group).
Patha (पाठा/velvet leaf), Shati (शठी/spiked ginger lily), Soosha, Sunishannaka (सुनिषण्णक/four-leaf clover), and Satina (सतीन/field pea leaves) are leafy greens.
Patha, Sunishannaka, Rajakshava, and Vastuka (वास्तुक/bathua) pacify all three humors (त्रिदोषघ्न/tridosha pacifier), are light (लघु/light), and bowel-binding (ग्राहि/binder).
Among these, Sunishannaka kindles digestive fire (अग्निकृत्/appetite booster) and is aphrodisiac, while Rajakshava is supreme.
Vastuka (वास्तुक/bathua) cures IBS (ग्रहणी/irritable bowel syndrome), piles (अर्शः/hemorrhoids/piles), and clears bowel feces (वर्चोभेदि/laxative/stool breaker).
Kakamachi (काकमाची/black nightshade) pacifies all three humors (त्रिदोषघ्नी/tridosha pacifier), cures skin diseases (कुष्ठघ्नी/skin cure), is aphrodisiac (वृष्या/virility booster), warm, and rejuvenative (रसायनी/rejuvenative herb).
Kakamachi is laxative (सरा/laxative), improves voice (स्वर्या/voice enhancer), while Changeri (चांगेरी/wood sorrel) is sour and kindles digestive fire (अग्नि दीपनी/appetite kindling).
Changeri cures IBS (ग्रहणी/irritable bowel syndrome), piles (अर्शः/hemorrhoids/piles), Vata-Kapha disorders, is warm, bowel-binding (ग्राहि/binder), and light to digest.
Patola (पटोल/pointed gourd), Samala, Arishta (अरिष्ट/neem leaves), Sharngashta, Avalguja (अवल्गुज/bakuchi), and Guduchi (अमृता/giloy);
Vetragra (वेत्राग्र/cane shoots), Brihati (बृहती/poison berry), Vasa (वासा/malabar nut), Kuntali, and Tilaparnika;
Mandukaparni (मंडूकपर्णी/gotu kola), Karkota (कर्कोट/spine gourd), Karavellaka (कारवेल्लक/bitter gourd), and Parpata (पर्पट/fumitory);
Nadikalaya, Gojihva (गोजिह्वा/elephant foot), Vartaka (वार्त्ताक/eggplant), and Vanatiktaka (wild bitter greens);
Karira (करीर/caper shoots), Kulaka, Nandi, Kuchaatila, and Shakuladani;
Katila, Kembuka, Sheetalam, Koshataka (कोशातक/sponge gourd), and Karkasha;
These bitter greens (तिक्त शाक/bitter greens) have pungent vipaka, are bowel-binding (ग्राहि/binder), increase Vata, and pacify Kapha and Pitta.
Patola (पटोल/pointed gourd) is pleasing to heart (हृद्य/cardiac tonic), destroys worms (कृमिघ्न/worm destroyer), has sweet vipaka, and improves appetite (रुचिप्रद/palatability enhancer).
Two Brihati varieties (बृहती द्वय/nightshades) increase Pitta slightly, kindle digestion (दीपन/appetite booster), clear bowels (भेदि/purgative), and pacify Vata.
Vasa leaves (वासा/malabar nut) cure vomiting (वमि/emesis), cough (कास/cough), and are supreme for bleeding disorders (रक्तपित्तहर/bleeding disease pacifier).
Karavellaka (कारवेल्लक/bitter gourd) is pungent-bitter, kindles digestion (दीपन/appetite booster), and is supreme for pacifying Kapha (कफजित् परम्/supreme kapha reducer).
Vartaka eggplant (वार्त्ताक/brinjal) is pungent-bitter, warm, sweet in vipaka, and pacifies Kapha and Vata.
Eggplant contains natural alkaline properties (सक्षार/alkaline), kindles digestive fire (अग्निजनन/appetite booster), is heart-pleasing, appetizing, and does not increase Pitta.
Karira shoots (करीर/caper) cause abdominal distension (आध्मानकर/flatulence causing), and are astringent, sweet, and bitter in taste.
Koshataka and Avalguja clear bowel obstruction (भेदन/purgative) and kindle internal digestive fire (अग्नि दीपन/digestive fire booster).
Shyama, Shalmali shoots, Kashmarya shoots, Phanji, Karnaka, and Yuthika shoots;
Vrikshadani, Ksheerivriksha sprouts, Bimbi, and Naktavriksha sprouts;
Lodhra, Shana, Karbudara, Selu shoots, and Vishamushtika;
Bhallataka sprouts, Kovidara, Lotus sprouts, Utpala, and Kinshuka sprouts;
Possess properties similar to Patola group, being sweet-astringent, and supreme for pacifying Pitta (पित्तजित् परम्/supreme pitta pacifier).
They bind urine (बद्धमूत्र/antidiuretic), while Phanji and Karira shoots clear bowel movement without causing pain.
They are bitter, light (लघु/light), good for eyesight (चक्षुष्य/vision booster), aphrodisiac, and pacify all three humors (त्रिदोषप्रणुत्/tridosha conqueror).
Tanduliyaka (तंडुलीयक/spiny amaranth) is cooling (हिम/cooling), dry (रूक्ष/dry), sweet in taste and vipaka, and light to digest.
Tanduliyaka cures intoxication (मद/intoxication), Pitta disorders, poisons (विष/toxins), and blood diseases, while Munjata pacifies Vata and Pitta.
Munjata is unctuous (स्निग्ध/oily), cooling (शीत/cooling), heavy (गुरु/heavy), sweet, body-nourishing (बृंहण/nourishing), and increases semen (शुक्रकृत्/semen generator).
Palankya spinach (पालंक्या/spinach) is slimy (पिच्छिल/slimy), heavy (गुरु/heavy), increases Kapha, clears bowels (भेदिनी/laxative), and is cooling.
Upodika (उपोदका/malabar spinach) cures intoxication, while Chanchu is bowel-binding (ग्राहि/binder); both share Palankya properties.
Vidari tuber greens (विदारी/kudzu shoots) pacify Vata and Pitta, are diuretic (मूत्रल/diuretic), sweet, and cooling.
Vidari is vitalizing (जीवनी/vitalizing), body-building (बृंहण/nourishing), beneficial for throat (कंठ्या/voice enhancer), heavy, aphrodisiac, and rejuvenative (रसायनी/rejuvenative herb).
Jeevanti greens (जीवंती/leptadenia) improve eyesight (चक्षुष्य/eye tonic), pacify all three humors (सर्वदोषघ्नी/pacifier of all three humors), are sweet, and cooling.
Among all leafy greens (शाक/leafy vegetables), Jeevanti is supreme (प्रवर/superior quality), while Mustard greens (सर्षप/mustard seed) are the worst (अवर/inferior).
Bhandi, Parvani, and Parvapushpika greens pacify Vata and Pitta humors.
Kushmanda ash gourd (कूष्मांड/ash gourd), Tumba, Kalinga, Karkari, Evaru, and Tindisha gourds;
Along with Trapusa cucumber (त्रपुस/cucumber), Chinaka, and Chirbhita increase Kapha and Vata.
They clear bowels (भेदि/laxative), cause constipation/distension (विष्टंभि/flatulent), produce tissue exudation (अभिष्यंदि/clogging), have sweet vipaka, and are heavy.
Among creeper gourds (वल्लीफल/creeper fruits/gourds), Kushmanda (कूष्मांड/ash gourd) is supreme, pacifying Vata and Pitta.
Kushmanda cleanses urinary bladder (बस्तिशुद्धि/bladder cleanser) and is aphrodisiac, while Trapusa cucumber is strongly diuretic (अतिमूत्रल/excessively diuretic).
Tumba bottle gourd is dry and bowel-binding (ग्राहि/binder); Kalinga, Evaru, and Chirbhita gourds have similar actions.
Unripe gourds (बाल फल/unripe tender fruit) are cooling and pacify Pitta, whereas fully ripe gourds (पक्व फल/ripe fruit) are opposite in quality.
Overripe gourds detached from vine (शीर्णवृंत/naturally fallen fruit) possess alkaline properties (सक्षार/alkaline), increase Pitta, and pacify Kapha and Vata.
They improve appetite (रोचन/taste booster), kindle digestive fire (दीपन/digestion booster), are heart-pleasing, relieve prostatic enlargement (अष्ठीला/prostatic growth/tumor) and flatulence, and are light.
Mrinala (मृणाल/lotus stalk), Bisa (बिस/lotus stem), Shaluka (शालूक/lotus tuber), Shringataka (शृंगाटक/water chestnut), and Kaseruka (कसेरुक/water chestnut tuber);
Nandi, Mashaka, Keluta, Kraunchadana, and Kalodyaka tubers;
Along with Satarudha and Kadamba shoots are dry (रूक्ष/dry), bowel-binding (ग्राहि/binder), cooling, and heavy to digest.
Kalamba, Natika, Marsha, Kutinjara, and Kutumbaka greens;
Chilli, Nishpava leaves, Latvaka, Kuruthaka, and Gavedhuka shoots;
Jatuka, Salakalyani, Triparni, and Peeluparnika greens;
Kumarajeeva, Lonika (लोणिका/purslane), Yavashaka, and Suvarchala greens;
Kukunda, Nalinimustaka, Dhumakala, and Lakshmana greens;
Various edible yam tubers (आलुक/yam tuber), legume leaves (सूप्य/pulses/legumes), and Ralaka greens;
Jeevantaka, Chanchuparni, Prapunnata, and Kutheraka greens;
Are sweet, dry (रूक्ष/dry), slightly salty (सलवण/salty), increase Vata and Kapha, and are heavy to digest.
They are cooling, increase stool and urine volume (सृष्टविण्मूत्र/laxative diuretic), and digest with abdominal distension (विष्टंभ्य/constipating).
Steamed leafy greens whose bitter juice is squeezed out and cooked with ample ghee/oil (स्नेहाढ्य/rich in unction) do not vitiate humors severely.
Small-leaved Chilli green (लघुपत्र चिल्ली/small-leaved goosefoot) possesses properties identical to Vastuka (वास्तुक/lambsquarters green).
Tarkari and Varuna shoots are sweet, slightly bitter, and pacify Kapha and Vata.
Varshabhu (वर्षाभू/red punarnava) and Kalashaka greens are alkaline (सक्षार/alkaline), pungent-bitter, and kindle digestion.
They clear bowels (भेदन/laxative), cure artificial poisons (गर/toxins), edema (शोफ/swelling), Kapha, and Vata.
Chirabilva shoots (चिरबिल्व/holoptelea shoots) kindle appetite (दीपन/digestion booster), pacify Kapha-Vata, and are laxative (सार/refined essence).
Eranda shoots (उरुबुक/castor shoots) and Langali shoots are light, warm, laxative, and bitter in taste.
Tilavetasa shoots increase Vata, are pungent, bitter, sour, and clear bowel obstruction (भेदन/purgative/piercing).
Panchangula shoots share similar properties, while Bamboo shoots (वंशकरीर/bamboo shoots) cause burning sensation (विदाही/burning sensation causing).
Bamboo shoots increase Vata and Pitta, are dry (रूक्ष/dry), have pungent vipaka, and pacify Kapha.
Bilva, Rasna, and Bala leafy greens pacify Vata and cure severe diarrhea (अतिसारजित्/diarrhea conqueror).
Vatsadani shoots pacify Vata; Kanjeera and Chitraka shoots pacify Kapha.
Pattura greens kindle appetite (दीपन/digestion booster), are bitter, and cure spleen disorders (प्लीह/spleen), piles (अर्शः/piles), Kapha, and Vata.
Kasamarda greens (कासमर्द/negro coffee) overcome worms (कृमि/parasites), cough (कास/cough), Kapha nausea, and asthma.
Kusumbha greens (कुसुंभ/safflower leaves) are dry, warm, sour, heavy, increase Pitta, and clear bowels (सार/refined essence).
Mustard greens (सर्षप शाक/mustard leaves) are alkaline, sweet, unctuous, warm, heavy, and the worst among leafy greens.
Mustard greens bind stool and urine (बद्धविण्मूत्र/constipating), and vitiate all three humors (सर्वदोषकृत्/aggravating all three humors).
Tender radish (बाल मूलक/tender young radish) with unmanifest taste, slight alkaline-bitter flavor, pacifies humors.
Tender radish is light to digest (लघु/light), warm in potency (उष्ण/heating), and cures humoral disorders.
It cures abdominal tumors (गुल्म/phantom tumor), cough (कास/cough), wasting (क्षय/emaciation), asthma (श्वास/asthma), ulcers, eye and throat ailments.
It cures hoarseness of voice (स्वरभेद/hoarseness), low digestive fire, upward gas movement (उदावर्त/reverse peristalsis/upward gas), and rhinitis (पीनस/chronic rhinitis).
Large mature radish (बृहत् मूलक/large mature radish) is dry, warm, pungent, sweet in vipaka, and vitiates all three humors (सर्वदोषकृत्/aggravating all three humors).
Large radish cooked with unctuous ghee/oil (स्निग्ध सिद्ध/cooked with unction) becomes heavy, clogging, yet pacifies Vata humor (वातजित्/vata conqueror).
Dried radish (शुष्क मूलक/dried radish) pacifies Vata and Kapha, whereas raw undigested radish vitiates all humors.
Pindalu yam is pungent, warm, pacifies Vata and Kapha, and increases Pitta humor.
Kuthera, Shigru (शिग्रु/drumstick leaves), Surasa (सुरसा/tulsi), Sumukha, Asuri, and Bhustrina aromatics;
Dhanyaka (धान्यक/coriander), Tumbaru, Shaileya, Yavani (यवानी/carom), and Shringavera (शृंगवेर/fresh ginger);
Parnasha, Gunjana (गुंजन/wild onion), Ajaaji (अजाजी/cumin), Kandira, and Jalapippali;
Phanijja, Arjaka, Jambeera (जंबीर/lemon leaves), Kharashwa, and Kalamalika;
Dipyaka, Kshavaka, Dveepi, and Vastagandha greens bind stool (बद्धविट्/constipating).
They are pungent in taste and vipaka, excite humors slightly, and are light to digest.
They cause burning sensation (विदाही/burning), are dry, sharp, warm, and destroy eyesight, semen, and intestinal worms.
This Haritaka group (हरितक वर्ग/fresh aromatic greens) is used as side dishes, garnishes, and appetiizers.
They flavor dishes, are pleasing to heart (हृद्य/cardiac tonic), kindle appetite (दीपन/digestion booster), and improve taste (रोचन/taste kindling).
They relieve hiccups (हिध्मा/hiccups), cough (कास/cough), toxins (विष/poisons), asthma (श्वास/asthma), flank pain, and foul body odor.
Surasa and Sumukha tulsi varieties relieve swelling (शोफ/edema) and artificial poison (गरविष/slow artificial poison).
Fresh Coriander leaves (धान्यक/coriander) are astringent, bitter, sweet, diuretic, and do not increase Pitta.
Kharashwa relieves bladder colic (बस्तिशूलघ्नी/bladder pain cure); Chitraka is a supreme digestive fire booster (दीपन/kindling digestion).
Garlic leaves (लशुन पत्र/garlic leaves) are alkaline-sweet; middle stalk is sweet-slimy; bulb is sharp and warm.
Garlic bulb (लशुन कंद/garlic bulb) is pungent in taste and vipaka, laxative (सर/laxative), heart-pleasing, and good for hair.
Garlic is heavy (गुरु/heavy), aphrodisiac (वृष्य/virility booster), unctuous, kindles digestion, and digests chyme (पाचन/digestive/chyme transformer).
Garlic unites fractured bones (भग्नसंधानकृत्/fracture healer), gives strength (बल्य/strength), but vitiates blood and Pitta.
It cures leucoderma (किलास/vitiligo/white spots), skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin diseases/leprosy), abdominal tumors (गुल्म/phantom tumor/mass), piles (अर्शः/hemorrhoids/piles), diabetes (मेह/urinary disorder/diabetes), worms, and Vata-Kapha disorders.
Garlic acts as a rejuvenative (रसायन/rejuvenative elixir) curing hiccups, rhinitis, asthma, and chronic cough.
Onion (पलांडु/onion) possesses properties similar to garlic but is slightly inferior, being sweet in vipaka.
Onion increases Kapha (कफकृत्/kapha increaser), does not increase Pitta, and is a standalone pacifier of Vata humor (केवलअनिलनाशन/standalone vata pacifier).
Surana elephant-yam (सूरण/elephant foot yam) kindles appetite (दीपन/digestion booster), improves taste, pacifies Kapha, is clear, and light to digest.
Surana yam is specifically wholesome for hemorrhoids (अर्शस्/piles), whereas Bhukanda yam vitiates humors severely.
Heaviness increases sequentially from flower (पुष्प/flowers), leaf (पत्र/leaves), fruit (फल/fruits), stalk (नल/reed stalk), to root tuber (कंद/tuber root).
Among leafy vegetables (शाक/leafy vegetables), Jeevanti is the best (वरा/triphala), while Mustard greens (सर्षप/mustard seed) are the worst (अवर/inferior).
Thus ends the classification of vegetables and leafy greens (शाकवर्ग/vegetable classification group).
Raisins (द्राक्षा/grapes) are supreme among fruits (फलोत्तमा/supreme fruit/grapes), aphrodisiac, good for eyes, and clear urine and stool.
Raisins are sweet in taste and vipaka, unctuous (स्निग्ध/oily), slightly astringent, cooling (हिम/cooling), and heavy.
They overcome Vata, Pitta, blood disorders, bitter mouth taste, and alcohol intoxication (मदात्यय/alcoholism).
Raisins cure thirst (तृष्णा/thirst), cough (कास/cough), fever (ज्वर/fever), asthma (श्वास/asthma), hoarseness, and chest wounds (क्षतक्षय/chest wound wasting).
Sweet Pomegranate (स्वादु दाडिम/sweet pomegranate) overcomes excessive Pitta and all three humors (त्रिदोषजित्/tridosha pacifier).
Sweet pomegranate does not conflict with Pitta, is not overly hot, and sour pomegranate pacifies Vata and Kapha.
All pomegranates are heart-pleasing (हृद्य/cardiac tonic), light, unctuous, bowel-binding (ग्राहि/binder), appetizing, and kindle digestion.
Mocha, Kharjura (खर्जूर/dates), Panasa (पनस/jackfruit), Narikela (नारिकेल/coconut), and Parushaka (परूषक/phalsa);
Amrataka, Tala (ताल/toddy palm fruit), Kashmarya (काश्मर्य/gambhari fruit), Rajadana, and Madhuka fruits;
Sauveera, Badara (बदर/jujube), Ankolha, Phalgu (फल्गु/wild fig), and Shleshmataka fruits;
Vatama (वाताम/almond), Abhishuka, Akshoda (अक्षोड/walnut), Mukulaka, and Nikochaka nuts;
Urumana and Priyala nuts are body-building (बृंहण/nourishing), heavy (गुरु/heavy), and cooling (शीत/cooling).
They cure burning sensation (दाह/burning heat), chest injury, wasting (क्षय/depletion/wasting), and purify blood and Pitta (रक्तपित्तप्रसादन/bleeding pacifier).
They are sweet in taste and vipaka, unctuous, cause constipation/flatulence (विष्टंभी/constipating/clogging), and increase Kapha and semen.
Coconut fruit (नारिकेल/coconut) is heavy, unctuous, pacifies Pitta, sweet, and cooling in potency.
Coconut gives strength and muscle bulk (बलमांसकर/muscle builder), is heart-pleasing, body-building, and purifies urinary bladder (बस्तिशोधन/bladder cleanser).
Mocha plantain fruit (कदली/banana) is sweet in taste, astringent, not overly cold, and cures bleeding disorders.
Banana is aphrodisiac (वृष्य/virility booster), appetizing, increases Kapha, and is heavy to digest.
Rajadana fruit is unctuous, sweet, astringent, and heavy to digest.
Tala palm fruit increases Pitta; Kashmarya gambhari fruit is laxative (सर/laxative) and cooling (हिम/cooling).
Kashmarya cures stool and urinary obstruction, promotes hair growth (केश्य/hair tonic), enhances memory, and acts as a rejuvenative (रसायन/rejuvenative elixir).
Madhuka fruit is unpleasing to heart; Badara jujube fruit (बदर/jujube) is laxative in nature.
Almonds and walnuts (वातामादि/almond nut group) are hot in potency (उष्णवीर्य/heating), increase Kapha and Pitta, and are laxative.
Priyala nuts are supreme for pacifying Vata, unctuous, and non-heating in potency.
Priyala nut kernel (पियालमज्जा/priyala seed kernel) is sweet, aphrodisiac, and pacifies Pitta and Vata humors.
Jujube seed kernel (कोल मज्जा/jujube seed kernel) possesses similar properties, curing vomiting (छर्दि/vomiting), thirst (तृष्णा/thirst), and cough.
Tinduka, Ashmantaka, Aasina, Phalini, Bimbi, and Todana fruits;
Tanka, Ashvakarna, Bakula, Gangeru, Dhava, and Dhanvana fruits;
Shwetapaka, Kapittha, Sinchitika, Bhavya, and Jambu (जंबू/jamun) fruits;
Fruits of Ksheerivriksha trees and Pushkara lotus seeds pacify Kapha and Pitta.
They are astringent-sweet, dry (रूक्ष/dry), cooling (शीतल/cooling), heavy, and tissue-scraping (लेखन/scraping/emaciating).
They cause constipation, abdominal distension (विष्टंभ/constipation/flatulence), stiffness, and aggravate Vata humor.
Unripe Kapittha fruit (कपित्थ/wood apple) hurts throat, is astringent-sour, and vitiates all three humors.
Ripe Kapittha fruit is appetizing, astringent-sour, sweet, and cures hiccups (हिध्मा/hiccups) and vomiting (वमि/vomiting).
Ripe Kapittha pacifies humoral disorders and is praised in preparing flavored fruit drinks (खांडवारिष्ट/sweet fermented wine).
Unripe and ripe Kapittha both neutralize poisons (विषघ्न/antidote/anti-toxic) and are bowel-binding (ग्राही/bowel binder/astringent).
Sinchitika apple fruit is body-building (बृंहण/nourishing), pacifies Vata and Pitta, and is unctuous.
Bhavya fruit is clear and sour, while Jambu jamun fruit (जंबू/black plum) increases Vata severely.
Jambu fruit causes abdominal distension, hurts throat, while sour Ksheerivriksha fruits are sour and heavy.
Akshaki fruit pacifies Pitta and Kapha, is sour, and increases Vata humor.
Tender unripe mango (बाल आम्र/tender unripe mango) is astringent, pungent, sour, dry, and causes blood-Pitta vitiation.
Fully grown sour mango (संपूर्ण आम्र/fully mature mango) causes bleeding Pitta disorders and increases Kapha.
Ripe sweet mango (पक्व आम्र/ripe sweet mango) is sweet-sour, heavy, unctuous, pacifies Vata, and does not increase Pitta.
Ripe mango is pleasing to heart, increases Kapha, muscle tissue, semen, and body stamina.
Fragrant sweet mango juice (सहकार रस/fragrant mango juice) is heart-pleasing, unctuous, appetizing, kindles digestion, pacifies Pitta-Vata, and purifies semen and blood.
Lavali fruit is astringent, appetizing, pleasing to heart, and increases Vata.
Unripe Bilva fruit (बिल्व/bael fruit) is heavy, dampens digestive fire, vitiates humors, but pacifies Vata.
Ripe Bilva fruit is heavy, sharp, increases Pitta, but kindles digestive fire (दीपन/kindling digestion).
Both unripe and ripe Bilva pacify Vata and Kapha, are warm, unctuous, and supreme bowel-binders (ग्राही/bowel binder/astringent).
Uksha fruit is sour, bowel-binding, dry, warm, light, appetizing, and kindles digestion.
Uksha pacifies Vata and Kapha; Koshamra fruit is slightly inferior to Uksha.
Karanja fruit causes abdominal distension (विष्टंभी/constipating/clogging) and does not conflict with Pitta and Kapha.
Shami fruit is heavy, warm, sweet, dry, and hurts hair growth (केशघ्न/harmful to hair).
Peelu fruit (पीलु/toothbrush tree) is pungent in taste and vipaka, sharp, warm, clears bowels, and increases Pitta.
Peelu cures worms (कृमि/parasites), abdominal tumors (गुल्म/phantom tumor/mass), spleen disease (प्लीहा/spleen organ), piles, and Vata-Kapha disorders.
Sweet Peelu variety is not overly hot and pacifies all three humors (त्रिदोषजित्/tridosha pacifier).
Neepa, Shatakshika, Prachinamalaka, and Trinashulya fruits are slightly inferior to Peelu.
Ingudi fruit and Vikankata fruit possess properties similar to Peelu.
Rind of Matulunga citron (मातुलुंग त्वक्/citron rind) is bitter, pungent, unctuous, and pacifies Vata.
Citron fleshy pulp (मातुलुंग मांस/citron pulp) is sweet, body-nourishing, heavy, and pacifies Vata and Pitta.
Citron inner filaments (मातुलुंग केसर/citron filaments) are light, curing cough, asthma, hiccups, and alcoholism (मदात्यय/alcoholism).
Citron filaments cure dry mouth, Vata-Kapha constipation, vomiting, anorexia, abdominal tumors, and colic pain.
Fruit rind and flesh of Bhallataka (भल्लातक/marking nut) are sweet, cooling, and body-building.
Bhallataka nut kernel (भल्लातकास्थि/marking nut kernel) is like fire in potency, enhances memory (मेध्य/brain tonic), and pacifies Kapha and Vata.
Palevata fruit is sweet-sour, cooling or warm, heavy, and exists in two varieties.
Aaruka peach fruit is appetizing, sweet, and dampens excessive digestive fire.
Ripe Aaruka digests quickly, is not overly hot, but is heavy and vitiates humors if overeaten.
Raw unripe grapes, phalsa, and mango are sour and increase Pitta and Kapha.
Karamarda fruit (करमर्द/karonda) is heavy, hot in potency, pacifies Vata, and is laxative.
Kola, Karkandhu, Likucha, and Anatakaruka fruits are sour in taste.
Airavata, Dantashatha, Nooda, and Mrigalandika are sour fruits.
Ripe and dried Karamarda fruits do not increase Pitta severely.
Dried sour fruits of Amlika tamarind and Kola jujube kindle digestion, clear bowels, relieve thirst and fatigue.
Dried tamarind and jujube are light, pleasant, and pacify Kapha and Vata.
Among all fruits (फल/fruits), Likucha (लिकुच/monkey jack) is the worst (अवर/inferior), vitiating all three humors (सर्वदोषकृत्/aggravating all three humors).
Likucha causes blood disorders, skin diseases, impotency, and dampens digestive fire.
Thus ends the classification of fruits (फलवर्ग/fruit classification group).
Fruit properties (फल गुण/fruit qualities): sweet and sour in taste (मधुर अम्ल/sweet-sour), refreshing (तर्पण/refreshing), and quenches thirst (तृष्णाघ्न/thirst quencher).

Saindhava rock salt (सैंधव/rock salt) is sweet-astringent in taste, supreme among salts, beneficial for eyesight (चक्षुष्य/beneficial for vision), appetizing, and non-heating.
Samudra sea salt (समुद्र/sea salt) is sweet in vipaka, heavy to digest, and increases Kapha and Pitta.
Vida salt (विड/vid salt) is sharp (तीक्ष्ण/sharp), hot in potency, kindles digestion, and clears flatus and bowel blockages.
Sauvarchala black salt (सौवर्चल/black salt) is light, fragrant, heart-pleasing, clears throat, and cures abdominal colic.
Romaka salt is sharp, laxative, pungent in vipaka, and penetrates tissues rapidly.
Audbhida salt (औद्भिद/alkaline soil salt) is slightly bitter, sharp, laxative, and penetrates tissue channels.
Yavakshara (यवक्षार/barley ash alkali) cures urinary stones, dysuria, abdominal tumors, and spleen disorders.
Svarjikakshara alkali is sharp, hot, cures abdominal distension, and digests heavy food.
Fresh Ginger (आर्द्रक/shunthi) kindles digestion (दीपन/appetite booster), improves taste, pacifies Vata and Kapha, and clears throat.
Dry Ginger (शुंठी/dry ginger) is sweet in vipaka, unctuous, aphrodisiac, heart-pleasing, and pacifies Vata.
Black Pepper (मरिच/black pepper) is sharp, pungent, light, dry, kindles digestion, and pacifies Kapha-Vata.
Pippali long pepper (पिप्पली/long pepper) in fresh state increases Kapha; in dry state it pacifies Vata and Kapha.
Dry Pippali is sweet in vipaka, unctuous, non-heating, aphrodisiac, and acts as a rejuvenative (रसायन/rejuvenative elixir).
Combination of Shunthi, Maricha, and Pippali is called Trikatu (त्रिकटु/three pungents), digesting chyme (आमपाचन/chyme toxin digestion).
Asafoetida (हिंगु/hing) is sharp, hot, unctuous, kindles digestion, clears flatus, and cures abdominal colic (शूलघ्न/colic pain reliever).
Cumin (जीरक/cumin) and Caraway (कुरुविंदा/black cumin) are sweet-pungent, warm, light, appetizing, and good for eyesight.
Mustard seeds (सर्षप/mustard) are sharp, hot, unctuous, increase Pitta, and destroy skin diseases and parasites.
Ajamoda celery seed (अजमोद/celery seed) is bitter-pungent, warm, light, kindles digestion, and cures urinary colic.
Fenugreek (मेथिका/fenugreek) is bitter, warm, pacifies Vata, and kindles digestive fire.
Coriander (धान्यक/coriander) is sweet-astringent, cooling, diuretic, and pacifies all three humors.
Fennel seeds (शतपुष्पा/fennel) are warm, sweet-pungent, pacify Vata and Kapha, and improve vision.
Cardamom (एला/cardamom) is light, fragrant, pleasing to heart, and cures dyspnea, cough, and nausea.
Cinnamon bark (त्वक्/cinnamon) and Cinnamon leaf (पत्र/bay leaf) are warm, fragrant, and improve taste and appetite.
Nagakeshara (नागकेशर/ironwood flower) is astringent-pungent, fragrant, cooling, and cures bleeding disorders.
Combination of Twak, Ela, and Patra is called Trijata (त्रिजात/three aromatics); adding Nagakeshara makes Chaturjata (चतुर्जात/four aromatic spices).
Camphor (कर्पूर/camphor) is cooling, fragrant, clears mouth odor, and pacifies Pitta and blood heat.
Nutmeg (जातिफल/nutmeg) is light, sharp, warm, improves taste, and cures diarrhea and abdominal colic.
Cloves (लवंग/cloves) are cooling, light, heart-pleasing, improve vision, and cure thirst and cough.
Sandalwood (चंदन/sandalwood) is cooling, bitter-sweet, fragrant, relieves burning heat (दाह/burning sensation), and purifies blood.
Vetiver (उशीर/khus vetiver) is cooling, relieves thirst, fever, and burning sensation.
Turmeric (हरिद्रा/turmeric) is bitter-pungent, warm, dry, improves complexion, and cures skin diseases and diabetes (मेह/urinary disorder/diabetes).
Daruharidra (दारुहरिद्रा/tree turmeric) possesses properties similar to turmeric, specifically curing eye and throat diseases.
Guduchi (गुडूची/giloy) is bitter-sweet, rejuvenative (रसायनी/rejuvenative herb), pacifies all three humors, and cures fevers.
Ghee cooked with digestive herbs acts as a supreme rejuvenative and kindles internal digestive fire.
Fresh butter (नवनीत/fresh butter) and ghee (घृत/cow ghee) treat facial paralysis (अर्धित/facial paralysis) and nervous exhaustion.
Wheat (गोधूम/wheat) cooked in ghee is vision-enhancing (चक्षुष्य/beneficial for vision), whereas oil-fried wheat harms vision.
Raw radish causes humoral vitiation, but radish cooked in unctuous ghee/oil becomes non-vitiating (अदोषल/non-vitiating).
Heated honey (उष्ण मधु/heated honey) turns toxic like poison, though honey used in emetic formulations is safe.
Raisins stored in improper containers turn excessively sour and vitiate bodily humors.
Thick dry paste of sandalwood (चंदन लेप/sandalwood paste) traps body heat and causes burning sensation if left unwashed.
Aguru paste traps inner body heat when applied cold, but relieves cold when applied warm.
Sesame seeds (तिल/sesame) enhance intellect and are cooling to touch, while sesame oil is heating.
Sesame seeds increase Kapha slightly, whereas sesame oil and oil-cake (खल/oil cake residue) do not.
Curds (दधि/yogurt) cause facial and tissue edema (श्वयथु/swelling/edema), whereas buttermilk (तक्र/spiced buttermilk) and butter do not.
Curd, milk, and caper shoots (करीर/caper shoot) are naturally wholesome for inhabitants of desert regions (मरु देश/arid desert region).
Alkali preparations are customary in eastern lands; fish in coastal regions; rock salt in mountain regions.
Oil and sour tubers are customary in Malabar and Konkan regions.
Gruel (पेया/thin rice gruel) and roasted grain churns (मंथ/churned herbal drink) are customary in northern lands; wheat in Avanti region.
Inhabitants of Bahlika, Pahlava, and Yavana regions are accustomed to meat, wheat, wine, and sharp spices.
Their bodies are hardened to weapons and fire, making those heavy foods wholesome for them.
Ghee, milk, wine, and meat are naturally wholesome (सात्म्य/wholesome habit) for certain individual constitutions.
Gruel suits one person, meat soup another, wheat suits one, and rice suits another.
Even unwholesome foods become wholesome (सात्म्य/wholesome habit) through long-standing habituation (ओकसात्म्य/habitual adaptation).
Food, drinks, and medicines administered considering disease, dosage, season, and habituation yield rapid strength.
Properly habituated food (सात्म्य आहार/wholesome adapted food) provides immediate vitality without causing severe humoral vitiation.
Cow milk (गव्य क्षीर/cow milk) is naturally laxative, yet acts as bowel-binding in certain unique individual constitutions.
Clear grain gruel (मंद/dull/slow digestion) kindles digestive fire, especially during cold seasons with Vata-Kapha dominance.
Even milk processed with anti-toxic herbs increases venom inside a venomous snake's body.
Emetic and purgative evacuations are contraindicated in obese, elderly, and infant patients.
Raw crocodile meat destroys Kapha in stomach, but creates Kapha congestion in throat.
Raisins and dates pacify Vata generally, but generate Vata gas in the gastrointestinal tract (कोष्ठ/gastrointestinal tract).
Wild peacock meat is wholesome for hearing, voice, youth, and eyesight.
Substances cooling to touch suit eyesight, while warm substances suit hearing organs.
Milk is sweet, laxative, and cooling, whereas curd is sour, heavy, and heating.
Over time, milk transforms into curd, and curd transforms into butter and ghee.
Milk and curd pacify Vata, but unfermented immature curd aggravates Vata humor.
Buttermilk (तक्र/spiced buttermilk) is astringent-sour and bowel-binding; sour buttermilk acts as a laxative.
Dhataki flowers, jaggery, and water are the essential fermenting triggers for wines (आसव/fermented infusion) and vinegars (शुक्त/fermented acidic wine).
In cold winter seasons, unctuous, sour, and salty diets are wholesome at the beginning and end of meals.
Drinking churned grain drinks (उदमंथ/water-mixed barley drink) and day sleep (दिवास्वप्न/daytime sleeping) are forbidden except in summer.
Seasonal changes cause natural accumulation (चय/accumulation phase) of Vata, Pitta, and Kapha even with balanced diet.
Seasonal dietary regimens vary in taste, dryness, unctuousness, and strength across the six seasons.
Kakamachi is a rejuvenative (रसायन/rejuvenative elixir), but when cooked and kept overnight (पर्युषित/stale overnight food), it acts as poison.
Tender radish (बाल मूलक/tender young radish) pacifies humors, whereas large mature radish acts in the opposite manner.
In acute fever (ज्वर/fever), thin gruels (पेया/thin rice gruel), herbal decoctions (कषाय/astringent taste), ghee, milk, and purgatives are used in 6-day stages.
Each 6-day period (षडह/six-day period) should be monitored according to disease strength before progressing therapy.
Emesis therapy (वमन/therapeutic emesis) is indicated in vomiting, heart disease, and abdominal tumors (गुल्म/phantom tumor/mass).
Enema therapy (बस्ति/medicated enema) is specifically indicated in hemorrhoids (अर्शः/hemorrhoids/piles) and skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin diseases/leprosy).
In fever, matching season and humor (तुल्यऋतुदोषत्व/identical seasonal humor) makes the disease easily curable (सुखसाध्य/easily curable).
In diabetes (प्रमेह/obstinate urinary disorders/diabetes), matching affected tissue (तुल्यदूष्यता/identical tissue vulnerability) determines easy curability.
In phantom uterine tumors (रक्तगुल्म/uterine blood tumor), maturity beyond 10 months ensures easy management.
Eye drops (आश्च्योतन/eye drops) in conjunctivitis (अभिष्यंद/channel oozing/clogging) should be applied after 3 days of acute onset.
Eye collyrium (अंजन/collyrium) is applied when humor is mature (पक्व दोष/ripened humors), and nasal drops (नावन/nasal drops) in rhinitis.
Venesection (सिरामोक्ष/venesection/vein puncturing) is indicated in early cataract (तिमिर/cataract/blurriness) before deep visual loss occurs.
Bloodletting (सिराव्यध/vein puncturing) is indicated in vitiated blood diseases, but forbidden in active internal hemorrhage (अस्रपित्त/bleeding disorder).
Even unwholesome food served at night to eye patients acts as harmful food (अपथ्य/unwholesome).
Dry roasted grain flour (सक्तु/roasted barley flour) is unwholesome generally, but wholesome for diabetic patients (प्रमेही/diabetic patient).
For abdominal tumor patients (गुल्मी/tumor patient), milk and curd processed with Hapusha and digestive herbs are wholesome.
Honey (मधु/honey) is recommended during monsoon season (वर्षा/monsoon season) to pacify Vata, despite honey's dry quality.
Wine acts as an antidote for excessive wine intoxication, and specific poison acts as antidote for another poison.
Ghee prepared in marshy regions (आनूप देश/marshy habitat) and consumed in winter (हेमंत/early winter season) provides superior body strength.
Lethargy and heaviness appearing in Vata fevers subside with mild warm fomentation (स्वेद/sweat waste).
Burning sensation caused by garlic or lotus remedies subsides with cooling applications.
Food cooked yesterday and eaten today (पर्युषित/stale overnight food) digests through day sleep in exceptional cases.
Digestive fire trapped in gut by abdominal fat or piles causes severe digestive impairment.
Medicated ghee is difficult to digest when fire is low, whereas milk kindles digestion under proper conditions.
Scorpions (वृश्चिक/scorpion) are born spontaneously from decomposing snake carcasses and organic matter.
Scorpions bitten by their own offspring lose life immediately due to concentrated venom.
Their own intense venom does not afflict them internally during normal lifespan.
Their venom pervades their whole body tissue like reproductive semen (शुक्रवत्/pervading like semen).
Their flesh is described in the meat section (मांसवर्ग/meat classification group) for therapeutic utility.
Excreta of houseflies (मक्षिकाविट्/fly droppings) cures vomiting, whereas housefly itself induces vomiting.
Though Kapha disorders warrant fasting (लंघन/fasting/depletion therapy), fasting in acute fever with abdominal tumor requires caution.
Even in identical region and season, excessive fasting (लंघन/fasting/depletion therapy) is improper for weak patients.
Buttermilk (तक्र/spiced buttermilk) overcomes all humoral disorders in IBS (ग्रहणी/irritable bowel syndrome), but raw unfermented milk vitiates humors.
In rhinitis, asthma, and cough, properly processed medicated preparations are highly praised.
These and many other subtle pharmacological principles have rare complex underlying causes.
A brief demonstration of these subtle actions (कर्मवैचित्र्य/diverse karma results) has been presented here.
An intelligent physician (बुद्धिमान्/wise practitioner) should infer remaining unspoken principles following these guidelines.
One should not rely blindly on scripture alone without practical reasoning, nor ignore classical authority.
Thus ends the section on dietary quantities and food properties (मात्रादि प्रकरण/portion size section).
Thus ends the seventh chapter (सप्तमः अध्यायः/seventh chapter) named "Nature of Food Substances" (अन्नस्वरूपविज्ञानीयः/knowledge of food nature) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the rules for food protection (अन्नरक्षा/food safety).
For rulers and wealthy lords (ईश्वर/rulers), especially kings (राजा/king), enemies (अमित्र/enemies) are generally more than friends.
Therefore, persons employed by enemies attempt to give poison (विष/poison) in food and drinks (अन्नपान/food and beverages).
Even royal women (स्त्री/women) employed by agents may do so out of desire for supreme favor (सौभाग्य/favor).
Therefore, the king should employ an expert physician (प्राणाचार्य/royal doctor) who is noble, faithful, devoted, righteous, skilled (दक्ष/capable), well-versed in Ayurvedic eight branches (अष्टांग/eight branches), and proficient in antidote formulations (अगद/antidote).
The king should honor him with wealth and respect (अर्थ मान/wealth and honor) as a disciple honors a teacher (गुरु/master) or a son honors a father (पिता/father).
Even if his advice feels unfavorable (प्रतिकूल/unpalatable), the king should regard it as timely and beneficial (हित/wholesome).
For even a noble elephant (गजपति/king elephant) without a control hook (अंकुश/goad) is not praised by people.
Therefore, the king should entrust his diet and daily routine (आहार विहार/diet and lifestyle) entirely to the physician.
Even an attained lifespan (जीवित/life span) stays protected within its time through proper medical measures (उपाय/remedy).
Furthermore (अपिच/moreover):
Kings have many responsibilities, and swift-acting diseases like colic and collapse (शूल संन्यास/colic and syncope) require constant medical monitoring.
Because kings are surrounded by distractions, intolerant of pain, prone to unwholesome desires, and encircled by flattering attendants (परिवार/entourage).
Therefore, the king should make the physician reside right near the royal palace (राजगृह/royal mansion).
So that (तथाहि/for instance):
He can directly supervise (अपरोक्ष/direct vision) all equipments and items used for the king's body.
The physician should stand before the king, holding protective herbs (औषधि/medicines), and serve thoroughly tested, pure food that was first offered to fire (अग्नि/fire) and inspected by peacocks (शिखि/peacock).
While the king is eating, drums smeared with anti-poison antidotes (अगद/antidote drums) should be sounded.
In poisoned food (सविष अन्न/poisoned food), liquid boiled food becomes non-dripping, cooks slowly, appears stale immediately, turns cold, loses normal color, and shows moon-like circular spots (चंद्रिका/circular spots).
Side dishes dry up quickly, broth turns dark/blackish, shadows appear distorted or invisible, and foam layers, thread-like lines, and bubbles (फेन बुद्बुद/foam and bubbles) appear on top.
Especially in salty preparations (लवण/salty food), a dense layer of foam (फेनमाला/foam ring) forms.
In meat soup (रस/meat broth), a blue line (नील राजी/blue streak) appears in the middle.
In milk (पयस्/milk), a copper-colored line (ताम्र/copper hue) appears.
In wine and water (मद्य तोय/alcohol and water), a black line (काली राजी/black streak) appears.
In curd (दधि/curd), a greyish line (श्यावा/grey streak) appears.
In buttermilk (तक्र/buttermilk), a bluish-yellow line (नील पीता/bluish yellow streak) appears.
In whey/liquid milk part (मस्तु/whey), a pigeon-grey color (कपोताभा/pigeon color) appears.
In fermented grain gruel (धान्याम्ल/sour gruel), a black color (कृष्णा/black) appears.
In liquid medicine (द्रव औषध/liquid drug), a brownish color (कपिला/brown hue) appears.
In ghee (घृत/clarified butter), a watery appearance (सलिलाभा/water-like look) appears.
In honey (क्षौद्र/honey), a greenish color (हरिता/greenish hue) appears.
In oil (तैल/oil), a reddish tint (अरुणा/reddish color) and fat smell (वसा गंध/fat odor) appear.
Unripe fruits (आम फल/unripe fruit) ripen prematurely when poisoned.
Ripe fruits (पक्व फल/ripe fruit) undergo rapid putrefaction and rotting (प्रकोथ/decay).
Wet/fresh substances (आर्द्र द्रव्य/moist items) suddenly wither (म्लानता/wilting) and lose their natural state.
Dry substances (शुष्क द्रव्य/dry items) turn dark, blackish, and discolored (वैवर्ण्य/discoloration).
Hard articles (कठिन द्रव्य/hard items) become soft (मृदु/soft).
Soft articles (मृदु द्रव्य/soft items) become hard (कठिन/hard).
Garlands (माल्य/flower garland) wither, lose fragrance (गंध नाश/loss of smell), and their flower tips burst open.
Bedspreads and garments (वस्त्र/clothing) show dark patches, threads separate, and hair fibers fall off.
Metallic and gem articles (लोह मणि/metals and gems) get coated with dirt, lose shine, weight, color, and smooth touch.
The poisoner (विषद/poison giver), terrified of his crime, shows sweating, trembling (वेपथु/shivering), dry dark face, and looks around anxiously (सोद्वेग/anxiously).
He looks with special anxiety toward the place where he introduced the poison.
His upper garment slips, he hides behind pillars or walls, walks falteringly, feels ashamed, answers incoherently when asked, stammers, cracks fingers, scratches head, licks lips, yawns, scrapes the ground, acts in haste, and cannot stand firm.
However, some innocent nervous persons (अनवस्थित/unsteady mind) may also act similarly due to fear of king's command or other faults.
Therefore, one must test food on fire (अग्नि/fire) and other means.
Fire coming in contact with poisoned food turns into a single swirl, produces dry dim flame, rainbow colors (इंद्रधनुष/rainbow hues), and loud crackling sounds (चटचटायते/crackles).
The smoke smells like a corpse (कुणप गंध/cadaver smell) and causes fainting, salivation, goosebumps, headache, rhinitis, and blurred vision (दृष्टि/eyesight trouble).
In such cases, one should apply nasal drops and eye salve (नस्य अंजन/nasal drops and collyrium) made of spikenard, saussurea, and cuscus grass with honey.
Or inhale smoke prepared from chaff-flower, vidanga, bala, chitrak, and grapes mixed with ghee and jaggery (घृत गुड/ghee and jaggery).
Fire may also show unusual flame due to fat or salt combinations.
Therefore, one should also test food using birds (वयांसि/birds).
Crows (काक/crow) eating poisoned food utter feeble harsh sounds (क्षाम स्वर/weak voice).
Flies (मक्षिका/houseflies) do not sit on poisoned food.
If flies sit on it, they die immediately (विपद्यंते/die).
Upon mere sight of poisoned food, the eyes of the Greek pheasant/Chakora bird (चकोर/chakora bird) turn red.
The voice of the cuckoo/Kokila (कोकिल/cuckoo) undergoes abnormal distortion.
The gait of the swan (हंस/swan) falters and stumbles.
The bhringaraja bird (भृंगराज/fork-tailed drongo) hums mournfully.
The demoiselle crane/Krouncha (क्रौंच/crane) becomes intoxicated.
The rooster/cock (कृकवाकु/rooster) crows loudly in distress.
The parrot (शुक/parrot) screams out aloud.
The maina bird (सारिका/starling maina) vomits (छर्दि/vomit).
The golden oriole (चामीकर/golden bird) flies away.
The duck/Karandava (कारंडव/water duck) dies on the spot.
The Jeevanjeevaka pheasant (जीवंजीवक/pheasant) faints and languishes.
The mongoose (नकुल/mongoose) gets erection of body hairs (हृष्ट रोमा/goosebumps).
The monkey (वानर/monkey) passes feces, the spotted deer (पृषत/spotted deer) cries, and the peacock (मयूर/peacock) becomes overjoyed; by peacock's mere sight, poison loses its intensity.
Vapor from poisoned food rises with a peacock-neck color (मयूर कंठ/peacock neck blue-green color).
Inhaling that vapor causes fainting and stupor (मूर्च्छा/fainting).
Treatment for that vapor inhalation is similar to poison smoke treatment.
Touching poisoned food with hands causes burning sensation, swelling, numbness, and falling of fingernails (नख शात/nail shedding).
For that, paste of indigo, cochineal insect, and blue water lily (श्याम इंद्रगोप उत्पल/indigo and water lily) should be applied.
Eating poisoned food causes tingling lips, burning inside mouth, heavy tongue root, jaw stiffness (हनुस्तंभ/jaw lock), tooth sensitivity, excessive salivation, and loss of taste perception.
In that condition, therapies prescribed for poison smoke and poisoned toothbrush should be performed.
When poison reaches the stomach (आमाशय/stomach), it causes sweating, intoxication, fainting, vomiting, discoloration, flatulence, shivering, burning, anorexia, and cardiac distress.
In that stage, emesis (वमन/vomiting) should be induced immediately using emetic nut, bottle gourd, and sponge gourd with curd and honey.
After proper emesis, when the body is oleated, purgation (विरेचन/purgation) should be administered.
Drink powder of triphala, trikatu, nagakeshara, liquorice, and peacock-feather ash mixed with bile (पित्त/bile) of lion, tiger, wolf, leopard, or iguana and honey; this is Jeevana Agada (जीवन अगद/life-restoring antidote), supreme cure for all poisons.
When that is digested, feed rice gruel cooked with trikatu, milk, and ghee (पयस् घृत/milk and ghee).
After gruel digestion, give light boiled rice with green gram soup cooked with trikatu and ghee.
Apply paste of liquorice, albizia, and sandalwood (चंदन/sandalwood) over the body.
When poison reaches the large intestine (पक्वाशय/large intestine), it causes thirst, burning, fainting, diarrhea, tympanites, sensory organ dysfunction, debility, emaciation, and anemia.
In that stage, administer purgation using ghee cooked with indigo fruit (नीलिनी/indigo).
Administer Dushi-vishari antidote (दूषीविषारि अगद/anti-latent-poison formulation) with honey and curd.
Using a poisoned toothbrush causes bristling brush fibers, drug odor, dryness, and swelling of tongue, teeth, lips, and oral gums.
Apply paste of dhátaki flowers, jamun seeds, and myrobalan with honey on gums.
Gargle (गंडूष/gargle) with juice of pomegranate, karamarda, mango, and plum mixed with honey.
By this, procedures for tongue scrapers, mouth gargles, and rinses are also explained.
Using poisoned eye salve causes tearing, discharge, burning, redness, pain, distorted vision, and blindness (आंध्य/blindness).
In that condition, drinking ghee (सर्पिः पान/ghee intake) is indicated.
Boil milk with long pepper and roasted grain flour.
Prepare ghee with that paste for soothing eye irrigation (नेत्र तर्पण/eye pooling).
Apply collyrium made of kapittha, meshashringi, and bhallataka flowers with varna resin.
Inhale or apply powder of brihati, albizia seeds, lotus, and nagabala steeped in honey.
Eye collyrium combined with antimony (स्त्रोतोंजन/antimony) and gold dust is beneficial.
Using poisoned nasal drops or snuff causes headache, phlegm discharge, nasal bleeding, and sensory impairment.
Administer ghee cooked with ativisha, kaakamachi, and madayantika in milk for nasal drops and drink.
Using poisoned massage oil causes skin burning, sweating, suppuration, eruptions, and skin cracking.
Sprinkle cold water and apply paste of sandalwood, ushir, kushta, lotus, and gulvel.
Drink the same formulation mixed with kapittha juice and cow urine (गोमूत्र/cow urine).
Apply paste of girikarnika, priyangu, sariva, liquorice, and bael root crushed in decanting water.
By this, rules for poisoned body pastes, powders, baths, ornaments, vehicles, beds, garments, armors, chariots, and footwear are explained.
Specifically for disorders caused by poisoned ornaments, apply paste of ashwagandha, apamarga, and khadir with cow bile (गोपित्त/ox bile).
For poisoned footstools, apply paste of shleshmataka and sarpasugandha with honey.
Using a poisoned umbrella causes rapid eruption and suppuration of blisters resembling ripe jamun berries.
Apply paste of liquorice, patala, kasheruka, lodhra, and khadir over the entire body.
By this, rules for poisoned royal fly-whisks and fans (चामर व्यजन/fly-whisk and fan) are also explained.
Using poisoned head massage oil causes head pain, skin nodules, and hair fall (केश च्यवन/hair fall).
Apply mud paste steeped in black nightshade, amaranth, ghee, and honey over the head.
Or apply cow dung juice, malati, soot from chimney (अगारधूम/kitchen soot), or wild rat dung paste.
Pour decoction of shleshmataka bark, patala, albizia, liquorice, and turmeric mixed with goat milk (अजा क्षीर/goat milk) over the head.
By this, rules for head baths, water, combs, garlands, and turbans (उष्णीष/turban) are explained.
Using poisoned ear drops causes ear swelling, earache, suppuration, and hearing loss (श्रोत्र वैगुण्य/deafness).
Fill ear with juice of bahuputra mixed with ghee and honey, or cold juice of somavalka.
Using poisoned face ointment causes facial dark spots, prickly skin eruptions, and massage oil symptoms.
Apply face pack of liquorice, milkweed, bandhujiva, and sandalwood with ghee, and drink ghee.
Smelling poisoned flowers causes headache, tearing eyes, and loss of smell sensation (गंध अज्ञान/anosmia).
In that condition, apply procedures mentioned above for smoke and vapor inhalation.
Here are verse stanzas (भवति चात्र/here are verses):
The wash-water of fruits, roots, and leaves (फल मूल प्रक्षालन/washing water) should be tested.
Similarly, the physician should carefully check vessels and side dishes without laxity.
First smell auspicious items, then touch them gently.
Add antidotes (प्रतिवाप/antidote addition) and wait for one nadika measure (नाडिका/24 minutes).
After verifying the purity of both vessel and water (भाजन उदक/vessel and water),
The king (वसुधाधिप/ruler of earth) should partake of his food appropriately.
Mild poison loses its potency when brought near strong anti-poison charms.
Therefore, a wise physician should tie potent anti-poison drugs (विषघ्न/anti-poison) on his hand.
Holding anti-poison charms bestows fortune, destroys evil spirits, and increases affection.
Furthermore (अपिच/moreover):
Medicine storehouse containing pots, crucibles, and drug shelves,
Should be located in the east or north direction (प्राक् उदीची/east or north direction).
It should be built high, in an auspicious spot, with many windows (वातायन/windows), and spacious.
The royal kitchen (महानस/kitchen) must be well cleaned and served by trustworthy staff.
Its entrance should be guarded by sentries, with proper ceiling and canopy.
It should have clean water pots, well-washed vessels, pure water, and clean fuel.
Cooks (सूद/cooks) working there should be skilled, prompt, vigilant, and honest.
They should have trimmed hair and nails, hereditary loyalty to king, and no external ties.
Their supervisor should be a noble brahmin, well-tested, disciplined, and clean.
He should be devoted, loyal, pure, and obedient to the royal physician.
All attendants staying near the king should always possess such noble virtues.
Free from mutual conflicts and petty factions, they must work solely for king's welfare.
The single virtuous physician should mentally watch over all of them vigilantly.
Constantly devoted to protecting the king's house, diet, and equipments.
When the king marches against enemies, the physician equipped with medicines, instruments, and surgical tools (भेषज शस्त्र यंत्र/drugs, tools, and instruments),
Should pitch his tent near the royal flag and treat wounded soldiers in war (युद्ध/battlefield).
Because enemies corrupt the paths, water, shade, food, fodder, and firewood (मार्ग उदक अन्न/paths, water, and food),
The physician should identify all poisoned items and purify them immediately.
Never permit marching or military camping without inspecting the route.
Soil, water, grass, timber, stones, routes, and roadside trees,
When contaminated with poison, look smeared and abnormal.
Grass, shrubs, and plants wither, and insects and reptiles die.
Animals walking on poisoned ground suffer hooves and nails falling off, burning, itching, and pain.
Troops suffer vomiting, fainting, fever, confusion, and severe headache.
For ground decontamination, drink drumstick root, somavalli, ushir, citrus juice, and asafoetida (हिंगु/asafoetida).
Administer the remedy in proper dose according to constitution.
Mix urine of goats, sheep, and elephants with animal blood and meat,
Combine with all aromatic spices (सर्वगंध/all aromatics), boil, and sprinkle over poisoned ground.
Mix babchi, sunanda, sarala, gandhanakuli, charati, and trayamana,
And sprinkle that liquid over the poisoned terrain to purify it.
Poisoned water becomes tasteless, lukewarm, marked with strange streaks,
Foamy, heavy, separated, avoided by birds, and full of dead fish.
Touching poisoned water causes pain, inflammation, vesicles, and severe itching.
Rice cooked in poisoned water causes burning sensation immediately upon eating.
It digests with great difficulty; if uncooked, it causes fainting and fever.
Poisoned food shows blue, yellow, red, and multi-colored patches.
In such cases, sprinkle and drink Shigru-agada (शिग्वादि अगद/drumstick antidote formulation) to cure ground poison.
Burn ajashringi, vishala, vishaghni, uttamaroni, phanijjaka, and ativisha into ash.
Filter the ash multiple times through cloth,
Boil the filtrate and add paste of sarala and turmeric,
Along with cardamom, ushir, manjishtha, sunanda, and babchi.
Pour drops of this liquid into water; wherever it touches, water becomes poison-free.
Burn patala, paribhadra, ashvakarna, shamyaka, and shigru inside a clay pot,
And throw the ash into poisoned water reservoirs.
In cold season, poisoned wind feels hot; in summer, it feels icy cold.
Poisoned air causes clouds, fainting, and respiratory distress.
In that condition, Shigru-agada antidote is recommended.
Powder devdaru, nata, anantamula, liquorice, antimony, red ochre (गैरिक/red ochre),
Vajrakanda, lata, and lodhra into fine dust.
Dust this powder on tree tops, royal flags, and high banners.
Upon contact with this fine powder, the air becomes completely purified and poison-free.
When a tree is poisoned, its shade turns abnormal and distorted.
Its flowers become odorless or foul-smelling, causing headache and cardiac distress.
Its fruits and leaves cause itching, skin suppuration, and severe diarrhea.
All remedies prescribed for soil poison are effective for treating poisoned trees.
Never touch or approach an unknown, unexamined maiden (कन्या/maiden).
For clever malicious people prepare various sinister poison combinations.
A poison maiden (विषकन्या/poison maiden) is fed minute poison doses from birth.
Her mere touch, breath, or sweat kills a person; her test is as follows:
By the touch of her sweat or breath, flower petals wilt immediately.
Bedbugs, lice, and water organisms die instantly in her bed or bathwater.
Knowing such signs, one should avoid a poison maiden from afar.
The king should never consume anything uninspected, unblessed by physician, or untested by cooks.
Food must first be tasted by royal cooks and tested before serving the king.
The auspicious eye collyrium named Sarvarthasiddhi (सर्वार्थसिद्धि अगद/all-accomplishing antidote salve) destroys sins, evil spirits, and poisons.
It is supreme for eyesight, longevity, destroying enemies, and granting royal victory.
During bright fortnight on auspicious nakshatra like Pushya or Punarvasu, gather pure blue antimony (अंजन/antimony collyrium) from Indus river bed.
Mix eight parts antimony with one part gold dust, silver, and copper in a crucible, offering prayers and firing with khadir wood.
Worship Avalokiteshvara, Tara, Brahma, Ashvins, Indra, Agni, Dhanvantari, and Sushruta with fried grain, jaggery, ghee, and milk rice; quench the heated collyrium separately in cow dung, urine, ghee, milk, curd, honey, fat, marrow, wine, and herbal decoctions.
Tie it in white silk cloth and suspend it in rainwater for twelve days.
Dry in shade, grind with pearls, crystals, and gold dust through a pure maiden, and store in a gold, silver, or gem collyrium pot.
Consecrate it with Savitri sacred mantras and Vedic rituals.
Carry it on an elephant back with white umbrella, fly-whisks, conch shells, and Vedic chants to the royal physician's home.
The physician, purified after worshipping cows and brahmins, should apply this divine eye salve using a gold rod while reciting the holy Dharani incantation.
Homage to the King of Eye Purification, the Tathagata, Arhat, Perfectly Awakened Buddha!
Namely (तद्यथा/namely):
Om! Purify my physical eye, wisdom eye, knowledge eye, and spiritual vision! Svaha!
Thereafter, remembering this sacred Dharani every morning and evening,
Apply this supreme, holy, health-giving collyrium originally created by Ashvin twins for Indra.
Therefore, a wise physician should prepare this for kings, ministers, and scholars.
Here are additional protective stanzas (भवंति चात्र/here are verses):
Now auspicious formulations declared by Lord Brihaspati (बृहस्पति/guru Brihaspati) will be stated,
By using which a king is never deceived or harmed by enemies.
Decoction of bael, mahua, barley ash, patala, and shalmali,
Is a supreme anti-poison sprinkling wash (प्रोक्षण/sprinkling wash).
Sprinkling poisoned items with it renders them instantly poison-free.
For fodder, fuel, water, clothes, beds, seats, cooked rice,
Armors, ornaments, umbrellas, fans, and royal residences.
Shleshmataka, patala, ativisha, drumstick, sariva, punarnava,
Samanga, poison root bark, kapittha, and bull's blood,
Mixed with pomegranate peel forms a supreme anti-poison wash.
Lac, priyangu, manjishtha, samanga, reuka, liquorice,
Ground with honey and brown mongoose bile (बभ्रु पित्त/mongoose bile),
Kept inside a cow horn buried underground for seven nights.
Made into a protective gem amulet encased in gold and worn on hand.
Whatever poisoned item touches this gem becomes immediately poison-free.
Realgar, orpiment, shami flowers, white mustard, kapittha, kushta, manjishtha,
Ground finely with bile of dog, cow, and brown cat.
This supreme antidote named Soumya (सौम्य अगद/soumya antidote) should be worn as a gem.
It acts as a supreme poison conqueror when tied on hand.
Or roots of musakaja tied on hand destroy all poisons.
Harenu, jatamansi, manjishtha, turmeric, liquorice, honey,
Terminalia bark, holy basil, lac, and dog bile made into a gem amulet.
Musical instruments and royal banners smeared with this paste,
Render anyone hearing, seeing, or smelling them instantly free from poison.
Trikatu, five salts (पंचलवण/five medicinal salts), manjishtha, two turmerics,
Cardamom, trivrit, tejpatta, vidanga, and indravaruni,
Mixed with liquorice and honey, kept inside a cow horn (गोविषाण/cow horn).
Taken in proper dose with warm water before meals,
Causes ingested poison to digest without causing any harm to the body.
Fumigation (धूप/incense fumigation) with lac, sal resin, ushir, mustard, cardamom, bhallataka, and arjuna flowers,
In the bedroom destroys all stationary and mobile poisons.
No venomous insects, rats, or reptiles can stay where this fumigation burns.
Nor can evil sorcery or witch spells affect that home.
Fumigation with peacock feathers, crane bones, mustard, sandalwood, and ghee,
Purifies beds, clothes, seats, and royal mansions from poison.
Vishala, trikatu, manjishtha, liquorice, five salts, turmerics, trivrit,
Mixed with honey and stored in cow horn, added to bath water.
Decoction of sun-flower, white apamarga, and mustard with curd and ghee,
Or paste of sandalwood, milk, and dhátaki bark,
Purifies all water sources and bath waters.
Applying facial tilak mark made of rochana, lotus, and saffron,
Protects against poison and makes one beloved to royal women.
Dusting powders of turmeric, manjishtha, and neem renders items poison-free, says Sage Gautama.
Administer life-restoring antidotes through nasal drops, drinks, collyrium, and pastes.
Use sharp emetics with long pepper, liquorice, honey, sugar, and sugarcane juice.
Perform purgation and bloodletting (विरेचन सिरामोक्ष/purgation and venesection) when indicated.
First protect the cardiac region (हृदयावरण/heart protection) before administering anti-poison cures.
Drink Ajeya Ghrita (अजेय घृत/unconquerable ghee antidote) on an empty stomach.
Ghee, honey, curd, milk, cold water, and meat soup are beneficial.
Meat soup of iguana, peacock, mongoose, and deer cooked with ginger and long pepper.
Served cool with ghee to a person who has consumed poison.
After proper emesis and purgation, administer fine copper dust (ताम्र रजः/copper powder) with honey to purify the heart.
Once the heart is purified, administer gold powder (हेम चूर्ण/gold dust/swarna bhasma).
Poison cannot cling to a person protected by gold, just as water drops do not stick to a lotus leaf.
He attains long life and vitality; this same procedure applies for artificial poisons (गरविष/artificial toxin).
Thus the physician must protect the king from poison and artificial toxins.
For the downfall of a king brings destruction to all subjects and their undertakings.
Command, courage, patience, and generosity in a king are superhuman divine qualities.
Therefore, the king should be served with deep devotion like a deity.
The physician should enter the royal chamber only with permission and recognition.
Even if favored, he should never occupy improper seats or vehicles.
He should stand humbly before the king and never interrupt his speech.
Speak timely, righteous words concerning the king's interest and public welfare.
Never advise the king unasked, for that is considered great rashness.
Never suggest unpalatable measures that cause radical harm.
Speak agreeable and beneficial words; if harmful, warn him in private.
Use gentle soothing words; if he overlooks faults, remain silent.
Avoid hostile debates or offensive conversations in royal presence.
A scholar who ignores the king's mood fails, while a wise attendant thrives.
Excessive intimacy or hostility with a king can quickly turn disastrous.
Inform the king before undertaking even small tasks.
Do not linger unnecessarily in the royal treasury or inner quarters.
Express contentment with even small gains, without arrogance.
Avoid secret whispering, gossiping, and taking sides in court rivalries.
Do not wear clothes or imitate lifestyle matching the king's royal status.
Wear only royal gifts with humble gratitude.
Smile only when appropriate, following the lord's mood.
Remain mute when secrets of others are discussed.
Show patience, composure, and sweetness regarding one's own vulnerabilities.
Do not elevate oneself excessively through extreme effort.
For a fall from height causes great sorrow, whereas high elevation alone gives no lasting joy.
Serving near a king is like playing with fire, weapons, or venomous snakes.
It can be sustained only through great skill, humbleness, and vigilance.
Having attained rare wealth and royal honor, stay vigilant to enjoy it long.
Arrange protective charms and sacred mustard around the king's bed.
Keep sacred ash, white mustard, and unhusked rice at night.
Place barley sprouts and protective charms near the headboard.
Keep a full water pot decorated with fresh flowers, fruits, and leaves.
Offer evening worship offerings after meals and at night.
Perform these auspicious rituals with purity and promptness.
This bestows long life, nourishment, and destroys evil spirits, poison, and sorcery.
Serving close to the king is like playing with weapons, venomous snakes, and fire (शस्त्र अहि पावक/weapon snake fire).
It is carried out safely only through supreme skill, humility, and constant vigilance (कौशल विनय/skill and humility).
Having attained hard-to-obtain sovereignty, wealth, and high honor from the king (ऐश्वर्य बहुमान/sovereignty and honor),
The physician should remain constantly vigilant (अप्रमादवान्/careful) so he may enjoy his position for a long time.
One should place around the king's bed protective items consecrated with protective chants (रक्षा मंत्र/protective chants).
At night, consecrated white mustard seeds (सिद्धार्थक/white mustard), sacred ash (भूति/sacred ash), and unbroken holy grains (अक्षत/unbroken rice) should be kept clean.
At the head of the bed (उच्छीर्ष/headrest), protective amulets, barley shoots (यवांकुर/barley sprouts), and lac dyes should be placed.
Pure Bdellium incense (गुग्गुलु/guggulu), a full auspicious water pitcher (पूर्णकलश/full pitcher) with flowers, fruits, and fresh leaves should be arranged.
Auspicious offerings (उपहार/propitiatory offerings) should be made at dusk, after meals, and during the night.
This auspicious protective ritual (स्वस्त्ययन कर्म/protective rite) must be performed promptly by a purified physician.
It promotes longevity (आयुष्य/longevity promoter), provides nourishment (पौष्टिक/nourishing), and overcomes evil spirits, poisons, sorcery, and sins (भूत विष पाप/demons toxins sins).
This brief outline for protection against poison (विषपालन/poison protection) is stated here; detailed procedures appear in Uttarasthana (उत्तरस्थान/supplementary section).
Examining all clinical signs of disorders carefully, a wise prudent physician should apply appropriate remedies.
