In this universe, every physical substance (द्रव्य/material substance) is composed of five basic elements (पंचमहाभूत/five subtle elements).
Its substratum is Earth element (पृथिवी/earth element), its origin is Water element (जल/water element), and Space, Air, and Fire elements cause distinct structural modifications.
Substances are named according to the predominance (उत्कर्ष/predominance) of particular mahabhuta elements contained within them.
Because of the combination of multiple elements (भूतसमवाय/elemental combination), no substance possesses only a single taste (एक रस/single taste diet).
Similarly, diseases are also never caused by a single humor (दोषज/humor-born disorder) alone.
The primary taste (व्यक्त रस/manifested taste) is directly perceivable upon tongue contact.
The secondary taste (अनुरस/secondary taste) is subtle or unmanifested initially, becoming perceivable only at the end of digestion or when uninhibited.
Tastes possess two main functional effects (कर्म/action): purificatory/scraping (छेदन/scraping/cutting) and pacifying/soothing (उपशमन/pacification), alongside beneficial or harmful influence.
Substances based on Earth and other elements manifest qualities like heavy (गुरु/heavy to digest) or light (लघु/light to digest) depending on origin, processing, region, and season.
Earth-predominant substances (पार्थिव द्रव्य/earthy substance) are heavy, hard, clear, slow, dense, gross, stable, and fragrant, producing body stability, weight, and compactness.
Water-predominant substances (जलीय/aquatic substance) are liquid, unctuous, cold, heavy, slow, smooth, viscous, promoting moisture, softness, and contentment.
Fire-predominant substances (आग्नेय/fiery substance) are sharp, hot, dry, subtle, light, clear, and radiant, causing burning heat, digestion, illumination, and color brilliance.
Air-predominant substances (वायव्य/aerial substance) are dry, subtle, light, clear, non-viscous, cold, and rough, causing dryness, lightness, fatigue, and bodily movement.
Space-predominant substances (आकाशीय/ethereal substance) are soft, subtle, light, clear, non-viscous, smooth, causing softness, porosity, and lightness in tissues.
Thus, there exists no substance in this entire universe (जगत्/world) that cannot serve as medicine (औषध/therapeutic agent) when used appropriately.
Substances rich in Fire and Air elements generally possess an upward-moving inclination (ऊर्ध्वभाग/upward direction - emetic).
Fire naturally ascends due to lightness, and Air moves upwards due to its floating nature (प्लवनत्व/floating buoyancy).
Substances rich in Earth and Water elements generally possess a downward-moving inclination (अधोभाग/downward direction - purgative) due to heaviness.
Substances containing mixed elemental compositions act in both upward and downward directions (उभयभागहर/dual evacuant).
Pacifying substances (शमन/pacification therapy) act by possessing qualities directly opposite to the aggravated humors (दोष/bodily humors).
In composite substances, nomenclature and properties are assigned based on the predominant element (बाहुल्य/abundance).
Substances predominant in Air element possess bowel-binding/absorbent qualities (ग्राही/bowel binder/astringent).
Substances predominant in Fire element kindle appetite (दीपन/appetite booster) and digest chyme (पाचन/digestive/chyme transformer).
Substances possessing both Fire and Air elements perform scraping/attenuating action (लेखन/tissue scraping).
Substances rich in Earth and Water elements perform body-nourishing and bulk-promoting action (बृंहण/nourishing effect).
Pungent (कटु/pungent taste), sour (अम्ल/sour taste), and salty (लवण/salty taste) tastes are progressively hotter in thermal potency (उष्ण वीर्य/heating potency).
Bitter (तिक्त/bitter taste), astringent (कषाय/astringent taste), and sweet (मधुर/sweet taste) tastes are progressively colder in thermal potency (शीत वीर्य/cooling potency).
Bitter, pungent, and astringent tastes are dry (रूक्ष/dry), binding feces, urine, and flatus.
Salty, sour, and sweet tastes are unctuous (स्निग्ध/unctuous), facilitating smooth passage of feces, urine, and flatus.
Salty, astringent, and sweet tastes are heavy for digestion (गुरु/heavy digestion).
Similarly, sour, pungent, and bitter tastes are light for digestion (लघु/easy to digest).
Some authorities consider salty taste as possessing moderate qualities regarding heavy, light, unctuous, and dry attributes.
Potency (वीर्य/potency) is classified into eight types by some seers: heavy, light, unctuous, dry, sharp, slow, cold, and hot.
Other Ayurvedic masters state the fundamental definition of potency in classical verses:
'Potency (वीर्य/potency) is that inherent power of a substance by virtue of which pharmacological action is executed.'
'No therapeutic action takes place without potency; all pharmacological actions are entirely driven by potency.'
Proponents of eightfold potency emphasize that among all attributes, these eight qualities possess supreme pharmacological strength.
They encompass and represent the ultimate potency dominating over tastes, post-digestive effects, and general attributes.
Hence, in clinical practice and pharmacological literature, substances are primarily described by these dominant potencies.
When a drug exhibits unique actions overriding all general taste and quality rules, that specific power is named (प्रभाव/specific action) (प्रभाव/specific drug action).
Even when therapeutic actions appear similar, distinct qualities are designated under specific potencies (वीर्य/potency) or unique drug actions (प्रभाव/specific action).
Other sages divide potency into two thermal types—hot (उष्ण/hot in potency) and cold (शीत/cold in potency)—reflecting Agni (fire) and Soma (water) cosmic divisions.
They state that despite diverse attributes, all substances are dominated by Fire (अग्नि/digestive fire) and Water (सोम/cooling lunar energy) cosmic forces:
'Though substances have diverse compositions, Fire and Water forces govern the entire universe, manifest or unmanifest.'
Nothing in this world ever transcends these two fundamental thermal poles of Fire (अग्नि/digestive fire) and Water (सोम/cooling soma principle).
Hot potency (उष्ण वीर्य/heating potency) produces burning, digestion, sweating, liquefaction, exudation, and pacifies kapha humor.
Cold potency (शीत वीर्य/cooling potency) provides refreshing joy, stanching/stiffening (स्तंभन/astringent/stopping), vitalizing, and pleases bleeding pitta.
Post-digestive effect (विपाक/post-digestive effect) generally turns sweet for sweet/salty tastes, sour for sour taste, and pungent for bitter/pungent/astringent tastes.
Post-digestive effect produces metabolic results similar to its corresponding tastes upon tissue assimilation.
One should evaluate the degree of post-digestive effect—mild, moderate, or intense—based on specific drug qualities.
Sage Parashara states his specific view on threefold post-digestive transformation:
'Transformation is threefold: sour taste becomes sour vipaka, pungent taste becomes pungent vipaka, and sweet taste becomes sweet vipaka.'
'Four other tastes transform accordingly, while mixed tastes yield combined post-digestive effects upon assimilation.'
If bitter, pungent, and astringent tastes all yield pungent vipaka, how could bitter and astringent tastes pacify Pitta humor?
Substances sweet in taste and vipaka with cold potency, sour substances with hot potency, and pungent substances with pungent vipaka follow standard rules.
Their effects on dosha aggravation or pacification can be inferred directly from their standard taste-potency-vipaka combination.
However, in substances with mixed attributes like milk, wine, or black pepper, actions alter due to specific combination factors.
For instance, honey (मधु/honey) is sweet yet pacifies Kapha because of pungent vipaka, astringent sub-taste, and dry nature; it increases Vata due to cold potency.
Similarly, barley (यव/barley grain) is sweet yet dry and pacifies Kapha while increasing Vata.
Marshy animal meat, though cold in touch, increases Pitta due to hot potency; sesame oil is pungent in vipaka, binding urine and feces.
Sour fermented gruel (कांजिक/sour fermented gruel) pacifies Kapha because of its dry and hot qualities.
Kapittha fruit pacifies Pitta because of its cold potency and dry nature.
Amalaki fruit pacifies Pitta humor because of cold potency and sweet post-digestive effect (मधुर विपाक/sweet post-digestive effect).
Sour pomegranate (दाडिम/pomegranate) pacifies Pitta because of mild unctuousness and non-heating nature.
Fish meat, though sweet in taste, aggravates Pitta humor because of hot thermal potency.
Ginger (शुंठी/dry ginger) pacifies Kapha and Vata because of hot potency, unctuousness, and heavy qualities.
Onion (पलांडु/onion) acts similarly, nourishing tissue strength.
Onion increases Kapha through unctuousness and heaviness, while tender radish is sweet in post-digestive effect.
Bitter herbs like Ativisha, Arka, and Aguru aggravate Pitta humor because of their hot thermal potency.
The astringent-bitter Brihat-panchamula pacifies Vata humor but does not pacify Pitta because of hot thermal potency.
Astringent horse gram (कुलत्थ/horsegram legume) aggravates Pitta because of its sour post-digestive effect (अम्ल विपाक/sour post-digestive effect).
These examples are stated merely as illustrations to guide clinical understanding.
Therefore, Ayurvedic doctrine summarizes drug actions:
'A substance acts by virtue of its taste (रस/nutrient plasma/taste), another by its potency (वीर्य/potency), another by its qualities (गुण/pharmacological quality), another by its post-digestive effect (विपाक/post-digestive effect), and another by its specific action (प्रभाव/specific action).'
'If one among taste, potency, quality, or vipaka is exceptionally strong, it overrides weaker attributes and executes its action.'
'When opposing qualities meet within a substance, the stronger quality inevitably overcomes the weaker quality.'
'Post-digestive effect (विपाक/post-digestive effect) overrides taste (रस/nutrient plasma/taste); thermal potency (वीर्य/potency) overrides both taste and vipaka; and specific action (प्रभाव/specific action) overrides all of them.'
This hierarchy of dominance represents the natural inherent strength (निसर्ग बल/natural inherent power) of pharmacological attributes.
Even when attributes are mutually opposing, they co-exist within a substance to achieve therapeutic results without destroying one another.
Taste, potency, and vipaka reside in a drug depending on the predominance or weakness of elemental constituents (भूत/primordial element).
Attributes like sweetness, coldness, viscosity, unctuousness, heaviness, and slowness co-exist harmoniously in cow milk.
The same qualities mentioned for substances also apply to bodily humors (दोष/bodily humors) during their increase, decrease, or normal equilibrium.
Taste (रस/nutrient plasma/taste) is perceived upon tongue contact; potency (वीर्य/potency) is perceived during digestion and assimilation; and vipaka is known by ultimate metabolic action.
In sweet substances like ghee, oil, and jaggery, distinct sub-attributes differentiate their therapeutic actions despite sweet primary taste.
Minute variations in elemental composition produce distinct therapeutic results without altering the primary taste classification.
Though primary tastes are classified into six distinct types, minor elemental nuances create subtle pharmacological variations.
Just as general qualities do not exceed their natural class, tastes remain strictly categorized within the primary sixfold division.
Coating of mouth and sweet taste are perceived in ghee and oils, unlike sour pomegranates.
Powerful qualities among heavy and light attributes are designated as Potency (वीर्य/potency), while weaker attributes remain general qualities (गुण/pharmacological quality).
Some scholars claim vipaka follows taste strictly into six types, but clinical evidence proves otherwise.
For instance, sweet paddy rice becomes sour upon digestion; non-sour pomegranate does not turn sour; oil turns pungent; and pungent Pippali turns sweet in vipaka.
If post-digestive effect strictly matched taste, such metabolic inversions would never occur in nature.
Barley (यव/barley grain), though sweet and heavy, increases Vata humor; ghee is appetite-kindling and cold; animal fat (वसा/muscle fat) is hot yet dampens digestive fire.
Green gram (मुद्ग/green gram legume), though pungent in vipaka, pacifies Pitta; black gram (माष/black gram legume) increases Pitta; jaggery syrup (फाणित/half-boiled molasses), though sweet in vipaka, increases Vata.
Such metabolic variations are observed across numerous medicinal and dietary substances.
Sour Amalaki fruit pacifies Kapha and Pitta while increasing Vata; curds (दधि/curd/yogurt) are heavy and kindle digestive fire without worsening Pitta when properly taken.
Kapittha and sour pomegranate are absorbent (ग्राही/bowel binder/astringent), whereas sour Amalaki is mild laxative (स्रंसन/mild laxative).
Astringent Dhataki flower is absorbent and cold, whereas astringent Haritaki is laxative and wholesome.
Hence, taste, potency, and vipaka act under the supreme umbrella of the physical substance (द्रव्य/substance/drug) itself.
Because specific action (प्रभाव/specific action) resides in the substance, the substance (द्रव्य/substance/drug) is considered supreme in Ayurvedic pharmacology.
When two drugs have identical taste, potency, and vipaka, yet perform different actions, that distinct action is born of Prabhava (प्रभाव/specific action).
For example, Danti and Chitraka are identical in taste, potency, and vipaka, yet Danti acts as a purgative while Chitraka does not.
Similarly, raisins (मृद्वीका/dried raisins) match licorice (मधुक/licorice root) in attributes, yet raisins act as laxative; ghee kindles digestive fire unlike milk.
Garlic (लशुन/garlic), despite pungent taste, hot potency, and unctuous heavy qualities, pacifies Vata and Kapha due to its unique Prabhava.
Red rice (रक्त शालि/red rice paddy) pacifies all three humors despite opposing qualities due to its specific action (प्रभाव/specific action).
If post-digestive effect (विपाक/post-digestive effect) always matched primary taste (रस/nutrient plasma/taste), such contradictions would never occur.
For instance, barley (यव/barley) is sweet and heavy, yet it increases vata humor (अनिलप्रद/vata increaser).
Ghee (आज्य/ghee) is cooling yet kindles digestive fire (दीपन/appetite booster), whereas muscle fat (वसा/muscle fat) is heating yet dampens fire.
Moong dal (मुद्ग/green gram) has pungent vipaka yet pacifies pitta, whereas black gram (माष/urad dal) increases pitta (पित्तल/pitta increaser).
Molasses (फाणित/treacle) has sweet vipaka, unctuousness, heat, and heaviness, yet it increases vata (चलकृत्/vata aggravator).
Just as this occurs with sweet taste, similar contradictions are seen in drugs of other tastes.
Amalaki fruit (आमलकी/indian gooseberry) is sour in taste, yet it pacifies kapha and pitta without increasing vata.
Curd (दधि/yogurt) is heavy and kindles digestive fire (वन्हि/digestive fire), but does not promote strength (बल/strength) in the same way.
Kapittha and Dadima (दाडिम/pomegranate) are sour and bowel-binding (ग्राहि/astringent binder), unlike Amalaki.
Dhataki flower (धातकी/dhataki) is astringent, cooling, and bowel-binding (ग्राहि/binder), unlike Haritaki which is purgative.
Therefore, individual attributes like taste (रस/taste) are secondary when considering the whole drug (द्रव्य/substance).
Since specific pharmacological action (प्रभाव/specific action) resides in the whole substance, the substance (द्रव्य/drug) is considered supreme.
When two drugs have identical taste, potency, and vipaka, any unique specific action produced is due to Prabhava (प्रभाव/specific potency).
For example, Danti (दंती/wild castor) is identical in taste and potency to Chitraka, yet Danti acts as a purgative (विरेचनी/purgative).
Similarly, raisins (मृद्वीका/raisins) excel over licorice (मधुक/licorice), and ghee (घृत/ghee) kindles digestion unlike milk (क्षीर/milk).
Garlic (लशुन/garlic) pacifies kapha and vata through its pungent taste, vipaka, unctuousness, and heaviness.
Garlic pacifies vata and kapha through specific inherent qualities (प्रभाव/inherent specific potency) rather than standard individual attributes alone.
That red rice (रक्तशालि/red rice) overcomes mutually opposing vata, pitta, and kapha humors is due to its specific action (प्रभाव/specific action).
And that inferior grains (यवक/yavaka grain) aggravate humors is likewise a manifestation of specific action (प्रभाव/specific action).
That Shirisha (शिरीष/dita bark tree) acts as a supreme antidote for poisons (विषघ्न/poison destroyer) is due to its innate specific potency (प्रभाव/specific action).
The diverse actions produced by sacred gems (मनि/gems), mantras (मंत्र/incantations), and divine herbs (ओषधि/divine herbs),
Such as extracting foreign bodies (शल्य/foreign bodies), repelling evil spirits, protecting life, and enhancing intellect,
And how potent antidotes (अगद/antidote formulation) neutralize poisons merely by sight, touch, or wearing,
Or how certain heating substances induce instant purgation or rapid semen generation,
Or how upward-purging emetics and downward-purging purgative drugs act,
When administered in proper dosage (मात्रा/dose) and conditions as elaborately described in classical texts,
All these unique actions are born of Prabhava (प्रभाव/specific potency); hence Prabhava is declared inconceivable (अचिंत्य/beyond logic).
Whatever normal action a drug performs through taste, potency, qualities, and post-digestive effect (विपाक/post-digestive effect),
Can be instantly transformed or overridden by Prabhava (प्रभाव/specific potency); hence it is beyond empirical reasoning (अचिंत्य/inconceivable).
Thus ends the seventeenth chapter (सप्तदशः अध्यायः/seventeenth chapter) named "Principles of Substances, Qualities, and Tastes" (द्रव्यादिविज्ञानीयः/knowledge of dravya and guna) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the classification and mechanics of tastes (रसभेद/analysis of tastes) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Taste (रस/taste) is fundamentally unmanifest in pure water (आप्य/water-based) before elemental combination.
Due to six seasons (षट् ऋतु/six seasons) and varying elemental proportions (महाभूत/primordial elements), water transforms into six distinct tastes (रस/tastes).
Sweet taste (मधुर रस/sweet taste) originates from the predominance of Earth (पृथ्वी/earth element) and Water (जल/water element).
Sour taste (अम्ल रस/sour taste) originates from the predominance of Earth (पृथ्वी/earth element) and Fire (तेजस्/fire element).
Salty taste (लवण रस/salty taste) originates from the predominance of Water (जल/water element) and Fire (तेजस्/fire element).
Bitter taste (तिक्त रस/bitter taste) originates from the predominance of Air (वायु/wind element) and Space (आकाश/ether element).
Pungent taste (कटु रस/pungent taste) originates from the predominance of Air (वायु/wind element) and Fire (तेजस्/fire element).
Astringent taste (कषाय रस/astringent taste) originates from the predominance of Air (वायु/wind element) and Earth (पृथ्वी/earth element).
Sweet taste (मधुर/sweet taste) coats the mouth, pleases senses, gladdens the body, and is loved by bees and ants (षट्पद पिपीलिका/bees and ants).
Sour taste (अम्ल/sour taste) makes the tongue tremble, burns throat and chest, waters mouth, shrinks eyes, and makes body hair stand on end (रोमाञ्च/goosebumps).
Salty taste (लवण/salty taste) causes salivation in mouth, burns throat and cheeks, and enhances relish for food (अन्न रोचन/appetite relish).
Bitter taste (तिक्त/bitter taste) cleanses the mouth, purifies throat, and temporarily suppresses taste perception (रसन/taste sense).
Pungent taste (कटु/pungent taste) agitates tongue tip with tingling (चिमिचिमायन/tingling sensation), waters cheeks, and burns mouth, eyes, nose, and body.
Astringent taste (कषाय/astringent taste) stiffens the tongue, chokes throat, and causes distress in the heart region (हृदय/heart region).
Sweet taste (मधुर/sweet taste) nourishes all body tissues (धातु/body tissue), promotes longevity (आयुष्य/longevity promoter), benefits voice, skin, hair, and pacifies Vata, Pitta, burning heat, and poisons.
When consumed in excess (अतिसेवन/excessive consumption), sweet taste causes obesity (स्थौल्या/obesity), sluggish digestion (अग्निसौद/low appetite), heaviness, indigestion (अलसक/intestinal stagnation), and excessive sleep.
It also leads to dyspnea (श्वास/asthma), diabetes (प्रमेह/diabetes), goiter (गंडमाला/cervical adenitis), tumors (अर्बुद/tumors), and loss of consciousness.
It produces vomiting (छर्दि/emesis), allergic skin rashes (उदर्द/urticaria), headache, cough (कास/cough), rhinitis (पीनस/catarrh), and intestinal worms (कृमि/parasites).
It brings on elephantiasis (श्लीपद/filaria), fevers (ज्वर/fever), enlarged abdomen (उदर/ascites), and excessive spitting.
Sour taste (अम्ल/sour taste) pacifies Vata wind, aids downward movement (अनुलोमन/downward movement), kindles digestion (दीपनपाचन/appetizer and digestive), is hot in potency, unctuous, and light.
Excessive sour intake causes body tissue flaccidity (शिथिलता/looseness), Kapha liquefaction, itching (कंडू/itching), anemia (पांडुता/pale anemia), and acute pain.
It provokes erysipelas (विसर्प/erysipelas), bleeding disorders (रक्तपित्त/bleeding disease), excessive thirst, swelling (श्वयथु/edema), and vomiting due to Fire dominance.
Salty taste (लवण/salty taste) breaks stiffness and impaction, kindles appetite (दीपन/kindling digestion), cleanses channels (स्रोतः/bodily channels), is unctuous, heating, sharp, and pacifies Vata.
Overuse of salt causes baldness (खलति/hair loss), graying hair (पालित/premature graying), thirst, burning heat, fainting, erysipelas, skin lesions (किटिभ/psoriasis), and gout.
It worsens bleeding disorders (रक्तपित्त/hemorrhage), destroys physical strength (बल/stamina), and depletes vital essence (ओजः/vital essence).
Bitter taste (तिक्त/bitter taste) cures anorexia, poisons, worms, fever, burning, thirst, skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin diseases/leprosy), dries moisture and fat; is light, cold, and digestive.
Excessive bitter intake causes severe loss of tissue strength (धातुबल क्षय/tissue loss), fainting, dizziness (भ्रम/vertigo), and dryness.
It produces roughness (रौक्ष्य/roughness), harshness, and stiff exhaustion across all body systems.
Pungent taste (कटु/pungent taste) cures indigestion (अलसक/intestinal stagnation), swelling, obesity, worms, mouth diseases, skin disorders; dries unctuousness; is light, dry, sharp, and heating.
Excessive pungent intake produces severe thirst (तृष्णा/thirst), intoxication (मद/dizziness), fainting, body exhaustion, and collapse.
It causes tremors (कंप/tremors), vertigo (भ्रम/dizziness), burning exhaustion, and severe emaciation (अतिकर्शन/extreme wasting).
It violently provokes Vata wind in hands, feet, sides, back, and joints with pricking pains.
It induces sharp cutting pains (तोद भेद/cutting agony) due to excessive Fire and Air element dominance.
Astringent taste (कषाय/astringent taste) pacifies Kapha, Pitta, and blood; constipates stools, is dry, heavy, cold, and promotes wound healing (रोपण/wound healing).
It dries body moisture, restores skin color, and acts as a tissue scraper (लेखन/scraping/emaciating).
Excessive astringent intake causes semen obstruction (शुक्ररोध/sperm retention), thirst, abdominal distension (आध्मान/flatulence bloating), stiffness, and severe emaciation.
It obstructs body channels (स्रोतोबंध/channel obstruction), causes retention of urine and feces, paralysis (पक्षाघात/hemiplegia), and convulsions (आक्षेपक/convulsions).
Sweet drug group (मधुर स्कंध/sweet taste group): Ghee, honey, oil, milk, fat, marrow, sugarcane, raisins, walnut, dates, jackfruit, Shatavari, Bala, Ashwagandha, Gokshura, lotus seeds, Trina-panchamula.
Sour drug group (अम्ल स्कंध/sour taste group): Pomegranate, Amalaki, mango, Matulunga, Tamarind, Karamarda, curds (दधि/curd/yogurt), whey (मस्तु/curd whey), buttermilk (तक्र/spiced buttermilk), Dhanyamla, and vinegars (शुक्त/fermented acidic wine).
Salty drug group (लवण स्कंध/salty taste group): Rock salt (सैंधव/rock salt), sea salt, black salt, alkaline salts (क्षार/alkaline ash), and lead/tin ashes.
Bitter drug group (तिक्त स्कंध/bitter taste group): Aguru, Usheera, Sandalwood, Nimba, Turmeric, Guduchi, Katuki, Kiratatikta, Vacha, Patola, and leafy bitter vegetables.
Pungent drug group (कटु स्कंध/pungent taste group): Black pepper (मरिच/black pepper), Hingu, Vidanga, Garlic (लशुन/garlic), Onion (पलांडु/onion), Mustard (सर्षप/mustard seed), and Panchakola herbs.
Astringent drug group (कषाय स्कंध/astringent taste group): Haritaki, Priyangu, Lodhra, Dhataki, Arjun bark, Jamun, Mango seed, Khadira, Lotus, and Red Ochre (गैरिक/red ochre).
As a rule, sweet foods aggravate Kapha EXCEPT old rice (पुराण शलि/aged old rice), barley (यव/barley grain), wheat (गोधूम/wheat grain), green gram (मुद्ग/green gram legume), honey (मधु/honey), and wild meat.
As a rule, sour foods aggravate Pitta EXCEPT pomegranate (दाडिम/pomegranate) and Amalaki fruit (आमलकी/emblic myrobalan).
As a rule, salty foods harm eyesight (अचक्षुष्य/harmful to vision) EXCEPT Saindhava rock salt (सैंधव/rock salt).
As a rule, bitter and pungent foods aggravate Vata EXCEPT Guduchi, Patola, fresh ginger (शुंठी/dry ginger), Pippali, and Garlic (लशुन/garlic).
As a rule, astringent foods are cold and constipating EXCEPT Haritaki fruit (हरीतकी/chebulic myrobalan).
Thus, the primary representative drugs for daily wholesome usage across all six tastes are summarized.
Ghee (Sweet), Amalaki (Sour), Saindhava (Salty), Patola (Bitter), Pippali (Pungent), and Haritaki (Astringent) are supreme in their respective taste groups.
These six prime drugs represent the highest efficacy in their taste classes; next, land habitats (देश/habitat/region) of tastes are described.
Marshy land (आनूप देश/marshy region) with abundant water, lotus ponds, lush grass, thick vegetation, heavy animals, and Kapha-Pitta climate is the natural habitat/origin of Sweet taste (मधुर रस/sweet taste).
Arid land (जांगल देश/arid region) with dry sand, deep water, sparse vegetation, healthy long-lived inhabitants, and Vata climate is the natural habitat/origin of Pungent taste (कटु रस/pungent taste).
Moderate lands combining marshy and arid qualities (आनूप जांगल साधारण/marshy arid mixed land) produce mixed taste combinations.
Marshy-moderate land gives rise to Salty and Sour tastes, while arid-moderate land gives rise to Bitter and Astringent tastes.
The total mathematical combinations of six tastes across various proportional mixtures equal fifty-seven (सप्तपंचाशत्/57 combinations).
Explanatory verses on the permutation breakdown of taste combinations follow.
Combining two tastes yields 15 distinct pairs (5 sweet combinations, 4 sour, 3 salty, 2 bitter, 1 pungent).
These fifteen dual-taste combinations (द्विक संयोग/dual taste combination) provide specific therapeutic actions.
Combining three tastes yields 20 distinct triplets (10 sweet triplets, 6 sour, 3 salty, 1 bitter).
These twenty triple-taste combinations (त्रिक संयोग/triple taste combination) serve complex humoral imbalances (त्रिदोष/three bodily humors).
Combining four tastes yields 15 distinct quadruplets.
Combining five tastes yields 6 distinct quintuplets (each omitting 1 taste).
Four-taste combinations (चतुष्क संयोग/quadruple taste combination) total 15 distinct variations.
Ten of these quadruplet taste variations include sweet taste as a primary constituent.
Four-taste and five-taste combinations allow precise drug tailoring for multi-humoral diseases.
Omitting one taste at a time from all six gives 6 quintuple taste combinations (पंचरस/five-taste combination).
Including all six tastes together yields 1 sixfold combination (षड्रस/six tastes), making 63 overall mathematical permutations (त्रिषष्टि भेद/63-fold classification).
Considering primary tastes (रस/nutrient plasma/taste), secondary aftertastes (अनुरस/secondary taste), and varying degrees of strength (तारतम्य/gradation/relative degree), taste combinations become infinite.
These infinite taste variations are skillfully applied by wise physicians according to disease state and drug properties.
Thus ends the eighteenth chapter (अष्टादशः अध्यायः/eighteenth chapter) named 'Classification and Mechanics of Tastes' (रसभेदीयः/mechanics of tastes) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the knowledge of bodily humors, tissues, and wastes (दोषादिविज्ञानीयः/knowledge of doshas and dhatus) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
The human body (देह/body) is rooted in humors (दोष/doshas), tissues (धातु/dhatus), and waste products (मल/waste materials).
Vata humor (वायु/wind humor) sustains the body through respiration (उच्छ्वास निश्वास/inhalation exhalation), enthusiasm (उत्साह/energy), motor activity (प्रस्पंद/movement), sensory acuity (इंद्रिय पाटव/sharp senses), and urging natural discharges (वेग प्रवर्तन/urges urge).
Pitta humor (पित्त/bile humor) sustains the body through digestion (पक्ति/digestion), metabolic heat (ऊष्मा/body heat), appetite (क्षुत्/hunger), thirst (पिपासा/thirst), complexion brilliance (प्रभा/radiance), vision (दर्शन/eyesight), intellect (मेधा/intelligence), courage (शौर्य/valour), and softness (मार्दव/softness).
Kapha humor (श्लेष्मा/phlegm humor) sustains the body through stability (स्थैर्य/steadiness), unctuousness (स्नेह/lubrication), joint binding (संधि बंध/joint stability), virility (वृषता/potency), forbearance (क्षमा/patience), intellect (धी/wisdom), strength (बल/stamina), and greedlessness (अलौल्य/contentment).
Plasma nutrient fluid (रस/plasma tissue) sustains the body through satisfaction (तुष्टि/contentment), nourishment (प्रीणन/refreshing), and feeding blood tissue (रक्त पुष्टि/blood nourishment).
Blood tissue (असरृक्/blood tissue) sustains the body through vitalizing (जीवन/life giving), complexion clarification (वर्ण प्रसाद/skin radiance), and nourishing muscle tissue (मांस पोषण/muscle growth).
Muscle tissue (मांस/muscle tissue) sustains the body through plastering muscles (देह लेप/body plastering), waste secretion, and nourishing fat tissue (मेदः पुष्टि/fat growth).
Fat tissue (मेदः/adipose tissue) sustains the body through lubricating eyes and limbs (नेत्र गात्र स्नेह/eye limb lubrication), firmness of fat, and nourishing bone tissue (अस्थि पुष्टि/bone growth).
Bone tissue (अस्थि/bone tissue) sustains the body through supporting upright posture (देह धारण/body support) and nourishing bone marrow tissue (मज्ज पोषण/marrow growth).
Marrow tissue (मज्जा/marrow tissue) sustains the body through lubrication (स्नेह/unctuousness), strength (बल/stamina), filling bone cavities (अस्थि पूरण/bone cavity filling), and nourishing reproductive tissue (शुक्र पुष्टि/semen growth).
Reproductive tissue (शुक्र/semen tissue) sustains the body through exhilaration (हर्ष/pleasure), physical strength (बल/vital vigor), and embryo production (गर्भ उत्पादन/procreation).
Feces waste (शकृत्/fecal waste) sustains the body through supporting gut stability (अवष्टंभ/intestinal support) and maintaining abdominal air and fire (अनिल अनल धारण/vata fire support).
Urine waste (मूत्र/urinary waste) sustains the body through carrying out water moisture (अम्बु क्लेद निर्वाहण/fluid excretion).
Sweat waste (स्वेद/perspiration waste) sustains the body through holding skin moisture (क्लेद/moisture), skin softness (त्वक् स्नेह/skin unctuousness), and hair health (रोम धारण/body hair support).
Aggravated Vata humor (वृद्ध वायु/increased vata) afflicts the body with emaciation (कार्श्य/lean body), dark discoloration (कार्ष्ण्य/blackish skin), muscle tremors (गात्र कंप/body shaking), twitching (स्फुरण/muscle twitching), craving warmth (उष्ण कामिता/heat desire), loss of consciousness (संज्ञा नाश/fainting), insomnia (निद्रा नाश/sleeplessness), loss of strength and senses (बल इंद्रिय उपघात/sensory weakness), bone pain (अस्थि शूल/bone aching), marrow dryness (मज्जा शोष/marrow drying), constipation (मल संग/stool retention), bloating (आध्मान/flatulence), rumbling (आटोप/intestinal sound), confusion (मोह/delusion), helplessness (दैन्य/misery), fear (भय/anxiety), grief (शोक/sorrow), and delirium (प्रलाप/irrational speech).
Aggravated Pitta humor (वृद्ध पित्त/increased pitta) afflicts the body with yellow skin (पीत त्वक्/yellowish skin), exhaustion (ग्लानि/faintness), sensory weakness (इंद्रिय दौर्बल्य/sensory fatigue), loss of vital essence (ओजो विस्रंस/ojas slipping), desire for coldness (शीत अभिलाष/cold longing), burning sensation (दाह/burning heat), bitter mouth taste (तिक्तास्यता/bitter mouth), excessive thirst (तृष्णा/thirst), fainting (मूर्च्छा/dizziness), reduced sleep (अल्प निद्रता/poor sleep), and anger (क्रोध/irritability).
Aggravated Kapha humor (वृद्ध श्लेष्मा/increased kapha) afflicts the body with coldness (शैत्य/chills), paleness (शौत्य/whitish skin), obesity (स्थौम्य/weight gain), lethargy (आलस्य/sluggishness), heaviness (गौरव/heaviness), limb weakness (अंग साद/body fatigue), channel blockage (स्रोतो पिधान/channel obstruction), fainting (मूर्च्छा/dizziness), sleepiness (निद्रा/excessive sleep), drowsiness (तंद्रा/stupor), dyspnea (श्वास/breathlessness), cough (कास/coughing), salivation (प्रसेक/drooling), nausea (हृल्लास/nausea), loss of digestive fire (अग्नि साद/indigestion), and joint looseness (संधि विश्लेष/loose joints).
Aggravated Rasa plasma (वृद्ध रस/increased plasma) causes excessive salivation (प्रसेक/drooling), anorexia (अरोचक/loss of appetite), perverted taste (आस्य वैरस्य/foul taste), nausea (हृल्लास/nausea), channel blockage (स्रोतो रोध/channel congestion), aversion to sweet taste (स्वादु द्वेष/sweet dislike), bodyache (अंगमर्द/body pain), and other Kapha disorders.
Aggravated Rakta blood (वृद्ध रक्त/increased blood) causes skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin disorders), erysipelas (विसर्प/red swelling), pustules (पिटिका/skin boils), menorrhagia (असृग्दर/heavy bleeding), eye diseases (अक्षि रोग/eye inflammation), mouth and anal suppuration (मुख गुद पाक/ulcerations), enlarged spleen (प्लीहा/spleen enlargement), abdominal tumor (गुल्म/phantom tumor), abscess (विद्रधि/abscess), hyperpigmentation (व्यंग/freckles), jaundice (कामला/jaundice), loss of digestion (अग्नि नाश/lost appetite), unconsciousness (तमः प्रवेश/blackouts), red skin and eyes (रक्तांग नेत्रि/red eyes), gouty arthritis (वातरक्त/gouty arthritis), and other Pitta disorders.
Aggravated Mamsa muscle (वृद्ध मांस/increased muscle) causes goiter (गलगंड/goiter), cervical lymphadenitis (गंडमाला/scrofula), tumors (अर्बुद/fibroids), cysts (ग्रंथि/nodules), palatal and throat disorders (तालु कंठ रोग/throat ailments), heaviness in hips, cheeks, lips, arms, abdomen, thighs, and calves (गौरव वृद्धि/muscle swelling heaviness), and other Kapha-Rakta disorders.
Aggravated Medas fat (वृद्ध मेदः/increased fat) produces pre-monitory signs of diabetes (प्रमेह पूर्वरूप/pre-diabetes symptoms), obesity complications (स्थौल्य उपद्रव/obesity ailments), and other Kapha-Rakta-Mamsa disorders.
Aggravated Asthi bone (वृद्ध अस्थि/increased bone) causes extra bone growth (अध्यस्थि/bony exostosis) and extra teeth (अधिदंत/extra teeth).
Aggravated Majja marrow (वृद्ध मज्जा/increased marrow) causes heaviness in eyes and body (नेत्र अंग गौरव/eye body heaviness), red eyes, and thick-rooted painful ulcers in joint spaces (पर्व ग्रंथि रुक्/painful joint swellings).
Aggravated Shukra semen (वृद्ध शुक्र/increased semen) causes excessive sexual desire (अतिस्त्री कामता/hypersexuality) and seminal calculus (शुक्राश्मरी/semen stones).
Aggravated Purisha feces (वृद्ध शकृत्/increased feces) causes abdominal distension (कुक्षि आधिक्य/enlarged belly), colic pain (शूल/abdominal pain), intestinal rumbling (आटोप/rumbling sound), and heaviness (गौरव/gut heaviness).
Aggravated Mutra urine (वृद्ध मूत्र/increased urine) causes bladder distension (बस्ति आधिक्य/bladder swelling), pricking bladder pain (तोद/stabbing pain), and urinary retention fullness (आध्मान/bladder fullness).
Aggravated Sveda sweat (वृद्ध स्वेद/increased sweat) causes excessive sweating itching (कंडू/skin itch) and foul body odor (दौर्गंध्य/foul smell).
Other increased bodily excretions (मल/wastes) like eye discharge and ear wax cause heaviness (गौरव/heaviness), fluidity (द्रवता/fluidness), and localized itching (कंडू/itching).
Decreased Vata humor (क्षीण वायु/reduced vata) afflicts the body with excessive salivation (प्रसेक/drooling), anorexia (अरुचि/loss of appetite), nausea (हृल्लास/nausea), loss of consciousness (संज्ञा मोह/mental confusion), sparse speech and movements (अल्प वाक् चेष्टा/sluggish activity), loss of joy (प्रहर्ष नाश/lack of joy), limb weakness (अंग साद/body fatigue), and erratic digestion (अग्नि वैषम्य/irregular digestion).
Decreased Pitta humor (क्षीण पित्त/reduced pitta) causes stiffness (स्तंभ/rigidity), coldness (शैत्य/chills), irregular pricking pain (अनियत तोद/variable pain), lack of burning sensation, loss of appetite (अरोचक/indigestion), rough skin (अंग पारुष्य/skin roughness), tremors (कंप/shaking), and whitish nails and eyes (नख नयन शौक्ल्य/pale eyes nails).
Decreased Kapha humor (क्षीण श्लेष्मा/reduced kapha) causes dizziness (भ्रम/vertigo), limb twisting (उद्वेष्टन/cramps), sleeplessness (अनिद्रा/insomnia), bodyache (अंगमर्द/body pain), organ dryness (परिशोष/internal dryness), stabbing pain (तोद/pricking pain), internal burning sensation (दाह/inner heat), cracking joints (संधि स्फोटन/joint popping), tremors (वेपन/shaking), sensation of fumes (धूमायन/smoky feeling), joint looseness (संधि शैथिल्य/lax joints), palpitation (हृदय द्रव/racing heart), and feeling emptiness in Kapha seats (श्लेष्माशय शून्यता/empty chest).
Decreased Rasa plasma (क्षीण रस/reduced plasma) causes intolerance to loud noise (शब्द असहत्व/noise sensitivity), heart palpitations (हृदय द्रव/fluttering heart), tremors (कंप/shaking), dryness (शोष/dry mouth), chest pain (शूल/cardiac pain), sensation of emptiness (शून्यता/emptiness), twitches (स्पंदन/muscle spasms), and exhaustion with thirst upon minor exertion (श्रम तृषा/fatigue thirst).
Decreased Rakta blood (क्षीण रक्त/reduced blood) causes skin roughness (त्वक् रौक्ष्य/dry skin), longing for sour and cold substances (अम्ल शीत अभिलाष/sour cold desire), and vein looseness (शिरा शैथिल्य/collapsed veins).
Decreased Mamsa muscle (क्षीण मांस/reduced muscle) causes wasting of hips, cheeks, and lips (स्फिक् गंड शुष्कता/emaciated cheeks hips), muscle pricking pain (तोद/muscle pain), dry skin (रौक्ष्य/skin dryness), eye fatigue (अक्षि ग्लानि/eye weakness), cracking joints (संधि स्फोटन/cracking joints), and arterial looseness (धमनी शैथिल्य/flaccid arteries).
Decreased Medas fat (क्षीण मेदः/reduced fat) causes spleen enlargement (प्लीहा वृद्धि/enlarged spleen), waist numbness (कटी स्वाप/lower back numbness), feeling empty joints (संधि शून्यता/empty joints), skin dryness (अंग रूक्षता/dry limbs), emaciation (कार्श्य/thinness), fatigue (श्रम/exhaustion), dryness (शोष/body dryness), and intense craving for fatty meat (मेदुर मांस अभिलाष/fatty meat craving).
Decreased Asthi bone (क्षीण अस्थि/reduced bone) causes falling teeth, nails, and body hair (दंत नख रोम शातन/hair nail loss), bone dryness (अस्थि रौक्ष्य/bone dryness), joint looseness (संधि शैथिल्य/lax joints), bone pricking pain (अस्थि तोद/bone pain), and craving for meat attached to bone (अस्थि बद्ध मांस अभिलाष/bone meat craving).
Decreased Majja marrow (क्षीण मज्जा/reduced marrow) causes hollow porous bones (अस्थि सौषिर्य/hollow bones), stabbing bone pain (निस्तोद/deep pain), physical weakness (दौर्बल्य/weakness), dizziness (भ्रम/vertigo), and dark spots before eyes (तमः दर्शन/blacking out).
Decreased Shukra semen (क्षीण शुक्र/reduced semen) causes exhaustion (श्रम/fatigue), weakness (दौर्बल्य/debility), dry mouth (आस्य शोष/dry mouth), blurred vision (तिमिर दर्शन/blurry eyes), pale skin (अंग पांडुता/paleness), weakness (साद/fatigue), impotency (क्लैब्य/erectile dysfunction), testicle pain (मुष्क तोद/testicular pain), smoky feeling, delayed ejaculation (चिरात् सेक/delayed discharge), or ejaculation mixed with blood (सरक्त सेक/bloody semen).
Decreased Purisha feces (क्षीण शकृत्/reduced feces) causes rumbling gas moving sideways and upwards in belly (वायु भ्रमण/trapped gas), intestinal twisting (आंत्र वेष्टन/bowel cramps), flank and heart pain (पार्श्व हृदय पीडा/flank pain), and producing very little stool.
Decreased Mutra urine (क्षीण मूत्र/reduced urine) causes pricking bladder pain (बस्ति निस्तोद/bladder pain), dry mouth (मुख शोष/dry mouth), painful scanty discolored urination (कृच्छ्र अल्प मूत्र/painful scanty urine), or passing bloody urine (सरुधिर मूत्र/hematuria).
Decreased Sveda sweat (क्षीण स्वेद/reduced sweat) causes hair erection stiffness (स्तब्ध रोमता/hair stiffness), hair falling out (रोम च्यवन/hair loss), skin cracking (त्वक् परिपाटन/skin peeling), loss of tactile sensation (स्वाप/skin numbness), skin roughness (पारुष्य/rough skin), and total absence of perspiration (स्वेद नाश/lack of sweat).
Decreased minor excretions (क्षीण मल/reduced minor wastes) cause emptiness (शून्यता/emptiness), lightness (लाघव/lightness), dryness (शोष/dryness), and pricking pain in their respective excretory passages (मलायन/excretory channels).
Increase and decrease of humors, tissues, and wastes (वृद्धिक्षय/increase and decrease) should be recognized by the appearance of opposite qualities and clinical symptoms.
Increase and decrease of bodily wastes (मल/waste products) are recognized by excessive retention (संघ/aggregation) or excessive discharge (उत्सर्ग/excretion).
Depletion of wastes in an increased state afflicts the patient severely due to unaccustomed bodily imbalance (अनौचित्य/unaccustomed habit).
Vata humor resides mainly in bone tissue (अस्थि/bone tissue); Pitta resides in blood (रक्त/blood tissue) and sweat (स्वेद/sweat waste); Kapha resides in all remaining tissues and wastes.
Therefore, therapies that increase or decrease a tissue also similarly affect its residing humor, except for bone (अस्थि/bone tissue) and Vata (वायु/wind humor/air) which have inverse relationship.
Almost all bodily tissue increases (वृद्धि/increase/aggravation) are caused by over-nourishment (अतिसंतर्पण/over-nourishment) and are accompanied by Kapha humor.
Conversely, all tissue depletions (क्षय/depletion/wasting) are caused by under-nourishment (अपतर्पण/depleting therapy) and are driven by Vata humor.
Therefore, disorders caused by tissue increase or decrease should be treated using depleting (लंघन/fasting/depletion therapy) or nourishing (बृंहण/nourishing body builder) therapies respectively.
Vata disorders specifically require balanced combination of nourishing (बृंहण/nourishing body builder) and depleting (लंघन/fasting/depletion therapy) measures.
Everywhere, treatment must utilize substances, qualities, and actions opposite to the cause (विपरीत/opposite) or similar in property without conflicting.
Bodily tissues (धातु/body tissues) grow when a person habitually consumes foods and activities possessing identical qualities (समान गुण/similar quality).
Tissues undergo depletion (क्षय/depletion/wasting) when habits possess opposite qualities (विपरीत गुण/opposite quality).
For example, heavy heavy tissues in the body are nourished by heavy foods (गुरु आहार/heavy food) through structural identity.
Light tissues are built by light foods (लघु आहार/light food).
Tissues are depleted by foods possessing opposite disintegrating qualities (विपरीत गुण/opposite quality).
Thus, blood tissue (रक्त/blood tissue) is directly replenished by consuming blood; muscle tissue (मांस/muscle tissue/flesh) by meat; fat tissue (मेदः/fat tissue) by fat; marrow (मज्जा/marrow tissue) by marrow; semen (शुक्र/semen/reproductive tissue) by semen; and fetus (गर्भ/fetus) by unformed fetal egg.
When identical tissues or foods are unavailable, or impossible to consume due to disgust, rules, or unwholesomeness,
One should consume foods and adopt lifestyle habits that possess a predominance of similar qualities (समानगुणभूयिष्ठ/predominantly similar quality).
For instance:
In depletion of semen (शुक्र क्षय/semen loss), one should consume milk (क्षीर/milk), ghee (घृत/cow ghee), and other sweet, unctuous, cold substances.
Physical activities that perform actions identical to a tissue's natural function should be practiced by one desiring its increase.
Similarly, activities opposite in action should be practiced to reduce an excessively increased tissue (धातु/body tissue).
Furthermore:
Disorders born of increased blood (रक्त वृद्धि/increased blood tissue) should be treated with bloodletting (रक्तमोक्षण/bloodletting procedure), blood-cleansing herbs (प्रसादन/purifier/clarifier), and purgation (विरेचन/purgation therapy).
Disorders born of increased muscle (मांस वृद्धि/increased muscle tissue) should be treated with purificatory evacuations (संशोधन/systemic purification), surgery (शस्त्र/surgical instruments), alkali (क्षार/alkaline ash), and cautery (अग्निकर्म/thermal cautery).
Disorders born of increased fat (मेदः वृद्धि/increased fat tissue) should be treated following obesity-reduction management (स्थौल्य चिकित्सा/obesity treatment).
Disorders born of depleted plasma (रस क्षय/nutrient plasma loss) should be treated with meat soups (मांस रस/meat soup), wine (मद्य/alcoholic wine), and milk (क्षीर/milk).
Disorders born of depleted bone (अस्थि क्षय/bone tissue loss) should be treated with medicated enemas (बस्ति/medicated enema) and milk-ghee prepared with bitter herbs (तिक्त द्रव्य/bitter herbs).
Disorders born of increased feces (शकृत् वृद्धि/feces accumulation) should be treated using anti-diarrheal remedies (अतिसार चिकित्सा/diarrhea treatment).
Disorders born of depleted feces (शकृत् क्षय/feces reduction) should be treated with barley (यव/barley grain), black gram (माष/black gram legume), unhusked grain soup, goat meat, sheep meat, and fermented drinks.
Disorders born of increased or decreased urine (मूत्र वृद्धिक्षय/urine volume changes) should be treated following diabetes (मेह/urinary disorder/diabetes) and dysuria (मूत्रकृच्छ्र/painful urination/dysuria) therapies.
Disorders born of depleted sweat (स्वेद क्षय/sweat reduction) should be treated with oil massage (अभ्यंग/oil massage), physical exercise (व्यायाम/physical exercise), wine, sleep, warm shelter, and sudation (स्वेदन/fomentation therapy).
Regarding this, the following verses are stated:
Due to sluggishness (मंदाग्नि/weak digestive fire) or hyperintensity (तीक्ष्णाग्नि/sharp hyperactive digestion) of the tissue-digesting fires (धात्वाग्नि/tissue fire), tissues undergo increase (वृद्धि/increase/aggravation) or decrease (क्षय/depletion/wasting).
Listen further to this mechanism:
Just as a forest wildfire (दावाग्नि/forest fire) grows or diminishes sequentially depending on the fuel it reaches,
In the same manner, the successive chain of bodily tissues (धातु परंपरा/sequential tissue chain) undergoes increase or depletion.
A substance that is similar in properties increases its corresponding tissue, while a dissimilar substance causes its reduction.
Because from its own metabolic seed (धातु बीज/tissue seed factor), a corresponding tissue element is quickly produced.
An increased preceding tissue increases the succeeding tissue, while a depleted preceding tissue causes similar depletion in the next.
Vitiated humors (दोष/bodily humors) pollute tissues (धातु/body tissues) through metabolic channels, and both together pollute waste products (मल/waste products).
Two lower orifices, seven head orifices, and sweat-carrying pores (स्वेदवह स्रोतः/sweat-carrying channels) are the waste channels (मलायन/excretory channels).
Waste passages (मलायन/excretory channels) harbor corresponding wastes; diseases originating in them will now be described:
Vata-born diseases in these channels will be stated in the chapter on Vata diagnosis (वातव्याधि निदान/vata disorder etiology).
Pitta humor localized in skin (त्वच्/skin) causes pustular eruptions (विस्फोट/pustular eruption) and smallpox-like lesions (मसूरिका/smallpox-like pocks).
Pitta localized in blood (रक्त/blood tissue) causes erysipelas (विसर्प/erysipelas/herpes) and severe burning heat (दाह/burning sensation); in muscle (मांस/muscle tissue/flesh), it causes tissue sloughing (मांसकोथन/muscle sloughing).
Pitta localized in fat (मेदः/fat tissue) causes burning glandular swellings (ग्रंथि/nodular swelling), excessive sweating (स्वेद/sweat waste), thirst (तृष्णा/excessive thirst), and severe dizziness (भ्रम/dizziness/giddiness).
Pitta localized in bone (अस्थि/bone tissue) causes intense internal bone burning; in marrow (मज्जा/marrow tissue), it turns nails and eyes deep yellow (हरिद्रा नख नेत्र/turmeric yellow nails eyes).
Pitta localized in reproductive tissue (शुक्र/semen/reproductive tissue) causes foul-smelling yellow-colored semen (पूति पीत शुक्र/foul yellow semen).
Pitta in blood vessels (शिरा/blood vessels) causes anger, feverish heat (ताप/febrile heat), and delirium (प्रलाप/delirium/irrational speech); in ligaments (स्नायु/ligaments/tendons), it causes unquenchable thirst (तृषा/intense thirst).
Pitta in gastrointestinal tract (कोष्ठ/gastrointestinal tract) causes intoxication (मद/intoxication), thirst, burning heat, and spreading systemic wasting diseases (यक्ष्मा/tuberculosis/wasting).
Kapha humor localized in skin (त्वच्/skin) causes rigidity (स्तंभ/stiffness/rigidity) and whitish skin discoloration (श्वेतावभास/pale whitish luster).
Kapha localized in blood (रक्त/blood tissue) causes anemia (पांडुरोग/anemic disease); in muscle (मांस/muscle tissue/flesh), it causes tumors (अर्बुद/tumor/mass) and cervical lymphadenitis (अपची/scrofula/cervical adenitis).
It creates a feeling as if the limbs are wrapped in wet leather (आर्द्र चर्म अवनद्ध/wet-hide binding feeling) along with profound heaviness (अति गौरव/excessive heaviness).
Kapha localized in fat (मेदः/fat tissue) causes gross obesity (स्थौल्य/obesity) and diabetes (मेह/urinary disorder/diabetes); in bones (अस्थि/bone tissue), it causes bone stiffness (स्तब्धत्व/rigidity).
Kapha in marrow (मज्जा/marrow tissue) causes pale white eyes (शुक्ल नेत्र/whitish pale eyes); in semen (शुक्र/semen/reproductive tissue), it causes excessive seminal stagnation (शुक्र संचय/semen accumulation).
Kapha in blood vessels (शिरा/blood vessels) causes channel obstruction (विबंध/constipation), heaviness, and limb rigidity (स्तब्धगात्रता/body stiffness).
Kapha in ligaments (स्नायु/ligaments/tendons) causes joint emptiness (संधि शून्यता/feeling empty joints); in gut (कोष्ठ/gastrointestinal tract), it causes abdominal enlargement (जठर उन्नति/abdominal enlargement),
Anorexia (अरोचक/anorexia/loss of appetite), indigestion (अविपाक/indigestion), and various other Kapha-born disorders.
Disorders of feces and urine localized in their respective seats will be explained in their respective diagnostic chapters.
Distress and functional damage occur when wastes accumulate in their respective sense organs.
Due to physical overexertion (व्यायाम/physical exercise), hyperintense digestive heat (ऊष्मा तैक्ष्ण्य/sharp heat), and unwholesome habits (अहित आचरण/unwholesome conduct),
Vitiated humors (दोष/bodily humors) travel from gut (कोष्ठ/gastrointestinal tract) to peripheral tissues (शाखा/peripheral tissues), bones, and vital spots (मर्म/vital anatomical spot) due to the swift mobile nature of Vata wind.
Conversely, humors move back from tissues to gut through channel clearing (स्रोतोमुख विशोधन/channel mouth cleansing),
Driven by humor increase (वृद्धि/increase/aggravation), fluid exudation (अभिष्यंदन/channel clogging), digestive cooking (पाक/metabolic transformation), or by controlling Vata wind (वायु निग्रह/controlling vata).
Residing in those temporary seats, humors linger waiting for secondary exciting causes (हेतु प्रतीक्षा/awaiting etiology).
Gaining seasonal or environmental strength (काल बल/seasonal strength), they become aggravated even in foreign tissue seats (अन्याश्रय/foreign site).
When humors reside in foreign tissue seats, if the host tissue is weak, one should treat the localized humor (स्थानी दोष/native resident humor).
If the invading humor is very powerful and overpowers the host seat, treat the invading humor (आगंतु दोष/foreign invading humor) primarily.
One should pacify the secondary invading humor (आगंतु/external cause) or treat the native host humor (स्थानी/native resident humor) based on relative strength.
The ultimate essence (तेजः/radiance/fire element) of all bodily tissues is called Ojas (ओजः/vital essence).
Ojas is gentle (मृदु/soft/mild), watery in nature (सोमात्मक/cooling lunar nature), pure, clear, reddish-yellow in color (रक्त ईषत् पीतक/reddish yellow-tinged).
It is the primary essence formed at the time of embryonic conception (गर्भसार/embryonic essence), derived from embryonic nutrient fluid.
As the embryonic heart (हृदय/heart) forms in the beginning, Ojas takes up its permanent abode there.
It constitutes the unctuous essence (स्नेह/unctuous oil/fat) of all body fluids, wherein fundamental life principles (प्राण/vital breath/life energy) are rooted.
Without Ojas, life cannot exist; nourished by Ojas, all living beings stay refreshed and energized.
Although residing primarily in the heart (हृदय/heart), Ojas pervades the entire body and is the supreme seat of life (जीवित पद/abode of life).
Ojas undergoes depletion (ओजोक्षय/vital essence depletion) due to anger (क्रोध/anger), hunger (क्षुधा/hunger), worry (ध्यान/anxiety/worry), grief (शोक/grief/sorrow), and physical overexertion (श्रम/fatigue/physical exertion).
In Ojas depletion, the person feels constant fear (बिभेति/fears/anxious), debility (दुर्बल/weak), worries continuously, and suffers sensory affliction (व्यथितेन्द्रिय/distressed senses).
The person exhibits poor skin luster (दुश्छाया/poor complexion), mental gloom (दुर्मनः/dejected mind), dry skin (रूक्ष/dry/rough quality), and emaciation (क्षमा/patience/forgiveness).
The treatment for Ojas depletion includes vitalizing herbs (जीवनीय औषध/vitalizing herbs), milk (क्षीर/milk), and nourishing meat soups (मांस रस/meat soup).
When Ojas increases (ओजोवृद्धि/increased vital essence), the body experiences contentment (तुष्टि/contentment), nourishment (पुष्टि/nourishment/corpulence), and enhanced physical strength (बल/physical strength).
Whatever unwholesome food a person feels aversion to, and whatever wholesome food they crave,
By respecting those natural cravings without violating health, one overcomes tissue increase (वृद्धि/increase/aggravation) or decrease (क्षय/depletion/wasting).
Agravated or depleted humors (दोष/bodily humors) create natural desires for foods that possess opposite or similar qualities.
Both increased and depleted humors indicate their state through cravings, though foolish people fail to recognize them.
According to their strength, increased humors produce their full characteristic symptoms (रूपाणि/clinical manifestations).
Decreased humors abandon their normal functions, while balanced humors (सम/balanced state) perform their natural physiological duties.
The very same humors (दोष/bodily humors) that promote growth and vitality when balanced, cause destruction when imbalanced.
Therefore, through wholesome diet and conduct (हित चर्या/wholesome conduct), one must protect humors from undergo increase or depletion.
Thus ends the nineteenth chapter (एकोनविंशः अध्यायः/nineteenth chapter) named 'Knowledge of Bodily Humors, Tissues, and Wastes' (दोषादिविज्ञानीयः/knowledge of doshas and dhatus) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the differential classification of bodily humors (दोषभेद/subtypes of doshas) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Vata humor is composed of Space (आकाश/ether space element) and Air (वायु/air element/wind humor) elements (वायु आकाश/air and space elements).
Pitta humor is composed primarily of Fire (अग्नि/digestive fire) element (तेजः/fire element).
Kapha humor is composed of Water (जल/water element) and Earth (पृथिवी/earth element) elements (जल भूमि/water and earth elements).
All bodily organs and limbs of living beings are nourished by these humors (Vata, Pitta, Kapha) just as plants are nourished by water and nutrients.
Although present throughout the entire body, these humors reside in specific primary locations.
Specially divided into five subtypes each, residing below heart, between heart and navel, and above heart, they sustain the body through mutual cooperation.
The Vata humor is categorized into five distinct subtypes (पंच भेद/five subtypes): Prana, Udana, Samana, Vyana, and Apana.
Prana Vayu (प्राण वायु/vital air) resides in head, chest, throat, nose, tongue, mouth, and navel.
It governs spitting, sneezing, eructation, respiration, and swallowing food (आहार प्रवेश/food intake).
Udana Vayu (उदान वायु/upward air) resides in chest, throat, and nasal passages.
It governs speech production (वाक् प्रवृत्ति/speech), effort, physical energy, strength, complexion, and memory retention (स्मृति/memory).
Samana Vayu (समान वायु/digestive air) resides in GI tract near digestive fire and tissue channels.
It governs receiving food, aiding digestion, separating nutrients from waste, and moving waste downward.
Vyana Vayu (व्यान वायु/pervading air) moves throughout the entire body and governs all physical movements.
It governs walking, extension, flexion, jumping, circulation of nutrient plasma (रससंवहन/plasma circulation), and sweat/blood excretion.
Apana Vayu (अपान वायु/downward air) resides in large intestine, rectum, bladder, genitals, testes, navel, and thighs.
It governs the downward expulsion of semen (शुक्र/semen/reproductive tissue), menstrual fluid (आर्तव/menstrual blood), urine (मूत्र/urine waste), feces (शकृत्/feces waste), and fetus (गर्भ/fetus).
When these five Vata subtypes function normally in their respective locations, they sustain the physical body intact.
Each subtype performs its specific physiological duties to maintain structural and functional health.
When vitiated, these five subtypes abandon normal duties and cause various localized diseases destroying bodily strength.
The Pitta humor is categorized into five distinct subtypes (पंच भेद/five subtypes): Pachaka, Bhrajaka, Ranjaka, Sadhaka, and Tarpaka.
Pachaka Pitta (पाचक पित्त/digestive pitta) resides between stomach and intestines (आमाशय पक्वाशय/stomach and intestines).
It digests food, separates nutrients from feces and urine, kindles internal fire, and assists other Pitta subtypes.
Bhrajaka Pitta (भ्राजक पित्त/skin pitta) resides in the skin layer (त्वचा/skin).
It illuminates skin glow, processes warm poultices, unguents, massages, and absorbs skin applications.
Ranjaka Pitta (रंजक पित्त/coloring pitta) resides in liver (यकृत्/liver organ) and spleen (प्लीहा/spleen organ).
It imparts red color to clear nutrient plasma (रस/nutrient plasma/taste), transforming it into blood tissue (रक्त/blood tissue).
Sadhaka Pitta (साधक पित्त/intellectual pitta) resides in the heart (हृदय/heart).
It governs intellect (मेधा/retentive intellect), courage (शौर्य/valor/courage), wisdom (धी/intellect), and fulfillment of cherished desires.
Tarpaka Pitta / Alochaka Pitta (तर्पक/आलोचक पित्त/ocular pitta) resides in eyes.
It enables visual perception and nourishes ocular structures.
The Kapha humor is categorized into five distinct subtypes (पंच भेद/five subtypes): Kledaka, Avalambaka, Bodhaka, Tarpaka, and Shleshaka.
Kledaka Kapha (क्लेदक कफ/moistening kapha) resides in the stomach (आमाशय/stomach).
It moistens dry food mass and nourishes remaining Kapha subtypes throughout the body.
Avalambaka Kapha (अवलंबक कफ/supporting kapha) resides in the chest (उरस्/chest region).
It supports shoulder/trika region, heart, and aids other Kapha sites via food essence.
Bodhaka Kapha (बोधक कफ/taste kapha) resides in tongue and throat.
It enables perception of six tastes (रसज्ञान/taste perception).
Tarpaka Kapha (तर्पक कफ/nourishing kapha) resides in the head/brain (शिरस्/head region).
It nourishes sensory organs including eyes, ears, and brain tissue.
Shleshaka Kapha (श्लेषक कफ/lubricating kapha) resides in all bony joints (संधि/bone joints).
It lubricates articular joint surfaces for smooth painless movement.
Now general symptoms of vitiated humors and specific disease signs are described separately.
Each humor produces distinct clinical features when aggravated in the body.
Vitiated Vata produces prolapse, dislocation, expansion, obstruction, splitting pain, malaise, horripilation, tremors, rigidity, contractions, and dark discoloration.
Vitiated Pitta produces burning sensation, heat, loss of appetite, excessive sweating, foul odor, intoxication, vomiting, jaundice, and yellow skin discoloration.
Vitiated Kapha produces unctuousness, malaise, heaviness, softness, drowsiness, stupor, excessive sleep, laziness, stiffness, itching, coldness, and white discoloration.
A wise physician should recognize diseases by observing these distinct clinical signs of vitiated humors.
Due to digestive fire impairment, undigested food turns sour in the stomach (अम्लीभवति/turns sour).
This sour chyme provokes Pitta humor excessively.
It causes internal burning, throat pain, thirst, and fainting.
It does not yield to cold sour therapies, but responds to warm therapies.
It yields only to warm measures that clear stomach dampness.
Hence warm sour measures are recommended in Pitta fever conditions.
Kapha and other humors divide into 72, 63, and 94 subtypes based on permutation of elements and sites.
Namely as follows:
Vata humor has 63 specific subtype variations (त्रिषष्टि भेद/63-fold classification).
Pitta humor has 72 specific subtype variations (द्विसप्तति भेद/72-fold classification).
Kapha humor has 94 specific subtype variations (चतुर्नवति भेद/94-fold classification).
Primary disease counts by predominant humor:
There are 80 specific disease manifestations caused primarily by Vata humor (वातज विकार/vata disorders).
There are 40 specific disease manifestations caused primarily by Pitta humor (पित्तज विकार/pitta disorders).
There are 20 specific disease manifestations caused primarily by Kapha humor (कफज विकार/kapha disorders).
These specific diseases will be detailed by name and signs in the Nidana section.
Vata diseases include tremors, paralysis (पक्षाघात/hemiplegia/stroke), facial palsy (अर्धित/facial paralysis), and tetanus.
Pitta diseases include burning fevers and inflammatory conditions.
Kapha diseases include itching (कंडू/itching), heaviness (गौरव/body heaviness), and lethargy (आलस्य/lethargy).
When an aggravated humor invades another humor's seat, it produces symptoms characteristic of itself in that foreign site.
Vata invading Pitta site produces burning sensation and heat.
Vata invading Kapha site produces itching and heaviness.
Pitta invading Vata site produces colic pain and distension.
Pitta invading Kapha site produces coldness and stiffness.
Kapha invading Vata site produces colic and obstruction.
Kapha invading Pitta site produces burning and heat.
Physician should examine local humor (स्थानी/native resident humor) versus invading humor (आगंतु/external cause) and treat according to strength.
Furthermore:
Vata is most powerful and afflicts even strong local humors.
Hence, in all cases, kindle digestive fire (अग्निसंधुक्षण/kindling digestive fire) and treat the stronger humor first.
In dual-humor aggravation (संसर्ग/dual humor combination), treat the stronger humor first.
If dual humors are equal, treat Vata first due to its swift action (आशुकारित्व/rapid-acting nature).
In triple-humor aggravation (सन्निपात/triple humor combination), treat the most aggravated humor first.
If all three humors are equal in sannipata, treat Pitta first due to rapid burning and thirst complications.
Then treat Kapha, and finally Vata.
If Pitta is combined with Vata, treat Pitta first due to burning heat.
Next treat Kapha.
Then treat Vata.
If Vata is combined with Pitta, treat Vata first due to severe pain.
Next treat Kapha.
Then treat Pitta.
If Vata is combined with Kapha, treat Vata first due to colic.
Next treat Pitta.
Then treat Kapha.
Similarly in other dual and triple combinations, plan treatment according to relative strength.
Furthermore:
Aggravated humors transform each other during stages of disease.
Namely as follows:
Vata, Pitta, and Kapha.
Aggravated Vata provokes Pitta.
Pitta in turn provokes Kapha.
Hence Vata fever exhibits Pitta fever symptoms.
Pitta fever exhibits Kapha fever symptoms.
Kapha fever exhibits Vata fever symptoms.
Physician should carefully analyze dual/triple humor interactions and administer appropriate remedies.
Here are concluding summary verses:
Vata is fivefold based on site and function: Prana, Udana, Samana, Vyana, and Apana.
Prana, Udana, Samana, Vyana, and Apana constitute five Vata subtypes.
Pachaka, Bhrajaka, Ranjaka, Sadhaka, and Tarpaka constitute five Pitta subtypes.
These five Pitta subtypes perform distinct physiological roles.
Kledaka, Avalambaka, Bodhaka, Tarpaka, and Shleshaka constitute five Kapha subtypes.
These five Kapha subtypes are established in their respective sites.
Residing in their sites, normal or vitiated, they govern bodily functions.
Each subtype is named according to its distinct function.
Prana and other Vatas residing in blood and tissues perform duties matching their names.
Residing in primary sites or invading foreign sites, they produce specific clinical effects.
Invading foreign sites, vitiated humors manifest individual pathological features.
When humors occupy foreign seats, physician should treat according to strength.
Vata is most powerful and afflicts even local humors.
Hence kindle digestive fire (अग्निसंधुक्षण/kindling digestive fire) and treat the stronger humor first.
This principle applies broadly across clinical practice.
Evaluate strength of humors, patient stamina, and disease stage.
In dual humor aggravation, treat the stronger humor first.
If equal, treat Vata first due to its swift spread.
A wise physician treats after thorough diagnostic examination.
Humors increase, decrease, or remain balanced in complex permutations.
In normal or abnormal states, they dictate health or disease.
Whatever disease form arises from these subtype variations,
The wise physician should analyze carefully and select correct remedies.
These subtype classifications are stated concisely for wise physicians.
Detailed descriptions are given in the Nidana section.
Humoral subtypes and disease forms are diverse,
Yet treatment in brief focuses on pacifying Vata, Pitta, and Kapha.
Here the remedies for humors are indicated concisely;
Their practical application will be seen in diagnosis and therapy.
Thus ends the twentieth chapter (विंशः अध्यायः/twentieth chapter) named 'Differential Classification of Bodily Humors' (दोषभेदीयः/subtypes of doshas) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the treatment principles of bodily humors (दोषोपक्रमणीय/management of humors) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Vata treatment involves oleation (स्नेहन/unctuation), sudation (स्वेदन/fomentation), mild purgatives with sweet, sour, and salty tastes, oil massage (अभ्यंग/massage), warm poultices (उपनाह/poultice), hot baths (परिषेक/pouring bath), comforting immersion (अवगाह/immersion bath), wines (सुरासव/fermented wines), 100 or 1000 times processed medicated oils (शतपाक सहस्रपाक तैल/processed oil), decoction enemas (बस्ति/enema therapy), unctuous enemas (अनुवासन/oil enema), meat broth (मांसरस/meat soup), avoiding sexual overindulgence, and winter routine (हेमन्त विधि/winter regimen).
Pitta treatment involves drinking ghee (सर्पिःपान/ghee drinking), oleation with ghee, downward purgation (विरेचन/purgative therapy), sweet, bitter, and astringent foods and medicines, sweet fragrant cool scents, immersion in cool fragrant waters, wearing pearls, gems, lapis lazuli, rubies, moonstones, garlands of lotus, water lilies, jasmine, and wearing them on the chest.
Frequent sprinkling of room walls, doors, windows, and balconies with cool fragrant water mixed with sandalwood (चंदन/sandalwood), Priyangu, Kaliyaka, lotus stems, camphor (कर्पूर/camphor), lotus, and water lilies.
Listening to pleasing, gentle, sweet music (गीत वादित्र/vocal instrumental music) and auspicious recitations.
Sitting with unpretentious, affectionate friends (सुहृद्/friends) dressed in pleasant attire during festive gatherings.
Affectionate gentle hugging of beloved children (अपत्यक/children) who speak sweet, innocent, tender words with sweet fragrant breath.
Enjoying the company of charming, graceful women (अंगना/women) clad in thin soft fragrant garments, adorned with pearl ornaments, possessing sweet gentle speech, captivating body features, and sweet scent, who bring complete delight to the heart and body.
Cool open terraces and water-splashed sandy courtyards bathed in soft moonlight (रजनीकर कर/moonbeams).
Fountain houses (धारागृह/shower house) with white sandy floors and multiple mechanisms flowing with crystal-clear cool water streams.
Cool shaded bowers near ocean-like lakes surrounded by flowering trees, decorated with white, red, and blue lotus pollen, scented with hanging water pitchers, blossoming shoots, sandalwood spray, providing relief from heat, thirst, and fatigue like the cool Himalayas.
Spacious pleasure lakes (दीर्घिका/lakes) resonant with sweet chirping of aquatic birds and adorned with red, white, and blue lotuses covered in fragrant pollen.
Shaded gardens (उपवन/gardens) with soft gentle breezes carrying fallen flower petals moved by swarms of humming bees.
Especially ghee (घृत/clarified butter), milk (पयः/milk), purgative drugs (विरेचन/purgatives), all cooling gentle things, day sleep (दिवास्वाप/day sleep), and summer regimen (ग्रीष्म विधि/summer routine).
Kapha treatment involves sharp purificatory evacuations (तीक्ष्ण शोधन/sharp purges), dry rough diet, foods combined with pungent, bitter, and astringent tastes, and strong aged heart-pleasing wines (मद्य/wine).
Running, fast walking, leaping, swimming, night vigil (जागरण/keeping awake), wrestling, sexual activity, physical exercise (व्यायाम/exercise), and dry body rubbing (रूक्ष उन्मर्दन/dry massage).
Especially honey (क्षौद्र/honey), herbal soups (यूष/soup broth), emetic therapies (वमन/vomiting therapy), total fasting (उपवास/fasting), medicated smoking (धूम/herbal smoke), gargling (गंडूष/gargling), avoiding idle comfort, and spring regimen (वसन्त विधि/spring routine).
Regarding this, the following summary verses (भवति चात्र/summary shlokas) are stated:
The specific therapeutic treatments outlined for individual humors should be adapted appropriately in dual (संसर्ग/combined humors) and triple (सन्निपात/triple humors) humoral aggravations.
Summer regimen is generally suited for Vata-Pitta; spring regimen for Kapha-Vata.
Because Vata is companion-conforming (योगवाहि/catalytic); whereas autumn regimen is suited for Kapha-Pitta.
Six tastes beginning with sweet, sour, and salty should be used in order when Vata is aggravated.
In Pitta aggravation, bitter taste first, followed by sweet, and astringent taste is beneficial.
In Kapha disease, pungent taste first, followed by bitter, and astringent taste at the end.
Accumulation, outburst, and pacification of Vata occur in summer, monsoon, and autumn seasons respectively.
For Pitta, accumulation occurs in monsoon, outburst in autumn, and pacification in early winter.
For Kapha, accumulation occurs in late winter, outburst in spring, and pacification in summer.
Vata accumulates due to light dry herbs in summer, but does not explode due to environmental heat.
Pitta accumulates in monsoon due to sour-digesting water and plants, but explodes in autumn due to heat.
Kapha accumulates in late winter due to unctuous cold water and plants, but explodes in spring when melted by sun rays.
Such is the natural seasonal cycle; however, dietary and lifestyle habits can trigger immediate accumulation or outburst out of season.
Aggravated humor rapidly pervades the entire body from head to toe like a sudden poison.
Whereas pacified waste subsides gradually step-by-step like receding flood waters.
One should subdue a humor in its accumulation stage (चय/accumulation stage) itself before it explodes into full disease.
When all humors explode simultaneously, treat the strongest humor first without provoking the others.
If all three humors are equal in strength, treat Vata first, then Pitta, then Kapha.
Because Vata possesses natural inherent superiority (स्वाभाविक बल/natural strength) over other humors.
Sage Parashara also declared the exact same principle confirming Vata's primary importance.
Because the physician should first conquer the leading humor, just as defeating the army commander collapses the entire army.
Some authorities desire destroying Kapha first based on its anatomical location in chest and head.
They argue that when head, chest, and throat are coated with Kapha, how can food or medicine be swallowed?
Without food intake, how can humors be controlled? Hence Kapha is destroyed first like unbolting a door bolt.
Others consider Pitta first because it is located centrally and acts with rapid urgency.
Therefore, treating Pitta first, then Vata, then Kapha is advocated by other classical masters.
Sushruta does not hold this rule universally for all diseases.
In fever (ज्वर/fever) and diarrhea (अतिसार/diarrhea), he prescribes treating Pitta first, then Kapha, then Vata.
Because fever is dominated by heat and fire element (तेजः/fire element).
And in diarrhea, fluid loss and loose bowels arise from Pitta's liquid sharp nature.
Secondary Kapha causes heaviness and sluggish digestion, while Vata inevitably increases later.
Therefore, in fever and diarrhea, this specific treatment order (Pitta → Kapha → Vata) is followed.
Others prescribe Kapha → Pitta → Vata in these two conditions due to stomach irritation.
Diagnosing the relative strength of humors through their specific clinical signs,
The physician should apply exact appropriate remedies rather than following a rigid mechanical order.
A treatment that cures one disease but excites another disease is not a pure treatment.
A pure treatment (शुद्ध उपचार/pure therapy) is one that pacifies the disease without provoking any other humor or disease.
An aggravated waste humor cannot be overcome if treated with insufficient dosage.
Nor do opposing qualities like coldness, liquidity, sourness, or saltiness cancel each other out when humors are balanced.
Just as the three cosmic attributes (सत्त्व रजः तमः/sattva rajas tamas) coexist to create the universe despite mutual opposition,
So also the three humors coexist in health and combine to produce disease.
Ama Vata presents with abdominal distension, gurgling sound, and erratic movement.
Ama Pitta presents with foul odor, dark discoloration, pungent taste, heaviness, and burning.
Ama Kapha presents with turbid sticky thread-like mucus, thick coating, and slimy feel.
In reverse, mature Pakva states present with proper digestion, copper color, clear yellow Pitta, and clear non-sticky Kapha.
Pakva Kapha is clear, white, sweet in taste, and non-slimy.
When digestive fire is weak, the first tissue element (plasma/rasa) remains raw and undigested.
This vitiated raw chyme residing in stomach is called Ama (आम/undigested chyme).
Others state that Ama forms from severe mutual corruption of vitiated humors, just as toxic liquor forms from fermenting grains.
One should never forcibly extract raw Ama humors pervading all bodily tissues.
Just as extracting juice from unripe raw fruit destroys the fruit itself, forcibly eliminating raw Ama destroys the body.
First mature raw humors with carminatives (पाचन/carminatives), appetizers (दीपन/appetizers), oleation, and sudation,
Then eliminate them with proper evacuants (शोधन/purification) at the right time according to strength.
Emetic drugs taken by mouth eliminate waste from stomach; nasal drops from head; enemas from large intestine.
When humors are excited and moving out naturally, do not stop them with astringent drugs.
Suppressing naturally outflowing excited humors produces severe complications like acute swelling and obesity.
If humors flow out on their own in a patient eating wholesome diet, observe them without stopping.
Or digest them using suitable carminative herbs and eliminate them safely.
Turbid, fibrous, thick, sticky, and slimy character defines vitiated Kapha humor.
Conversely, signs of proper digestion of humors include copper color and dark purplish clarity.
Pitta is yellow, clear, and warm; Kapha is clear, clumped, or viscous.
Un-vitiated Kapha is clear, slightly frothy, white, and sweet in taste.
Due to weakness of digestive fire (जठराग्नि/digestive fire), the primary body tissue chyme remains undigested.
This corrupted undigested chyme residing in stomach is called Ama (आम/undigested chyme).
Others state that Ama arises from severe mutual interaction of highly vitiated humors;
Just as toxic substances arise from the fermentation of Kodrava grain.
Ama-laden humors spread throughout the entire body should be carefully processed and eliminated;
Un-matured humors clinging deeply to tissues should not be forcibly extracted like raw unripe fruits.
For forcibly extracting un-matured humors destroys the host body due to extreme difficulty of removal.
They should first be digested with digestive/appetite-kindling remedies, oleated, and fomented;
Then eliminated with appropriate purificatory therapies at the proper time according to proximity and strength.
Emetic drugs taken orally eliminate morbid humors from the stomach.
Nasal drops eliminate morbid humors from above collarbone; enemas eliminate from colon.
Morbid humors moving on their own upward or downward should not be forcefully checked.
Checking active humors with suppressive drugs causes severe secondary diseases;
Such as obesity, skin diseases, and abdominal tumors as described in non-suppression of urges.
Spontaneously active morbid humors in a patient eating wholesome diet should be observed initially;
And subsequently digested with various digestive remedies or eliminated appropriately.
Thus ends the twenty-first chapter (एकविंशः अध्यायः/twenty-first chapter) named 'Management and Treatment Principles of Humors' (दोषोपक्रमणीयः/treatment of doshas) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the differential classification of diseases (रोगभेद/disease classification) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Diseases are categorized into seven primary origins (सप्तविध/sevenfold types): hereditary (सहज/hereditary), congenital (गर्भज/congenital), post-natal acquired (जातः/acquired), trauma-induced (पीडाकृत/trauma-derived), seasonal (कालज/seasonal), providential (प्रभावज/providential), and natural (स्वभावज/natural).
Each of these seven disease types is further divided into two subcategories (द्विविध/twofold classification).
Hereditary diseases (सहज/hereditary) arise from defects in paternal sperm or maternal ovum (शुक्र आर्त्तव दोष/sperm-ovum defects), such as skin diseases (कुष्ठ/dermatological disease), piles (अर्शः/piles), and diabetes (प्रमेह/diabetes), classified as paternal (पितृज/paternal) or maternal (मातृज/maternal).
Congenital diseases (गर्भज/congenital) arise from maternal dietary/lifestyle errors (जनन्यपचार/maternal misconduct), causing hunchback, lameness, blindness, or leucoderma, classified as nutrient-derived (अन्नरसज/plasma-derived) or unfulfilled maternal craving-derived (दौहृद विमानज/unfulfilled craving-derived).
Post-natal acquired diseases (जातज/acquired) arise from one's own dietary/lifestyle misconduct (स्वापचार/personal error), classified as over-nourishment-derived (संतर्पणज/over-nourishment) or under-nourishment-derived (अपतर्पणज/under-nourishment).
