When urge arises, bend forward, support head and sides, tickle throat gently with finger or lotus stalk, and vomit comfortably.
Vomit without violent straining, allowing the natural force of emetic medicine to expel Kapha and Pitta.
Observe the expelled matter: first comes emetic drug, then Kapha, then Pitta, and finally small Vata gas.
Signs of proper emesis (सम्यक् वमन/proper emesis): sequential expulsion of Kapha and Pitta, lightness of chest and head, clarity of senses, and freedom from nausea.
Signs of insufficient emesis (अयोग/insufficient emesis): lack of proper urge, expulsion of drug alone, itching, skin eruptions, heaviness, and sticky throat.
Signs of excessive emesis (अतियोग/excessive emesis): expulsion of blood, froth, intense thirst, fainting, throat dryness, and severe Vata fits.
After proper emesis, wash hands, feet, and face with warm water, inhale medicated smoke (धूम/herbal smoke), and rest in a windless room.
Follow the post-emetic dietary regimen (संसर्जन क्रम/post-purification diet): thin gruel (पेया/thin gruel), thick gruel (विलेपी/thick gruel), un-spiced soup (अकृत यूष/plain soup), spiced soup (कृत यूष/spiced soup), and meat broth over 3 to 7 days.
For maximum purification, give thin gruel for 3 meals, thick gruel for 3 meals, plain soup for 3 meals, and spiced soup for 3 meals.
For medium purification, give each food item for 2 meals; for minimum purification, give each food item for 1 meal.
Just as a tiny spark of fire gradually flares up when fed with dry grass and wood,
So also the digestive fire (जठराग्नि/digestive fire), weakened by emesis, gains supreme strength through sequential dietary regimen.
After emesis, allow 9 or 10 days of oleation and sudation before administering purgative therapy.
On the day prior to purgation, feed the patient light, unctuous, non-Kapha-provoking food with warm water.
On the morning of purgation, after previous food digests and morning time passes, evaluate bowel nature.
For soft bowel (मृदुकोष्ठ/soft bowel), milk, sugarcane juice, or Trivrit with raisins suffices to cause purgation.
For medium bowel (मध्यकोष्ठ/medium bowel), Triphala decoction with castor oil or Katuki powder acts effectively.
For hard bowel (क्रूरकोष्ठ/hard bowel), strong purgatives like Jayapala croton seed or Snuhi milk formulation are required.
Administer the appropriate purgative drink made with Trivrit, Haritaki, Aragvadha, or castor oil.
Sip warm water periodically to encourage bowel movement and ease purgation.
Observe the expelled stool: first comes fecal matter, then Pitta, then Kapha, and finally watery fluid.
Signs of proper purgation (सम्यक् विरेचन/proper purgation): clearing of intellect and senses, lightness of body, weakness of disease, and passage of Pitta.
Signs of insufficient purgation (अयोग/insufficient purgation): burning sensation, loss of appetite, heaviness of abdomen, itching, and retention of stool/gas.
Signs of excessive purgation (अतियोग/excessive purgation): expulsion of pure blood, fat, or flesh washings, prolapse of rectum, extreme thirst, and fainting.
In excessive purgation, wash body with cold water, give cooling sweet drinks, and apply stanching enema.
After proper purgation, follow the same sequential dietary regimen (संसर्जन क्रम/post-purification diet) as after emesis.
Avoid physical exertion, sun exposure, travel, day-sleep, anger, grief, and unwholesome food during post-purification period.
If purgative drug does not act and gets digested due to weak dose or dry bowel, do not repeat medicine on same day.
Feed the patient light unctuous food on same day and re-administer purgative medicine after 10 days with proper re-oleation.
In patients accustomed to suppressing natural urges out of fear or shyness, Vata gets aggravated making bowels hard.
Such patients should be well-oleated and sudated before attempting bowel purification.
In soft bowels, purgative medicine acts very quickly, whereas in hard bowels, strong medicine may be needed.
Improperly administered purgation causes abdominal distension, chest pain, nausea, vomiting, dizziness, and loss of digestive fire.
Re-oleate and sudate a person who has vomited before purging him the next day with light, non-Kapha, unctuous meat soup.
Complications of improper purgation include nausea, flatulence, anorexia, salivation, itching, burning, and Vata retention.
Proper purgation brings complete disease alleviation, lightness, appetite, and sensory clarity.
Excessive purgation leads to watery stool, blood, mucus, rectal prolapse, sunken eyes, and delirium.
Treat excessive purgation with cooling, astringent, stanching formulations and rectal suppositories.
After proper purgation, care for the patient according to emesis protocol excluding medicated smoke.
Gradually transition the patient through dietary steps back to his normal regular diet.
Emesis is indicated when digestive fire is active and Kapha is mature and accumulated in stomach.
Purgation is indicated when Kapha is cleared and Pitta is mature in intestines.
In low digestion or un-purified weak patient, administer light fasting if drug remains un-digested.
This prevents complications arising from drug indigestion and humor excitation.
Because purificatory evacuations, bloodletting, oleation, and fasting reduce digestive fire,
One must strictly observe the sequential gruel diet (पेयादि क्रम/gruel dietary sequence).
In chronic alcoholics or persons with low Kapha-Pitta, skip thin gruel and give refreshing sweet drinks (तर्पण/soothing drink).
Emetic drugs eliminate undigested immature humors, while purgative drugs eliminate digesting mature humors.
Therefore, do not wait for complete digestion before administering emetic therapy.
When emetic or purgative drug acts in reverse direction due to humor movement,
Sprinkle warm water on face for vomiting, and cold water on upper body for reverse purgation.
If a weak patient with abundant humors undergoes spontaneous purging due to humor digestion, feed him light bowel-binding food.
In weak, emaciated, or un-assessed bowel patients, give mild purgative in small repeated doses.
Mild doses taken repeatedly are safe, whereas a single large strong dose in weak patient risks life.
Eliminate abundant floating humors in small gentle increments repeatedly.
In weak persons, pacify small humors with mild herbs rather than aggressive evacuations.
Un-expelled morbid humors cause long-term suffering or death if left stagnant in tissues.
In low digestion and hard bowel, give processed ghee with alkali and salts to kindle digestion.
Once digestive fire is ignited and Kapha-Vata are subdued, perform proper purification.
In dry, Vata-predominant, hard-bowel persons who exercise daily and have bright fire,
Ordinary purgative medicine gets digested without purging the bowel.
Give non-unctuous decoction enema (निरूह बस्ति/decoction enema) first, followed by unctuous purgative drink.
Softened by enema and sharp suppository (फलवर्ति/suppository wick), the unctuous purgative easily expels stool.
In poisoning, trauma, skin diseases, edema, erysipelas, jaundice, anemia, and diabetes,
Purify patients with non-unctuous purgatives if unctuous, or unctuous purgatives if dry.
Between consecutive purificatory procedures like emesis and purgation,
Always administer oleation and sudation to restore body strength and mobilize humors.
Just as dry wood breaks if bent without unctuous sudation,
So also a body subjected to aggressive evacuations without oleation-sudation suffers severe tissue destruction.
A purgative or decoction enema is called sharp (तीक्ष्ण/sharp) when it acts swiftly, smoothly, without strain,
Without causing pain in heart or flanks, and completely expels all humors from internal organs.
Drugs untouched by insects or water, harvested in proper season, fresh, correctly processed, and given to well-oleated persons exhibit sharp potency.
Opposite conditions render the medicine mild and slow in action.
Match drug strength (sharp, medium, mild) to disease severity and patient strength.
Do not give a medicine to which the patient's body has become accustomed (सात्म्य/accustomed), as habitual drugs get digested without purging.
Improper purification acts like poison, whereas proper purification acts like divine nectar.
Properly performed purification brings mental clarity, sensory strength, tissue stability, bright digestive fire, and delays aging.
Thus, one should carefully administer purificatory evacuation therapies with full medical knowledge.
Here are summary verses on emetic and purgative procedures:
Administer emesis when Kapha is mature and digestive fire is active; administer purgation when Pitta is mature.
In weak digestion or un-cleared humors, feed light fasting to prevent drug indigestion.
This protects the patient from toxic ama complications and humor excitation.
Because evacuations reduce digestive fire, strictly observe the post-purification gruel diet.
For chronic drinkers or low Kapha-Pitta, substitute gruels with sweet soothing drinks.
Emesis expels undigested humors from stomach; purgation expels digesting humors from intestines.
Therefore, do not wait for complete digestion before giving emetic therapy.
When emetic or purgative acts in reverse direction, sprinkle warm or cold water accordingly.
If weak patient purges spontaneously after humor digestion, feed bowel-binding light foods.
In weak patients or unknown bowel types, give mild purgative in small repeated doses.
Repeated mild doses are safe, whereas a single strong dose risks life.
Eliminate abundant floating humors in small gentle increments repeatedly.
In weak persons, pacify small humors with mild herbs rather than aggressive evacuations.
Un-expelled morbid humors cause severe long-term suffering or death if left stagnant.
In low digestion and hard bowel, give processed ghee with alkali and salts to kindle digestion.
Once digestive fire is ignited and Kapha-Vata are subdued, perform proper purification.
In dry Vata-predominant persons, give decoction enema first, followed by unctuous purgative.
Softened by enema and suppository, the unctuous purgative easily expels stool.
In poisoning, skin diseases, edema, erysipelas, jaundice, and anemia, purify carefully according to unctuous/dry status.
Between consecutive purificatory procedures, always administer oleation and sudation.
Just as dry wood breaks if bent without unctuous sudation, un-oleated body suffers tissue damage.
A purgative or decoction enema is sharp when it acts swiftly, smoothly, without strain or cardiac pain.
Fresh, well-harvested, properly processed drugs given to well-oleated persons exhibit sharp potency.
Opposite conditions render the medicine mild and slow in action.
Match drug strength to disease severity and patient strength.
Do not give habitual drugs as they get digested without purging.
Improper purification acts like poison, whereas proper purification acts like divine nectar.
Properly performed purification brings mental clarity, sensory strength, tissue stability, bright digestive fire, and delays aging.
Administer emetic and purgative therapies in Spring, Early Monsoon, and Autumn seasons.
These three moderate seasons are ideal for purificatory evacuations without extreme heat or cold.
In seasonal transition periods (ऋतु संधि/seasonal junction), clear accumulated humors every 3 months.
By neutralizing cold, heat, and rain effects, execute purificatory actions without missing proper timing.
If no benefit is seen within 7 days, change the therapeutic line of treatment.
When previous disease force subsides, do not mix conflicting therapies unnecessarily.
Even for minor benefits, continue same line of treatment to achieve complete therapeutic excellence.
Medicine for a king should be pleasant, low dose, risk-free, pure, proven, and highly effective.
It should be approved by royal ministers and spiritual gurus, and tested by attendants before administration.
Herbs grown in a person's native habitat (देश/native region) are most wholesome for him.
Even when living elsewhere, herbs sharing similar elemental qualities are equally beneficial.
Potent, well-processed, fresh, aromatic medicine taken on empty stomach acts like divine nectar.
Administered with focused mind, proper dosage, and right timing, medicine restores perfect health.
Proper purification imparts mental clarity (बुद्धि प्रसाद/mental clarity), sensory power (इन्द्रिय बल/sensory power), tissue stability (धातु स्थिरता/tissue stability), and radiant digestive fire.
It delays the aging process (वयः पाक/aging delay) and grants long life when practiced regularly.
Thus, the wise physician should master the principles of emesis and purgation.
By applying these purificatory therapies with wisdom and precision, diseases are completely eradicated.
The physician who knows timing, dosage, and patient strength never fails in clinical practice.
Emesis and purgation clear the macro and micro channels, restoring natural balance of Vata, Pitta, and Kapha.
When channels are cleansed, nutrient plasma flows freely to nourish all seven bodily tissues.
The patient gains immense strength, glowing complexion, enthusiasm, and freedom from disease.
Therefore, purificatory evacuation is praised as the supreme line of treatment in Ayurveda.
This completes the detailed description of emetic and purgative procedures.
Let these eternal principles guide physicians for the welfare of all living beings.
Knowledge of purification leads to mastery over disease and grant of long life.
Thus ends the twenty-seventh chapter (सप्तविंशः अध्यायः/twenty-seventh chapter) named 'Emetic and Purgative Purification Therapies' (वमनविरेचनविधिः/emesis and purgation rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on enema purification therapies (बस्ति विधि/enema rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Enema therapy (बस्ति/enema treatment) is primarily indicated in Vata-dominant disorders (अनिल प्रधान/vata disorders).
Enema therapy is called Basti (बस्ति/bladder enema) because it nourishes the lower abdomen/bladder and is administered using a urinary bladder receptacle (मूत्राशय/urinary bladder).
It is the supreme among all therapeutic treatments (प्रधानतम उपक्रम/foremost treatment) because it acts rapidly, bestows instant comfort, builds tissue bulk (बृंहण/nourishing), and completely eradicates vitiated Vata (अनिल उच्छेद/vata eradication).
Vata humor is the sole independent mover (स्वतंत्र नेता/independent mover) of all humors, the singular cause of all bodily physical movements, fivefold in function, pervading all major and minor organs, and the master creator, sustainer, and destroyer of all diseases across all three disease pathways.
Because of its painless, gentle nature (सुखकर/painless treatment), enema therapy is prescribed for children (बाल/children), elderly (वृद्ध/elderly), emaciated (कृश/lean), obese (स्थूल/obese), depleted tissues/senses, women with Vata afflictions, infertility (अप्रजा/barrenness), and obstructed labor (कृच्छ्रप्रजा/difficult labor).
It bestows digestive fire (अग्नि/digestion), strength (बल/vitality), complexion (वर्ण/complexion), intellect (मेधा/intelligence), enthusiasm, long life (आयुः/lifespan), delays aging (वयःस्थापन/anti-aging), and is highly beneficial in paralysis (पंगु/lameness), fractures (भग्न/fractures), stiffness (स्तंभ/rigidity), spasms, flatulence (आध्मान/bloating), colic pain (शूल/colic), anorexia (अरोचक/loss of taste), upward gas (उदावर्त/reverse gas), and tenesmus (परिकर्तिका/cutting pain).
Enema therapy is categorized into three distinct types (त्रिविध/threefold types):
Non-unctuous decoction enema (आस्थापन/decoction enema), unctuous oil enema (अनुवासन/oil enema), and urethral/vaginal enema (उत्तरबस्ति/douche enema).
Non-unctuous decoction enema (आस्थापन/decoction enema) is prepared by combining diverse herbs according to the specific humor, disease, and affected tissue.
Its subtypes include exciting enema (उत्क्लेदन/humor exciter), drying enema (शोषण/moisture dryer), pacifying enema (शमन/humor pacifier), scraping enema (लेखन/fat scraper), nourishing enema (बृंहण/body builder), aphrodisiac enema (वाजीकरण/virility booster), slimy egg-white enema (पिच्छाबस्ति/slimy egg-white enema), and honey-oil enema (माषिकतैलिक/honey-oil enema).
Synonyms for the honey-oil enema include sustaining enema (यापन/life-supporting), suitable chariot enema (युक्तरथ/chariot-safe), humor-eliminating enema (दोषहर/disease remover), and perfected enema (सिद्धबस्ति/perfected enema).
Their inherent nature and therapeutic properties are indicated by their very designations.
It is called Asthapana (आस्थापन/stabilizing enema) because it establishes youth/lifespan and stabilizes vitiated humors in their proper physiological seats.
It is called Niruha (निरूह/toxin-cleansing enema) because it heals the body, eliminates morbid humors, and works through inconceivable potent action without remaining in the gut.
Unctuous oil enema (अनुवासन/unctuous oil enema) is prepared with medicated oils/fats processed with appropriate herbs for unction.
In oleation rules, unctuous fat intake is stated as fourfold: ghee, oil, muscle fat, and bone marrow.
A specific subtype is the small oil enema (मात्राबस्ति/small oil enema), equivalent in quantity to a modest drink of fat, usable daily like honey-oil enema by children, elderly, travelers, weightlifters, intellectuals, sexually active men, and delicate individuals without strict dietary restrictions, bestowing strength, complexion, easy stool passage, and humor pacification.
However, even small oil enemas should never be administered during indigestion (अजीर्ण/indigestion).
Nor should it be taken by those who sleep during daytime or are intoxicated (मत्त/intoxicated).
It is called Anuvasana (अनुवासन/retained oil enema) because it remains inside without causing harm or because it can be administered daily.
Urethral or vaginal enema (उत्तरबस्ति/urethral vaginal enema) acts like unctuous oil enema for oleation and like decoction enema for purification.
It is called Uttarabasti (उत्तरबस्ति/subsequent upper enema) because it is administered after decoction enema or inserted through the upper genital/urinary tract.
Indications for decoction enema (आस्थापन योग्य/decoction enema fit): abdominal tumors (गुल्म/phantom tumor), enlarged spleen (प्लीहा/spleen enlargement), flatulence (आनाह/bloating), severe colic pain (शूल/colic), chronic non-infectious diarrhea (शुद्धातीसार/chronic diarrhea), chronic fever (जीर्णज्वर/chronic fever), rhinitis (प्रतिश्याय/cold), gout (आढ्यरोग/gout), heart/chest pain (हृदय शूल/chest pain), flank pain (पार्श्व शूल/flank pain), uterine pain (योनि शूल/uterine pain), limb numbness (अंगसुप्ति/numbness), body wasting (शोष/emaciation), tremors (कंप/shaking), heaviness, urinary retention (अनिलाघात/urinary retention), intestinal rumbling (आंत्रकूजन/bowel sound), constipation (विट् संग/stool retention), dysuria (मूत्रसंग/urine retention), seminal obstruction (शुक्रसंग/semen retention), urinary calculi (अश्मरी/kidney stone), gravel (शर्करा/gravel), amenorrhea (नष्ट आर्तव/absent menses), lactation loss (स्तन्यनाश/loss of milk), insanity (उन्माद/mania), intestinal worms (कृमिकोष्ठ/worm infestation), irregular digestion (विषमाग्नि/irregular appetite), foul flatus, and all Vata disorders.
These disorders are completely destroyed by enema therapy at their root, just as cutting roots destroys a tree.
Contraindications for decoction enema (अनास्थाप्य/decoction enema unfit): over-oleated persons (अतिस्निग्ध/over-oleated), excited humors, chest injury (क्षतोरस्क/chest injury), extreme emaciation (अतिकृश/excessive lean), those who haven't received Niruha before, those undergoing emesis (वमन/vomiting), purgation (विरेचन/purgation), or nasal drops (नस्य/nasal drops), continuous vomiting (प्रसक्तच्छर्दि/persistent vomiting), spitting, cough (कास/cough), asthma (श्वास/asthma), hiccups (हिध्मा/hiccups), piles (अर्शस्/hemorrhoids), intestinal perforation (छिद्रोदर/bowel perforation), ascites (दकोदर/fluid ascites), severe bloating (आध्मान/flatulence), indigestion stagnation (अलसक/indigestion), gastroenteritis (विसूचिका/gastroenteritis), ama diarrhea (आमातीसार/ama diarrhea), anorexia (अरोचक/loss of taste), low digestion (अल्पाय्नि/weak digestion), anal edema (गुदशोफ/anal swelling), skin diseases (कुष्ठ/leprosy), intoxication (मद/intoxication), diabetes (प्रमेह/diabetes), and pregnant women before the 8th month.
In over-oleated persons and excited humors, decoction enema triggers abdominal dropsy, fainting, or severe body swelling.
In chest-injured and extremely emaciated persons, it rapidly destroys the weakened body.
The complications of administering Niruha without prior unction will be explained.
In those recently subjected to emesis or purgation, decoction enema burns the empty delicate channels like alkali poured on a raw wound.
In those recently unctioned, oil enema suppresses digestive fire and produces severe Kapha diseases.
In those undergoing nasal purge, it causes humor confusion due to wide-open upper channels.
In persistent vomiting, enema oil excites humors further.
In continuous vomiting, upward Vata carries the enema fluid forcefully into upper organs.
In hemorrhoids, blocked anal channels prevent enema entry, causing fatal pain.
Unctuous fat in hemorrhoids causes clogging and severe abdominal flatulence.
In perforated bowel and severe flatulence, enema causes fatal abdominal distension.
In alasaka indigestion, it worsens toxic ama accumulation.
In anorexia, it increases respective illness symptoms.
In pregnant women, it causes severe fetal injury or abortion as mentioned earlier.
Those fit for decoction enemas are also fit for unctuous oil enemas.
Specifically indicated in dry un-oleated persons, hyper-active digestion (अतिदीप्ताग्नि/excessive digestion), and pure Vata disorders.
They are supremely nourished by unctuous oil enemas, just as watering roots nourishes a tree.
Those unfit for decoction enemas are also unfit for unctuous oil enemas.
Additionally contraindicated in fasting persons without food, anemia (पांडुरोग/anemia), jaundice (कामला/jaundice), diabetes (प्रमेह/diabetes), rhinitis (प्रतिशवाय/cold), enlarged spleen (प्लीहा/spleen), Kapha ascites (कफोदर/kapha dropsy), gout (आढ्यवात/gout), loose stool, poisoning (विष/poison), acute Pitta-Kapha catarrh, heavy bowels, obesity (स्थूल/obesity), elephantiasis (श्लीपद/elephantiasis), goiter (गलगंड/goiter), scrofula (अपची/scrofula), and worm-infested intestines.
Administering oil enema to over-oleated and contraindicated states produces their respective severe complications.
Furthermore, regarding the dietary conditions for oil enemas:
If unctuous oil enema is administered to a fasting person with an empty stomach (रिक्तकोष्ठ/empty stomach),
The unctuous fat, being subtle, mobile, and deep-reaching, travels rapidly upward into the upper channels.
Consequently, it fails to conquer Vata located in its primary bowel seat.
Furthermore, it rapidly destroys internal digestive fire, especially in those without wind protection.
On the other hand, oil enema given after food is blocked by digested food in stomach, directing its action to bowel Vata.
It pacifies Vata located in the large intestine through food digestion.
Both sharp decoction enema and sharp Vata humor possess forceful rapid momentum.
When sharp decoction is given on a full stomach, it mixes with undigested food mass,
It moves upward or stays trapped with feces in the GI tract,
Trapping waste and food, it rapidly causes fatal toxicity.
Therefore, unctuous oil enema must be given after meals (भुक्तवत्/after meal), and decoction enema must be given on an empty stomach (अभुक्तवत्/empty stomach).
In anemia, jaundice, diabetes, rhinitis, spleen enlargement, and Kapha ascites,
Oil enema increases Kapha and fat, further clogging channels.
In gout, diarrhea, poisoning, and acute catarrh, oil enema aggravates morbidity.
In heavy bowels, obesity, elephantiasis, goiter, scrofula, and intestinal worms,
Oil enema increases heavy unctuous Kapha, leading to fatal channel obstruction.
Now, the specifications for enema equipment (बस्ति यन्त्र/enema apparatus) will be detailed.
The enema nozzle (नेत्र/enema nozzle) should be made of gold, silver, copper, iron, brass, bone, ivory, or hardwood.
It should be smooth, straight, tapering like a cow's tail (गोपुच्छ/cow tail shape), rounded, clean, and equipped with two ridge flanges (कर्णिक/ring flanges).
The nozzle length and orifice size should be calibrated according to patient age:
For a 1-year-old child: nozzle length 6 finger-widths (षडंगुल/6 fingers), orifice size of a mung bean (मुद्ग/mung bean size).
For an 8-year-old child: nozzle length 8 finger-widths (अष्टांगुल/8 fingers), orifice size of a pea (कलाय/pea size).
For a 16-year-old youth: nozzle length 10 finger-widths (दशांगुल/10 fingers), orifice size of a jujube fruit (बदर/jujube size).
For an adult (20+ years): nozzle length 12 finger-widths (द्वादशांगुल/12 fingers), orifice size of a wild plum (कर्कंधु/wild plum size).
The upper ring flange should be placed 2 finger-widths from nozzle base, and lower flange at the middle.
The enema pouch/bag (बस्तिपुटक/enema bag) should be made from a well-cured, supple, clean urinary bladder of a bull, buffalo, goat, or deer.
It should be dyed red, free from odor, soft, leak-proof, strong, and devoid of veins/holes.
In absence of animal bladder, a thick soft leather bag or water-proof cloth bag may be used.
The bladder bag is securely tied to the lower ring flange of the nozzle with strong thread.
Now, the dosage volume of decoction enema according to age is stated:
For a 1-year-old child, decoction volume is 1 prasrita (approx 96 ml).
For each additional year up to 12 years, increase volume by 1 prasrita annually.
From 12 to 18 years, increase volume by 1 prasrita every two years.
For adults (18 to 70 years), the standard maximum decoction dosage is 12 prasritas (approx 1.15 liters).
After 70 years of age, decoction volume should be reduced back to 10 or 8 prasritas due to tissue decline.
Now, the procedure for mixing decoction enema ingredients (निरूह कल्प/decoction preparation) is stated:
In a clean vessel, first mix rock salt (सैंधव/rock salt) and honey (क्षौद्र/honey) thoroughly.
Then add unctuous fat (गव्य घृत/medicated oil or ghee) and blend until uniform.
Next, add fine herbal paste (कल्क/herbal paste) and stir thoroughly.
Finally, pour warm herbal decoction (क्वाथ/herbal decoction) and churn into a smooth, homogenous mixture.
Proportion rule: 1 part salt, 2 parts honey, 3 parts fat, 2 parts paste, and 4 parts decoction.
The enema liquid should be warm, smooth, neither too thick nor too thin, and properly strain-filtered.
Now, the procedure for patient preparation and administration (बस्ति दान विधि/enema administration procedure) is stated:
The patient should first be gently massaged with warm Vata-pacifying medicated oil.
Then subjected to mild sudation/fomentation on abdomen, back, and waist.
After passing natural urges of urine and stool, the patient should lie on a comfortable level bed.
Positioning: lie flat on the left side (वाम पार्श्व/left lateral posture), extending left leg straight and flexing right knee toward chest.
The physician should lubricate the patient's rectum and enema nozzle tip with medicated oil.
Holding the lubricated nozzle parallel to spinal column, insert it smoothly into rectum up to the first ring flange without shaking.
Holding nozzle steady with left hand, squeeze the enema pouch continuously with right hand in one steady, confident pressure.
Do not squeeze completely dry; leave a small fraction of liquid inside to prevent air entry into rectum.
Withdraw nozzle gently, tap buttocks lightly, elevate legs, and massage waist, thighs, and feet.
The patient should then lie flat on back with a soft pillow under hips.
Now, the retention time and evacuation of decoction enema are detailed:
The ideal retention time for decoction enema is 1 muhurta (48 minutes).
Upon urge, the patient should sit on a comfortable raised toilet seat and void naturally without straining.
Signs of proper decoction enema expulsion (सम्यक् निरूढ/proper decoction cleansing):
Sequential expulsion of flatus, stool, urine, and vitiated Pitta/Kapha humors, followed by lightness of body, abdominal comfort, appetite kindle, and disease relief.
Signs of insufficient decoction enema expulsion (अयोग/under-cleansing):
Retention of enema liquid, constipation, bloating, body heaviness, pain in heart/groin, itching, and absence of humor release.
Signs of excessive decoction enema expulsion (अतियोग/over-cleansing):
Watery bloody diarrhea, severe exhaustion, thirst, cramps, fainting, and prolapse of rectum.
Emergency management if decoction enema fails to return within 48 minutes:
Administer a sharp salt-oil-urine enema, insert a fruit suppository (फलवर्ति/fruit suppository), or perform warm abdominal sudation immediately.
Unreturned decoction enema acts like trapped poison and can cause fatal toxicity if neglected.
After proper decoction evacuation, the patient should bathe with warm water.
Then eat a light meal with game meat broth (जांगल रस/game broth) or spiced mung soup.
In the evening, administer a small unctuous oil retention enema (अनुवासन/oil retention enema) to protect channels against Vata rebound.
Now, the rules for unctuous oil enema dosage and schedule are detailed:
The standard dosage volume for unctuous oil enema in adults is 3 prasritas (approx 288 ml).
For small oil enema (मात्राबस्ति/small oil enema), dosage is 1.5 prasritas (approx 144 ml).
Oil enema procedure: given immediately after evening meal while food is half-digested.
Patient lies on left side, lubricated nozzle inserted, fat injected smoothly, buttocks tapped, legs raised, and body rested on back.
Retention time for oil enema: should remain inside for 3 yamas (approx 9 hours) or overnight.
Next morning, oil returns naturally bringing out dark unctuous feces and flatus.
If oil returns immediately without staying, repeat administration with warm salt-oil mixture.
If oil stays inside without returning for 24 hours but patient feels light, comfortable, with appetite, no treatment is needed as fat is absorbed by tissues.
If oil stays inside causing bloating, nausea, and discomfort, administer a mild purgative or salt-water wash.
Now, the specific enema courses and schedules (बस्ति क्रम/enema schedules) are detailed:
Karma Basti (कर्मबस्ति/30-enema full course): 30 enemas total over 30 days.
Schedule: 1 initial oil enema + 12 alternating decoction/oil enemas + 5 final oil enemas (30 total).
Kala Basti (कालबस्ति/15-enema half course): 15 enemas total over 15 days.
Schedule: 1 initial oil enema + 6 alternating decoction/oil enemas + 3 final oil enemas (15 total).
Yoga Basti (योगबस्ति/8-enema short course): 8 enemas total over 8 days.
Schedule: 1 initial oil enema + 3 alternating decoction/oil enemas + 2 final oil enemas (8 total).
Enema course sequence according to humors:
For Kapha disorders: 1 or 3 unctuous oil enemas combined with dry sharp decoctions.
For Pitta disorders: 5 or 7 unctuous oil enemas combined with sweet cooling decoctions.
For Vata disorders: 9 or 11 unctuous oil enemas combined with warm Dashamula decoctions.
Alternating oil and decoction enemas prevents over-drying from decoctions and over-clogging from oils.
Never administer pure oil enemas exclusively nor pure decoction enemas exclusively for long periods.
Exclusive oil enemas destroy digestive fire and induce Kapha stagnation; exclusive decoction enemas aggravate Vata and dry out tissues.
Therefore, alternate oil enema (अनुवासन/oil enema) and decoction enema (निरूह/decoction enema) in proper rhythm.
Now, the glorious benefits of completing enema courses are declared:
One who undergoes an 18-enema course properly achieves a disease-free body, radiant senses, horse-like stamina, elephant-like strength, stable intellect, and 1000-year healthy lifespan.
Now, the detailed instructions for Urethral and Vaginal Enema (उत्तरबस्ति विधि/urethral vaginal enema) are detailed:
Uttarabasti is indicated after 2 or 3 purificatory decoction enemas have cleaned the lower bowel.
Male urethral nozzle specifications: 12 finger-widths long, smooth, rounded like a Jasmine flower stalk (मालतीपुष्प/jasmine stalk shape), orifice size of a mustard seed.
Male urethral procedure: patient sits comfortably on a soft stool at knee height.
Penis held erect and straight, a smooth probe inserted to clear channel, then lubricated nozzle inserted into urethra up to 4 finger-widths.
Medicated oil/ghee (approx 1 shukti = 24 ml) injected smoothly into urinary bladder.
Female vaginal/urethral nozzle specifications: 10 finger-widths long, orifice size of a mung bean (मुद्ग/mung bean size).
Female vaginal insertion depth: nozzle inserted 4 finger-widths into vaginal canal (अपत्यमार्ग/vaginal passage).
Female urethral insertion depth: nozzle inserted 2 finger-widths into female urethra (मूत्रवर्त्म/female urethra) (1 finger-width in young girls).
Female Uttarabasti timing: administered during menstrual period (आर्तव काल/menstrual period) when uterine mouth is open, un-constricted, and receptive.
Female patient posture: lies flat on back with knees flexed and thighs elevated (उत्तान/supine knees flexed).
Dosage volume for adult women: 1 prasrita (approx 96 ml) for vaginal douche; 1 shukti (approx 24 ml) for urethral douche.
Administered 3 to 4 times daily over 3 consecutive days/nights, increasing fat dosage gradually.
Rest 3 days, then repeat for another 3-day cycle if needed.
Indications for Uttarabasti in women: uterine pain (योनि शूल/uterine pain), vaginal prolapse (योनिभ्रंश/vaginal prolapse), menorrhagia (असृग्दर/menorrhagia), dysmenorrhea, dysuria, and female infertility.
Indications for Uttarabasti in men: urinary calculi, dysuria, strangury, seminal disorders, and prostate/bladder ailments.
Properly administered Uttarabasti cures all genitourinary disorders and restores reproductive fertility.
Sequential purging, emesis, decoction enemas, and urethral douches completely purify all channels.
Summary verse on humoral enema counts: 1 to 3 oil enemas for Kapha, 5 to 7 for Pitta, 9 to 11 for Vata.
Decoction formulas: Vata uses Dashamula decoction with Trivrit, salt, and warm fats.
Pitta uses Nyagrodhadi decoction with milk, ghee, sugar, honey, and lotus extracts.
Kapha uses Aragvadhadhi decoction with honey, cow's urine, sharp spices, and dry herbs.
Sannipata uses balanced combination of all three herb groups.
Charaka's school states enema is the half-treatment or full treatment for all diseases.
Because Vata governs all bodily humors and diseases, its elimination via enema resolves all pathology.
Enema cleanses excited humors, pacifies residual morbidities, and restores tissue health.
Proper enema course sequence: 1 initial oil, 12 alternating decoctions, 5 final oils (Karma Basti).
Kala Basti (15 enemas) and Yoga Basti (8 enemas) provided according to patient strength.
Never use exclusive oil or exclusive decoction enemas continuously without alternation.
Alternating oil and decoction enemas balances unction and purification.
One who undergoes 18-enema purification as prescribed becomes disease-free, energetic, and long-lived.
With elephant strength, horse stamina, sharp digestion, and indestructible sensory vitality.
Female Uttarabasti administered after 2-3 preliminary bowel cleansings.
Male nozzle 12 finger-widths; female nozzle 10 finger-widths; vaginal depth 4 fingers, urethral depth 2 fingers.
Smooth, rounded, tapering, made of noble metals or hardwood.
Inserted gently without pain into urinary or genital canal.
Like a flower stalk, smooth and leak-proof.
Bladder pouch soft, supple, light, containing exact dose.
Patient bathed, fed, seated or supine comfortably.
Lying flat on soft bed with flexed legs.
Erect penis probed smoothly, nozzle inserted 4 fingers.
Fat injected steadily into bladder without shaking.
Nozzle withdrawn, patient rested, buttocks tapped.
Vaginal canal opened during menses, nozzle inserted 4 fingers.
Fat injected into uterine cavity, patient rested.
Repeated 3-4 times daily for 3 days.
Resting 3 days, repeating cycle as needed.
Vaginal mouth open and receptive during menstrual phase.
Administered also in emergency menorrhagia or acute prolapse.
In genitourinary disorders of women, Uttarabasti is the supreme remedy.
Nozzle length 12 fingers for men, 10 fingers for women.
Smooth, rounded, tapering, made of noble metals or hardwood.
Inserted gently without pain into urinary or genital canal.
Like a flower stalk, smooth and leak-proof.
Bladder pouch soft, supple, light, containing exact dose.
Patient bathed, fed, seated or supine comfortably.
Lying flat on soft bed with flexed legs.
Erect penis probed smoothly, nozzle inserted 4 fingers.
Fat injected steadily into bladder without shaking.
Nozzle withdrawn, patient rested, buttocks tapped.
Vaginal canal opened during menses, nozzle inserted 4 fingers.
Fat injected into uterine cavity, patient rested.
Repeated 3-4 times daily for 3 days.
Resting 3 days, repeating cycle as needed.
Vaginal mouth open and receptive during menstrual phase.
Administered also in emergency menorrhagia or acute prolapse.
Curing uterine displacement, vaginal pain, menorrhagia, and discharge.
Nozzle 10 fingers for vaginal route, 4 fingers insertion.
Nozzle insertion 2 fingers for female urethral route.
For young girls, nozzle insertion 1 finger-width.
Dose: 1 prasrita for adult women, 1 shukti for young girls.
Patient supine with knees flexed toward chest.
Administered 3-4 times in 24-hour cycle.
Increasing fat dose over 3 consecutive nights.
Resting 3 days, repeating for another 3 days.
Purgation given 15 days after emesis; enema given 15 days after purgation.
Oil enema given immediately after decoction enema evacuation; oil enema given on 7th night after purgation.
Just as a clean cloth absorbs bright dye from colored water,
So enema therapy extracts all liquefied morbid toxins from the entire body.
Thus ends the twenty-eighth chapter (अष्टाविंशः अध्यायः/twenty-eighth chapter) named 'Enema Purification Therapies' (बस्तिविधिः/enema rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on nasal medication therapy (नस्य विधि/nasal medication rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Nasal medication therapy (नस्य/nasal drops) is specifically indicated in diseases of the supraclavicular organs (ऊर्ध्वजत्रु विकार/head and neck diseases).
The nasal passage is the direct gateway to the head (नासा शिरसो द्वारम्/nose is head's portal).
Drug instilled through the nostrils pervades the brain head region, reaches channels of nose, mouth, head, eyes, ears, and throat, detaches morbid toxins, and forcefully extracts them like pulling out a hook from a thicket.
That nasal medication therapy is threefold (त्रिविध नस्य/threefold nasal therapy):
Purificatory evacuant (विरेचन/cleansing drops), unctuous nourishing (स्नेहन/unctuous drops), and palliative pacifying (शमन/palliative drops).
It is further categorized into six administrative forms (षड्विध नस्य/sixfold nasal forms):
Full-dose drops (मर्ष/full dose drops), daily micro drops (प्रतिमर्ष/daily micro drops), pressed herbal juice (अवपीड/pressed juice), blowing powder (प्रधमन/blowing powder), medicated smoke (धूम/herbal smoke), and unctuous drops.
Purificatory evacuative nasal therapy (विरेचन नस्य/cleansing nasal drops) is indicated in Kapha discharge, head heaviness, headache, rhinitis (पीनस/cataract cold), stiffness, eye edema, cervical lymphadenitis (गण्डमाला/scrofula), tumors (अर्बुद/tumor), nodules, toothlock, epilepsy (अपस्मार/epilepsy), and insanity (उन्माद/mania).
It consists of both liquid juice preparations and dry powder formulations.
Blowing powder nasal therapy (प्रधमन नस्य/blowing powder) is administered by blowing medicated powder through a six-finger-long tube with mouth air.
It is indicated in severe Kapha eye-head congestion or during complete loss of consciousness (संज्ञा नाश/fainting).
Unctuous nourishing nasal therapy (स्नेहन नस्य/nourishing unctuous drops) is indicated in Vata-born head pain, dry wasting, falling teeth, ocular spasms, jawlock (हनुग्रह/jawlock), and neck stiffness (मन्यास्तम्भ/stiff neck).
Palliative pacifying nasal therapy (शमन नस्य/pacifying drops) is indicated in bleeding disorders (रक्तपित्त/bleeding disease), thirst, burning sensation, and red inflamed eyes.
Squeezed juice therapy (अवपीड नस्य/pressed juice) uses sharp herbal pastes or fresh juices, acting as evacuant or palliative in bleeding disorders and chest injuries.
Daily micro-dose therapy (प्रतिमर्ष/daily micro drops) uses 2 drops of fat and is safe at all times, protecting the head continuously just like full nasal therapy.
For both full dose and micro drops, unctuous oil (तैल/sesame oil) alone or processed with herbs is supreme,
Because the head is dominated by Kapha humor, and Kapha is counteracted and synthesized well by oil.
Medicated ghee (सर्पिस्/ghee) is used in Vata and Pitta conditions.
Muscle fat (वसा/muscle fat) and bone marrow (मज्जा/marrow) are used in severe standalone Vata disorders.
Seasonally, nasal therapy should be administered in early monsoon (प्रावृट्/early monsoon), autumn (शरद्/autumn), and spring (वसन्त/spring).
In early monsoon, give nasal drops when clouds rain and cool the air.
In autumn, give nasal drops when intense sky heat subsides.
In spring, give nasal drops to counteract Kapha aggravation.
Avoid nasal drops during extreme heat, severe cold, or heavy downpours, except in urgent medical conditions.
In summer (ग्रीष्म/summer season), administer nasal therapy during the cool forenoon.
In winter (हेमन्त/winter season), administer nasal therapy during noon.
In acute medical emergencies, administer anytime by cooling the head in summer or warming it in winter.
Nasal medication is contra-indicated in persons overheated by sun/fire, indigestion, drunken state, desiring alcohol, after drinking water/oil, after bloodletting, acute thirst, exhaustion, fainting, acute bleeding, excessive salivation, recently given oil enema, near delivery, puerperal women, acute asthma/cough, bowel retention, or after recent purificatory vomiting/purgation.
In overheated persons, nasal drops excite humors in the head, causing dizziness and severe Vata fits.
In indigestion (अजीर्ण/indigestion), it induces severe stiffness and head disorders.
In drunk or alcohol-desiring persons, it produces facial swelling and cataract/blindness (तिमिर/cataract).
In persons who just drank water or oil, it produces rhinitis and cervical lymph glands.
In blood-depleted patients, it excites severe Vata disorders.
In thirsty or exhausted persons, nasal heat increases humors excessively.
In bleeding disorders, it triggers fatal hemorrhages.
In excessive salivation, it increases Kapha further, producing heavy head congestion.
In oil-enema patients, it causes head heaviness and throat constriction.
In pregnant women near delivery, it causes fetal distress or miscarriage (गर्भभ्रंश/miscarriage).
In puerperal women, it causes vaginal pain and puerperal fever (सूतिका ज्वर/puerperal fever).
In asthma and cough sufferers, it excites humors and multiplies disease severity.
In bowel retention (आनाह/constipation), it excites severe Vata fits.
In enema patients, it corrupts stomach channels, causing severe head diseases.
For eligible patients at proper time, first lubricate and foment the chest, head, and neck with mild heat, ensure bowel/bladder evacuation, and then administer nasal drops.
Administer in morning for Kapha disorders.
Administer at noon for Pitta disorders.
Administer in evening or late afternoon for Vata disorders.
Administer at night in acute night conditions or cloudy weather emergencies.
Its dosage (मात्रा/dose) is threefold, measured in drops (बिन्दु/drops).
For purificatory evacuant nasal drops, the minimum dose is 8 drops.
The medium dose is 10 drops; maximum dose is 12 drops.
One drop (बिन्दु/drop) is defined as the liquid volume that falls from index finger dipped two joints deep into liquid.
For unctuous nourishing and palliative drops, the doses are 6, 8, and 10 drops respectively.
The exact procedural step-by-step method is as follows:
In a windless clean room, on a soft bed with head slightly lowered and feet raised, after mild face/neck oil massage and fomentation, the physician lifts the nostril with left hand and instills warm medicated drops in an unbroken stream into nostrils using a tube or leaf cup.
Then gently massage the forehead, cheeks, lips, jaw, and neck with oil-smeared palms.
Wipe the eyes with clean cloth; do not wash eyes with water immediately.
Because cold water wash over warm oil causes liquid congestion and destroys eyesight.
The patient should slowly draw the nasal oil into nostrils, turn prone, and spit out phlegm and saliva into a vessel without swallowing it.
Then warm the head slightly and gargle with Kapha-destroying herbal decoction 16 times.
Inhale medicated smoke (धूम/herbal smoke) to loosen remaining Kapha in throat.
Administer 2 or 3 times a day.
Maximum course is up to 21 times (3x7 days).
Hold Kapha-destroying gargle for 1 to 3 days post-treatment.
Proper purificatory nasal therapy brings Kapha pacification, lightness of head, and sensory clarity.
Excessive purificatory nasal therapy excites Vata, causing pricking head and brain pain.
Insufficient purificatory nasal therapy causes Kapha congestion, excessive salivation, and nausea.
Proper unctuous nasal therapy brings pleasant sleep, mental joy, and head lightness.
Excessive unctuous nasal therapy causes salivation and Kapha heaviness.
Insufficient unctuous nasal therapy causes dryness in head.
Proper palliative nasal therapy pacifies targeted diseases.
Insufficient palliative nasal therapy causes disease recurrence.
Excessive palliative nasal therapy produces its own specific complications.
After proper evacuation, follow with Vata-pacifying measures and medicated smoking.
Pressed juice drops and blowing powder follow similar rules to unctuous and evacuant nasal therapies.
Daily micro drops (प्रतिमर्ष/micro drops) should be applied using finger-tip (2 drops) upon waking, after vomiting, exercise, bath, long walks, sex, drinking, or meals.
It has 32 safe administration occasions and a fixed 2-drop dose.
Applied upon waking, it expels overnight accumulated nasal wastes.
Applied after vomiting, it clears residual stomach congestion.
Applied after exercise, it soothes head channels.
Applied after bath, it cleanses sense channels.
Applied after travel, it relieves dust and wind fatigue.
Applied after meals, it cleanses head channels while nutrient chyme ascends.
Therefore, daily micro drops are safe at all times and recommended even for newborn infants.
However, even micro drops should not be overused habitually without need.
Avoid in severely emaciated persons, acute bleeding, or indigestion.
In indigestion, micro drops induce severe stiffness.
On an empty fasting stomach, it produces mouth dryness.
In acute bleeding, it excites severe Vata disorders.
Here are summary verses on nasal therapy:
In all diseases of the head, nasal medication therapy is declared supreme.
Instilled through nostrils, it draws out Vata and all morbid humors localized in the head.
It quickly eliminates all diseases above the clavicle.
Therefore, nasal therapy should always be performed for head ailments.
A person who avoids nasal therapy suffers persistent head diseases.
Proper nasal administration ensures freedom from head diseases.
Diseases of head, neck, eyes, and chest never afflict those who practice nasal therapy.
Visual strength and sensory acuity increase steadily in those accustomed to nasal drops.
Equalize full dose drops and micro drops according to drop volume.
The maximum dose of full nasal drops is 12 drops, medium is 10 drops, minimum is 8 drops.
One drop is defined by liquid falling from index finger dipped 2 joints deep.
This standard drop measure is maintained across all nasal applications.
Nasal therapy should be followed by mild unctuous or sudation measures.
Head purification is followed by palliative or unctuous measures.
Nasal therapy may be administered 2 or 3 times according to patient strength.
Head cleansing is followed by unctuous soothing therapy.
Decoctions and nasal drops that pacify Vata are highly praised.
After drinking nasal drops, do not immediately wash with cold water.
Administer nasal therapy when head, throat, and chest are cleansed.
Proper head cleansing ensures clear channels and mental lightness.
Apply unctuous or palliative drops according to prescribed rules.
Assessing disease strength, humors, and season, the wise physician prescribes nasal drops.
A wise physician undoubtedly chooses the exact nasal formulation required.
One who receives proper nasal therapy in this manner,
Has all aggravated humors of the head completely pacified.
Sensory organs gain supreme clarity and the head feels light.
Eyesight becomes clear, and pain in nose, neck, and head disappears.
Thus ends the twenty-ninth chapter (एकोनत्रिंशः अध्यायः/twenty-ninth chapter) named 'Nasal Medication Therapy' (नस्यविधिः/nasal therapy rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on medicated smoking therapy (धूमपान विधि/medicated smoking rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Medicated smoking (धूमपान/medicated smoke) is administered to relieve pain in head, eyes, and ears, eye inflammation, heaviness, hemicrania (अर्धावभेदक/migraine), rhinitis (पीनस/rhinitis), cough (कास/cough), dyspnea (श्वास/asthma), bad mouth taste (आस्यवैरस्य/bad taste), excessive salivation (प्रसेक/salivation), hoarseness of voice (वैस्वर्य/voice loss), foul breath or nasal odor (पूतिघ्राण/foul nasal discharge), hiccups (हिध्मा/hiccup), throat ailments, toothache, weakness, anorexia (अरुचि/appetite loss), lockjaw (हनुग्रह/jaw stiffness), stiff neck (मन्याग्रह/neck stiffness), worm infestations (कृमि/worms), itching (कंडू/itching), paleness, hair defects, suppressed or excessive sneezing, drowsiness (तंद्रा/drowsiness), hypersomnia, and all Vata-Kapha disorders above the collarbone (ऊर्ध्वजत्रुगत/above clavicle).
It also clarifies the skull (शिरः कपाल/skull bone), sense organs (इन्द्रिय/senses), and channels carrying mental functions (मनोवह स्रोतस्/mental channels).
Even if prepared from cooling substances (शीत द्रव्य/cooling drugs), smoke becomes heating due to fire contact (अग्नि संयोग/fire contact), making it contrary to Pitta and blood (पित्त रक्त/pitta and blood).
Medicated smoking is threefold (त्रिविध/threefold).
Namely: Palliative pacifying (शमन/palliative), unctuous nourishing (बृंहण/nourishing), and purificatory cleansing (शोधन/cleansing).
Furthermore, cough-relieving (कासघ्न/anti-cough), emetic (वामन/vomiting inducer), and wound fumigation (व्रणधूपन/wound fumigation) varieties are also described.
Palliative smoke (शमन/palliative smoke) is also termed daily habitual (प्रायोगिक/habitual smoke) or medium (मध्यम/medium intensity).
Nourishing smoke (बृंहण/nourishing smoke) is also termed unctuous (स्नेहन/unctuous smoke) or mild (मृदु/mild intensity).
Cleansing smoke (शोधन/cleansing smoke) is also termed purgative (विरेचन/purgative smoke) or sharp (तीक्ष्ण/sharp intensity).
Medicated smoking is contraindicated in those who have undergone purgation (विरेचन/purgation), enema (बस्ति/enema), night vigil (रात्रि जागरण/night vigil), head injury, intake of honey, curd, milk, wine, fats, or gruel, poison ingestion, fish intake, anemia (पांडु/anemia), diabetes (प्रमेह/diabetes), ascites (उदर/dropsy), bloating (आध्मान/flatulence), upward Vata, cataract (तिमिर/cataract), severe diphtheria (रोहिणी/diphtheria), bleeding disorders (रक्तपित्त/hemorrhage), or during severe hot weather (अत्युष्ण/excess heat).
In these contraindicated subjects, smoking causes giddiness (भ्रम/dizziness), fever (ज्वर/fever), head heat, sensory impairment, palate dryness or ulceration (तालुशोष/palate dryness), sensation of smoke emission (धूमायन/smoke feeling), vomiting, fainting (मूर्च्छा/fainting), severe bleeding, or even death (मृत्यु/death).
Excessive smoking (अतिमात्र/over-smoking) produces similar fatal complications even in fit individuals.
In case of smoke complications, identify Pitta combined with Vata or Kapha, and administer intake of ghee (सर्पिस्/ghee), decoctions, nasal drops, facial pastes, collyrium, and cooling showers.
By this, management of all complications arising from improper smoking is explained.
When smoke severely obstructs all bodily channels, severe complications arise: burning heat (दाह/burning), lip swelling, eye redness (नेत्रराग/eye redness), asthma (श्वास/asthma), cough (कास/cough), rhinitis (पीनस/rhinitis), body weakness, and voice loss.
In such cases, use ghee, milk, sugarcane juice, raisins, and sugar, along with mild emesis and pungent or bitter nasal drops and gargles.
Habitual palliative smoke (प्रायोगिक/habitual smoke) has 8 occasions: after waking at night, after urination, after defecation, after toothbrushing, after unctuous intake, after emesis, after meals, and after surgical procedures.
Mild unctuous smoke (मृदु/mild unctuous smoke) has 11 occasions: after sneezing, sexual intercourse, laughing, prolonged sitting, yawning, urination, defecation, toothbrushing, comforting drinks, putapaka, and surgical procedures.
Sharp cleansing smoke (तीक्ष्ण/sharp cleansing smoke) has 5 occasions: after nasal drops, collyrium, vomiting, bath, and day sleep.
Because Vata and Kapha humors become naturally aggravated during these specific occasions.
The smoking pipe nozzle (धूमनेत्र/smoking pipe nozzle) should be made of metal or wood like enema nozzles, shaped like a cow-tail (गोपुच्छ/cow-tail shaped), straight, three-ringed, smooth, with top opening fitting a rajamasha seed, measuring 40 fingers (चत्वारिंशत्/40 fingers) for palliative, 32 fingers for unctuous, and 24 fingers for cleansing smoke in patient-finger measure.
For anti-cough and emetic smoking, pipe length is 10 fingers (दशांगुल/10 fingers); for wound fumigation, pipe length is 8 fingers (अष्टांगुल/8 fingers) with orifice passing a horsegram seed.
Moving from a distance through three rings, smoke becomes thin and gentle, soothing the organs without causing heat injury.
In anti-cough smoking, if metallic nozzle is unavailable, hollow reed pipes (नल/reed pipe), bamboo, or castor stems may be used.
Take 1 aksha (20g) of fine paste of smoking drugs, smear it onto a 12-finger long reed rod soaked in water overnight.
Coat it five times to make a thumb-thick, barley-centered wick (धूमवर्ति/smoking stick) 9 fingers long, dry in shade, pull out the inner rod, smear with ghee, ignite on charcoal, insert into pipe nozzle base, and offer to patient.
The fit patient sitting upright with cheerful mind, open lips and teeth, eyes focused on nozzle tip, should inhale through one nostril while closing the other, and exhale exclusively through the mouth (मुख/mouth).
Smoke inhaled through mouth must be exhaled through mouth only; exhaling through nose impairs eyesight (दृग्विघात/vision impairment).
For habitual smoke, inhale 2-3 puffs (आपान/puffs) across 3 turns; inhale through nose first if humors are above throat, through mouth first if in throat; administer twice daily (द्विवार/twice daily).
Unctuous nourishing smoke: 3-4 puffs until tear secretion starts; administered once daily.
Sharp cleansing smoke: 4 puffs through nostrils until channel lightness occurs; administered 3-4 times daily.
Inhaling smoke and releasing it constitutes one single puff (आपान/inhalation puff).
For anti-cough smoking, place herbal powder or pill on smokeless glowing charcoal in an earthen saucer, cover with a perforated saucer, attach pipe nozzle, and inhale through mouth only.
Smoke reaching chest must be exhaled through mouth only.
When smoke subsides, add fresh herb powder and repeat until channel purification and body lightness occur.
Similarly, for emetic smoking, drink thin sesame gruel first and then inhale smoke to induce vomiting.
Similarly, fumigate ulcers or wounds (व्रणधूपन/wound fumigation) to relieve moisture, pain, and foul odor.
Inadequate smoking (अयोग/under-smoking) excites humors and increases disease.
Excessive smoking (अतियोग/over-smoking) leads to previously stated severe complications.
Here are summary verses:
Clarity of heart, throat, and sense organs, lightness of head, and pacification of ailments indicate proper smoking.
These are the characteristic signs of properly administered medicated smoking.
Palliative smoke is indicated in Vata-Kapha combinations and healthy daily routine.
Nourishing unctuous smoke is praised in Vata disorders; sharp cleansing smoke is indicated in Kapha excess.
Thus ends the thirtieth chapter (त्रिंशः अध्यायः/thirtieth chapter) named 'Medicated Smoking Therapy' (धूमपानविधिः/medicated smoking rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on gargle, mouth rinse, and facial application rules (गंडूषादि विधि/gargle and paste rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Gargling therapy (गंडूष/gargle) is fourfold: unctuous (स्नेहिक/unctuous gargle), palliative pacifying (शमन/palliative gargle), purificatory cleansing (शोधन/cleansing gargle), and healing (रोपण/healing gargle).
The first three types pacify Vata, Pitta, and Kapha disorders respectively.
Healing gargle (रोपण/healing gargle) cures mouth ulcers (मुखव्रण/stomatitis/mouth ulcers); palliative gargle is also called stanching (स्तंभन/stanching), pleasing (प्रसादन/pleasing), or heat-relieving (निर्वापण/heat relieving).
Unctuous gargle (स्नेहिक/unctuous gargle) uses fats prepared or mixed with sweet, sour, salty, and warm herbs, meat broth (मांस रस/meat broth), sesame paste water (तिल कल्क उदक/sesame paste water), or milk (क्षीर/milk).
Palliative gargle (शमन/palliative gargle) uses bitter, astringent, sweet, and cooling decoctions of Patola, Neem, Jambu, Mango, Malati buds, lotus, licorice water, sugar water, honey, milk, sugarcane juice, or ghee.
Purificatory gargle (शोधन/cleansing gargle) uses pungent, sour, salty, and warm head-cleansing herbs (शिरोविरेचन/head errhines) mixed with vinegar (शुक्त/vinegar), wine (मद्य/wine), sour rice-gruel water (धान्याम्ल/sour gruel water), urine, or paste mixtures.
Healing gargle (रोपण/healing gargle) uses astringent, sweet, and cooling herbs as specifically indicated.
Furthermore (अपिच/moreover):
In tooth sensitivity (दंतहर्ष/tooth tingling), loose teeth (दंतचाल/loose teeth), and Vata mouth disorders (वातज मुखरोग/vata mouth ailments),
Lukewarm or cold sesame paste water (तिल कल्क उदक/sesame paste water) held in the mouth is wholesome.
For daily gargling (नित्य गंडूष/daily gargle), sesame oil (तैल/sesame oil) or meat broth (मांस रस/meat broth) is recommended.
In burning mouth sensation, inflammation, suppuration (पाक/suppuration), or traumatic mouth injury (क्षत/mouth injury),
Or in toxic exposure and alkali burns (क्षार दग्ध/alkali burn), ghee (सर्पिस्/ghee) or milk (पयस्/milk) should be held in the mouth.
Honey gargle (मधु गंडूष/honey gargle) produces clarity in the mouth (वैशद्य/mouth clarity), heals mouth ulcers (मुखव्रण/mouth ulcers),
And pacifies burning heat (दाह/burning heat) and excessive thirst (तृष्णा/thirst).
Sour rice-gruel water (धान्याम्ल/sour gruel water) destroys bad mouth taste (आस्यवैरस्य/bad taste) and foul oral odor (मुखदौर्गंध्य/foul breath).
Unsalted cold sour gruel water is supreme in curing mouth dryness (मुखशोष/dry mouth).
Alkali water gargle (क्षारांबु गंडूष/alkali water gargle) instantly breaks up accumulated Kapha phlegm (श्लेष्म चय/kapha buildup).
Sitting comfortably in a windless sunny place with focused mind, after gently sweating and massaging the neck, cheeks, and forehead, one should hold the liquid or paste (sized like a jujube seed) with head slightly tilted upward without swallowing, filling 1/2, 1/3, or 1/4 of the mouth capacity.
In mouth rinse therapy (कवल/mouth rinse), the held liquid is actively moved around between the cheeks and throat.
This active movement constitutes the specific difference between Kavala (कवल/mouth rinse) and Gandusha (गंडूष/gargle retention).
When the liquid quantity permits active movement inside the mouth, it is defined as Kavala (कवल/mouth rinse).
When the mouth is filled to capacity preventing any movement, it is defined as Gandusha (गंडूष/holding gargle).
After spitting out the liquid, gentle sweating and massaging of the face and neck should be repeated.
Thus excited Kapha phlegm moves toward the mouth for complete discharge.
The liquid should be retained until cheeks fill with phlegm, nose and eyes begin watering, or the medicine becomes diluted by Kapha saliva.
One should repeat 3, 5, or 7 gargles until proper clinical signs appear.
Or hold until signs similar to proper medicated smoking appear.
Health recovery indicates proper application; heaviness, loss of taste, salivation, and oral coating indicate under-gargling; mouth dryness, inflammation, exhaustion, taste loss, cardiac fluttering, voice fatigue, and tinnitus indicate over-gargling.
Any resulting complications should be managed with appropriate counteractive therapies.
Rubbing and coating therapy (प्रतिसारण/oral dusting and rubbing) is threefold: herbal extract jelly (रसक्रिया/concentrated extract), paste (कल्क/herbal paste), and fine powder (चूर्ण/herbal powder) made from gargling herbs.
It should be intelligently applied in eye disorders, palatal inflammation, and tonsillitis or uvulitis (गलगुंडिका/uvulitis/tonsillitis).
Excessive oral rubbing produces severe palate dryness, burning, excessive moisture, and swelling.
Facial paste application (मुखालेप/facial paste) is threefold: humor-pacifying (दोषघ्न/humor pacifying), poison-neutralizing (विषघ्न/poison antidote), and complexion-enhancing (वर्ण्य/complexion booster).
Its thickness is threefold: 1/4 finger (thin), 1/3 finger (medium), and 1/2 finger (thick).
While the face is coated with paste, one must avoid excessive speaking, laughing, anger, grief, crying, sweating, sleeping in daytime, or sun exposure.
To prevent itching, skin wrinkles, rhinitis, and impairment of eyesight (दृष्ट्युपघात/vision damage).
The paste should never be allowed to dry completely on the skin.
Dry paste damages skin complexion (छवि/complexion); hence it should be moistened with water before removing.
After removing the paste, the face should be gently anointed with oil or ghee.
Facial paste is contraindicated for those awake at night, suffering from indigestion, after nasal drops, or with lockjaw and acute rhinitis.
Properly applied, it cures premature wrinkles (वली/wrinkles), gray hair (पलित/graying), cataracts (तिमिर/vision blur), dark spots (व्यंग/hyperpigmentation), and freckles (तिलक/freckles).
In healthy individuals, it strengthens eyesight (दृष्टिबल/visual power) and imparts lotus-like luster to the face (पुंडरीक कांत वक्र/lotus-like complexion).
Head oil application (मूर्द्धतैल/head oiling) is divided into four distinct methods:
Anointing massage (अभ्यंग/head oil massage), pouring shower (परिषेक/head oil pour), oil pouch (पिचु/oil cloth pad), and head oil retention cap (शिरोबस्ति/head oil cap).
Their therapeutic potency increases sequentially from massage to head oil cap.
Anointing massage (अभ्यंग/head oil massage) is used for scalp dryness (रौक्ष्य/dry scalp), itching (कंडू/itching), and scalp dirt or dandruff.
Pouring oil shower (परिषेक/head oil pour) is used for scalp eruptions (अरूंषिका/scalp pustules), severe head throbbing (शिरस्तोद/head throbbing), burning, suppuration, and scalp wounds.
Oil cloth pad (पिचु/oil cloth pad) is used for hair fall (केशशात/hair loss), hair splitting (केशस्फुटन/split ends), scalp burning, and eyelid stiffness (नेत्रस्तंभ/eyelid stiffness).
Head oil retention cap (शिरोबस्ति/head oil cap) is used for scalp numbness, facial paralysis (अर्धित/facial palsy), insomnia, severe nasal and mouth dryness, cataracts, speech loss, and severe painful head disorders.
Among these, head massage, pour, and cloth pads follow standard methods.
For head oil cap therapy (शिरोबस्ति विधि/head oil cap rules), a double-open leather cap matching head circumference, 12 fingers high above ears, is prepared.
After body purification, evening massage and mild sweating, the seated patient gets a 3-finger smooth cloth band smeared with blackgram paste bound around the hairline above ears.
The leather cap is fixed over the band, tied firmly with a cord, sealed with blackgram paste to prevent leaks, and filled with warm medicated oil to 1 finger above the hair roots.
The oil is retained until secretions flow from ears, mouth, and nose, or until head pain subsides completely.
Retention time count: 10,000 counts in Vata disorders, 8,000 counts in Pitta and blood disorders, 6,000 counts in Kapha disorders, and 1,000 counts in healthy individuals.
After draining the oil and removing the cap, shoulders, neck, back, and forehead should be gently massaged.
The patient then bathes in warm water (उष्णांबु/warm water) and eats a wholesome meal.
Standard oleation post-treatment guidelines should be followed carefully.
Head oil cap therapy should be practiced for 3, 5, or 7 days continuously.
Here are summary verses (भवति चात्र/summary shlokas):
Head oil massage (अभ्यंग/oil massage) should be applied in scalp dryness, itching, and dandruff.
Oil shower (परिषेक/oil pour) in scalp pustules, head throbbing, burning, suppuration, and ulcers.
Oil pad (पिचु/oil pad) in hair loss, split ends, scalp burning, and eyelid stiffness.
Head oil cap (शिरोबस्ति/head oil cap) in scalp numbness, facial paralysis, insomnia,
Nasal and mouth dryness, cataracts, and severe intractable head diseases.
Head oiling cures hair fall, premature graying, yellowish discoloration, hair splitting, scalp cracking, and Vata head disorders.
It enhances sensory clarity (इन्द्रिय प्रसाद/sensory clarity), voice power (स्वर बल/voice strength), jaw strength (हनु बल/jaw strength), and head vigor (मूर्द्ध बल/head vigor).
Ear filling (कर्णपूरण/ear drops) should be held while massaging the root of the ear.
Retained until pain subsides, or for 100 counts in painless ear conditions.
One count is defined as the time taken to circle the right hand around the knee joint.
Equivalent to one normal eye blink duration (निमेष उन्मेष/blinking duration).
Thus ends the thirty-first chapter (एकत्रिंशः अध्यायः/thirty-first chapter) named 'Gargle, Mouth Rinse, and Facial Application Rules' (गंडूषादिविधिः/gargle paste rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on eye drops and collyrium rules (आश्च्योतनाञ्जन विधि/eye drops and collyrium) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Eye drops (आश्च्योतन/eye drops) are the primary foremost procedure in all ocular diseases.
When formulated with various medicinal substances, eye drops cure redness (राग/redness), watering (अश्रु/tearing), grittiness (घर्ष/friction), pain (रुज्/pain), burning (दाह/burning), pricking (तोद/pricking), severe piercing pain (भेद/splitting pain), suppuration (पाक/suppuration), swelling (शोफ/swelling), and itching (कण्डू/itching).
In unmanifest or early eye disorders, applying paste over the eyelids without touching eyelashes acts with similar efficacy, and this procedure is named Bidalaka (बिडालक/eyelid paste application).
For Bidalaka and pastes, night is the ideal time of application.
For eye drops, daytime or the immediate onset of disease is the appropriate time.
The patient should lie down in a windless room on a soft bed; after massaging around the outer eye corner (अपाङ्ग/outer corner), the practitioner gently opens the eye with the left hand and drops 12, 10, or 8 drops from a shell or cotton wick into the inner corner (कनीनक/inner eye corner) from 2 fingers height using the right hand.
Dropping from too close or dropping too forcefully causes eye trauma and aggravates pain and redness.
After instilling eye drops, the eye region should be gently wiped with a soft cloth.
In Vata and Kapha eye disorders, the eyes should be gently warm-fomented with a cloth dipped in warm water.
Eye drop liquids should be mildly warm in Vata and Kapha disorders.
In Pitta and blood disorders, eye drop liquids should be very cool.
Eye drops should never be excessively sharp, hot, cold, abundant, scanty, or un-filtered.
Excessively sharp or hot drops produce burning, redness, suppuration, and weakness of eyesight (दृष्टिदौर्बल्य/vision weakness).
Excessively cold drops cause eye stiffness, excessive tearing, grittiness, and pricking pain.
Excessive volume causes astringent roughness in eyelids, eyelid twitching, and intolerance to light and wind.
Deficient volume fails to relieve the ocular disease.
Un-filtered drops cause excessive tearing, grittiness, and pain.
When properly applied, medicine enters the conjunctival sacs, vascular networks, and nasal/oral channels, successfully eliminating elevated morbid humors.
When eye drops reduce discharge, stickiness, heaviness, itching, swelling, and redness in Pitta, Kapha, and blood eye diseases, indicating maturity (पक्व लक्षण/mature stage), collyrium (अञ्जन/collyrium) should then be applied directly to the eye.
Collyrium must never be applied during acute un-matured humoral surge.
Applying collyrium during acute un-matured surge causes severe aggravation of redness, corneal ulceration (शुक्र/corneal ulcer), and cataracts (तिमिर/cataract).
Collyrium is fourfold: scraping (लेखन/scraping), healing (रोपण/healing), unctuous (स्नेहन/unctuous), and soothing (प्रसादन/soothing).
Scraping collyrium formulated with 5 tastes (sour, salty, etc.) treats corneal opacities and pterygium.
Healing collyrium formulated with bitter, astringent, and unctuous herbs cures conjunctivitis (अभिष्यन्द/conjunctivitis) and corneal ulcers.
Unctuous collyrium prepared with ghee, muscle fat, and marrow treats Vata cataracts and optic nerve disorders.
Soothing collyrium prepared with sweet cooling unctuous herbs heals eyes afflicted by solar glare, lightning flash, trauma, or glare, and preserves healthy vision.
Fine soothing collyrium powder applied to eyes irritated by sharp collyrium is termed Pratyanjana (प्रत्यञ्जन/counter-collyrium).
Collyrium is also classified into 6 types based on six tastes.
Or broadly into 2 types: sharp (तीक्ष्ण/sharp) and mild (मृदु/mild).
Collyrium formulations are threefold in physical form:
Pill/stick (पिण्ड/collyrium stick), boiled extract paste (रसक्रिया/extract paste), and fine powder (चूर्ण/fine powder).
Pill sticks are strongest, extract paste is medium, and fine powder is mildest.
They are prescribed respectively in severe, moderate, and mild eye diseases.
Dose of sharp pill stick is equal to 1 pea grain (हरेणु/pea grain).
Dose of extract paste is equal to 1 Vidanga seed.
Double the dose is used for mild formulations.
Dose of sharp powder is 2 applicator dips.
Dose of mild powder is 3 applicator dips.
Sweet collyrium should be prepared or stored in gold vessels (सौवर्ण/gold vessel).
Sour collyrium in silver vessels (राजन/silver vessel).
Salty collyrium in ram horn vessels (मेषशृङ्ग/ram horn vessel).
Bitter collyrium in bronze vessels (कांस्य/bronze vessel).
Pungent collyrium in cat's eye gemstone or stone vessels.
Astringent collyrium in copper or iron vessels (ताम्र आयस/copper iron vessel).
Cooling collyrium in reed, lotus stem, quartz crystal, or conch shell vessels.
Proper storage vessels preserve the full potency and quality of collyrium.
The grinding stone for collyrium sticks should be sharp, smooth, 5 fingers long, and 3 fingers wide.
Collyrium applicator rods (शलाका/applicator rod) are made of gold, silver, copper, iron, or wood, or applied with a finger.
Gold and silver rods are used for soothing and unctuous collyrium.
Copper rods are used for scraping collyrium.
Iron and wood rods are used for healing collyrium.
Due to its soft texture, the index finger (अङ्गुलि/finger) is supreme for applying collyrium.
For painful or delicate inner eye conditions, finger application is safest.
Other metallic applicator rods should be 10 fingers long, thick as a pea, smooth, narrow in middle, with bud-shaped ends.
Collyrium should be applied at proper times when weather is neither too cold nor too hot.
It should never be applied continuously without pause.
In painful eye conditions, apply collyrium to the un-afflicted eye first, then to the painful eye.
Otherwise, eye irritation and lid contraction prevent the medicine from penetrating.
Applying collyrium during extreme cold or heat causes severe humoral surge and disease aggravation.
Collyrium must never be applied to persons who are angry, frightened, exhausted, intoxicated, immediately after purgation, smoking, wine intake, nasal drops, night vigil, suppression of urges, crying, or during acute fever.
In these forbidden states, collyrium causes severe tearing, pain, cloudiness, burning, swelling, corneal opacities, and blindness.
The patient sits comfortably; the practitioner gently lifts the upper eyelid with left thumb and applies collyrium from inner corner (कनीनक/inner corner) to outer corner (अपाङ्ग/outer corner) smoothly without scratching or squeezing.
For powder collyrium, move the applicator rod back and forth twice.
Improper application leads to redness, discharge, and visual impairment.
When properly applied, collyrium leaves eyesight clear, spotless, and radiant like the moon in a clear night sky.
It clears all impurities from the eye channels.
Therefore, wholesome soothing collyrium should be used regularly to preserve and enhance visual acuity.
Daily soothing collyrium continuously strengthens eyesight and protects against ocular diseases.
Thus ends the rules of eye drops and collyrium (इति अञ्जनविध्यादिः/rules of eye drops and collyrium).
Now, therefore, we shall explain the rules of tooth cleansing and daily hygiene.
Rising early in the morning during Brahma Muhurta (ब्राह्ममुहूर्त/pre-dawn hours, 45 mins before sunrise),
After attending to natural evacuations and personal cleanliness,
One should perform tooth cleansing (दन्तधावन/tooth brushing).
Using fresh twigs of Arka, Banyan, Khadira, Karanja, or Arjuna trees possessing astringent, bitter, or pungent tastes.
Astringent, bitter, and pungent twigs are recommended for tooth brushing.
The twig should be thick as the tip of little finger, crushed smooth at top, and 12 fingers long.
This constitutes the ideal herbal toothbrush (दन्तधावन/herbal toothbrush).
Clean the teeth carefully without injuring the gums (दन्तवेष्ट/gums).
Do not press hard or bleed the gums.
A tongue scraper (जिह्वा निर्लेखन/tongue scraper) made of gold, silver, copper, or smooth wood, 10 fingers long, should be used to scrape the tongue.
Clean the tongue thoroughly to remove coating and bad breath.
Then rinse the mouth with clean water and hold oil in mouth.
Holding oil in mouth renders teeth firm, strong, and resistant to decay.
Thus ends the thirty-second chapter (द्वात्रिंशः अध्यायः/thirty-second chapter) named 'Eye Drops, Collyrium, and Hygiene Rules' (आश्च्योतनाञ्जनविधिः/eye drops and collyrium rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on ocular pooling and cooked extract therapies (तर्पण पुटपाक विधि/ocular pooling and cooked extract rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Indications for unctuous ocular pooling (तर्पण/eye pooling): In eyes that are exhausted, dry (परिशुष्क/dry), rough, stiff, distorted, sunken, turbid, heavy-lidded, with falling eyelashes, difficulty in opening, red vessels, spots, cataracts (तिमिर/cataract), conjunctivitis (अभिष्यन्द/conjunctivitis), glaucoma (अधिमन्थ/glaucoma), and dry eye syndrome (शुष्काक्षिपाक/dry eye), when acute pain and discharge have subsided.
It should not be applied when acute complications persist, nor in extreme cold, heat, rain, cloudy days, or in patients unfit for nasal medication.
The same rules and contraindications apply to cooked herbal juice pooling (पुटपाक/cooked juice pooling).
During the last or first eighth part of the day, in a room free from wind, sun exposure, dust, and smoke, after previous meal digests, the supine patient lies comfortably on a bed; a 2-finger high leak-proof circular wall of kneaded blackgram flour paste (माष पिष्ट/blackgram flour paste) is constructed around eye sockets, and warm medicated ghee, ghee scum, or milk processed with herbs is poured over closed eyes until eyelashes and eyebrow hair are submerged.
Then slowly opening and blinking eyes, duration is counted in time counts (मात्रा/time count):
100 counts for eyelid disorders, 300 for joint/canthus disorders, 500 for corneal opacities (शुक्र/corneal opacity), 700 for iris disorders, 800 for pupil/vision disorders, and 1000 counts for glaucoma (अधिमन्थ/glaucoma).
According to bodily humors: 1000 counts for Vata, 600 for Pitta, 500 for Kapha, and 500 counts for healthy preventive care.
Then a small drain hole is punctured at the outer eye corner (अपाङ्ग/outer corner) using a probe, and the unctuous liquid is drained into a vessel.
The dough ring is removed, eye sockets wiped with herbal paste, and appropriate purificatory smoke (धूम/herbal smoke) inhaled to eliminate Kapha provoked by oleation.
The face is washed with warm water, and wholesome meals appropriate to the disease are served.
Direct exposure to bright sunlight, open sky, and luminous objects must be strictly avoided.
This procedure is performed daily for Vata, every 2nd day for Pitta and blood disorders, every 3rd day for Kapha and in healthy persons,
According to the severity of single or combined humor aggravation.
It should be continued for 1 day, 3 days, or 5 days according to patient need.
Insufficient, ideal, and excessive pooling signs correspond to Vata, Pitta, and Kapha symptoms respectively.
When ocular pooling (तर्पण/eye pooling) is properly completed, cooked herbal juice pooling (पुटपाक/cooked juice pooling) should be administered for the same eye diseases.
Cooked extract pooling is of three types:
Unctuous nourishing (स्नेहन/unctuous pooling), scraping evacuant (लेखन/scraping pooling), and soothing palliative (प्रसादन/soothing pooling).
Unctuous pooling (स्नेहन पुटपाक/unctuous cooked juice): Uses meat, fat, marrow, and lard of marshy animals processed with sweet herbs and milk, indicated for dry rough eyes.
Scraping pooling (लेखन पुटपाक/scraping cooked juice): Uses wild animal liver, pearls, coral, conch, copper, iron, cuttlefish bone, and calx processed with curd whey for over-unctuous eyes.
Soothing pooling (प्रसादन पुटपाक/soothing cooked juice): Uses wild meat, liver, heart, marrow, fat, sweet herbs, breastmilk, milk, and ghee.
It destroys Vata, Pitta, blood disorders, visual weakness, and corneal ulcers, but is contraindicated in Kapha excess.
Method: A meat and herb ball equal to Bael fruit size is wrapped in castor and Patola leaves, bound with grass thread,
Coated with 2-finger thick clay, and roasted in smokeless wood or buffalo dung coals until red-hot.
Upon cooling, clay and leaves are removed, and warm juice squeezed through a clean cloth.
The warm juice is poured over eyes in the evening following the same procedure as ocular pooling.
Retention count: 200 counts for unctuous, 100 counts for scraping, and 300 counts for soothing cooked juice pooling.
Followed by medicated smoke inhalation (except for soothing pooling).
Unctuous and scraping juices are applied warm;
Soothing juice is applied cold; administered for 1, 2, or 3 days.
Post-treatment care rules are twice as long as the treatment period.
At night, eyes are lightly bandaged with jasmine flowers.
This cooked juice extraction process (पुटपाक/cooked juice) is also utilized for treating mature chronic diarrhea (पक्कातिसार/mature diarrhea).
Explanatory verse follows:
Any complications arising from improper execution of eye showers, collyrium, pooling, or cooked juice should be treated according to their specific humor signs.
